<a id="cambodia-migration-guide"></a>

# Cambodia: migration guide

Sources checked up to 11 Nov 2026.

How to go to Cambodia on a Bangladeshi passport, visa by visa, step by step. A raised number such as 3 is an official source; N3 is a news, NGO, research or guide source, listed under "Needs an official source"; a year, like N3 (2022), marks a source older than 2025. "Need to know" marks a point no source has yet confirmed. "You'll need:" lists what to have before starting a procedure.

Tk is the Bangladeshi taka (1 lakh is Tk 100,000; 1 crore is 100 lakh). Wages and prices in Cambodia are given in US dollars (USD), as the sources give them, converted at Bangladesh Bank's rate of USD 1 = about Tk 124.65 on 11 November 2026 <sup>[1](#o1), [N1](#n1), [2](#o2)</sup>. The riel is Cambodia's own currency; 1,000 riel = about Tk 30.5, from the National Bank of Cambodia's rate of USD 1 = 4,084 riel on the same day <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>. BMET (Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training) is the Bangladesh government agency that registers jobseekers on its OEP (Overseas Employment Platform) website and gives each worker emigration clearance, a smart card, before departure; the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare tells workers never to go without that clearance <sup>[4](#o4), [5](#o5), [6](#o6)</sup>. In Cambodia no foreigner may work without two papers from MLVT (the Ministry of Labour and Vocational Training): a work permit and an employment card, applied for together by the employer; the work permit is valid for one year <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7), [N2 (2024)](#n2), [N3 (2024)](#n3), [N4](#n4)</sup>. The employer applies on FWCMS (Foreign Workers Centralized Management System), MLVT's online system where companies register, ask for a quota of foreign workers and get permits <sup>[8](#o8), [N5](#n5)</sup>. A worker enters on the E-class visa (the ordinary or "business" visa, for people with a job offer from a registered Cambodian company), which costs USD 35 (about Tk 4,360) online and gives a 30-day stay <sup>[9](#o9), [N6](#n6), [10](#o10), [N2 (2024)](#n2), [2](#o2)</sup>. Inside Cambodia the E-class visa is extended as an EB extension (for work, alongside a work permit) or an EG extension (for job-seekers), among others; an EB extension alone gives no right to work <sup>[N6](#n6), [11](#o11)</sup>. Extensions are granted by the GDI (General Department of Immigration) of the Ministry of Interior, which also runs FPCS (Foreigners Presence in Cambodia System), the online register of where foreigners live <sup>[N2 (2024)](#n2), [N7](#n7), [N8](#n8)</sup>. Bangladesh has no embassy in Cambodia: the Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok, Thailand, covers it, and Cambodia's embassy for Bangladesh is in New Delhi <sup>[N9](#n9), [12](#o12)</sup>. Every land crossing between Cambodia and Thailand is closed until further notice <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>.

<a id="background"></a>

## 1 Background

<a id="in-one-minute"></a>

### 1.1 In one minute

Cambodia took few Bangladeshi workers until 2025 (929 in 2024, its 23rd destination); in 2025 BMET cleared 12,263 for it, almost all men and mostly recorded as construction workers, on permission letters for Phnom Penh building firms at USD 420 (about Tk 52,400) a month <sup>[14](#o14), [15](#o15), [16](#o16), [17](#o17), [18](#o18), [19](#o19), [1](#o1), [2](#o2)</sup>. Most went in small batches of up to five workers whose job papers needed no check by the Bangladesh embassy; BMET's permission letters capped what an agency could take at Tk 80,520 to Tk 1,55,450, but people paid Tk 2 to 7 lakh, often through a middleman who promised a computer, data-entry or customer-service job <sup>[N10](#n10), [20](#o20), [21](#o21), [N11](#n11), [N12](#n12)</sup>. On arrival many were met by Bangladeshi brokers, kept in a hotel and sold for USD 1,500 to 5,000 (about Tk 1.9 to 6.2 lakh) to Chinese-run scam compounds, guarded sites where they were forced to cheat people online and beaten or given electric shocks if they refused <sup>[N13](#n13), [N14](#n14), [N15](#n15), [2](#o2)</sup>. BRAC counted more than 1,000 BMET-cleared Bangladeshis still trapped in compounds in July 2026, and a BRAC study found 98% of returnees from Cambodia had not got the promised job <sup>[N10](#n10), [N16](#n16)</sup>. Even a real job ties a worker to one employer's work permit, renewed every year, and an expired visa costs USD 10 (about Tk 1,250) a day and can end in detention and deportation: Cambodia deported more than 3,000 Bangladeshis in January to September 2026 <sup>[N2 (2024)](#n2), [7 (1997)](#o7), [N4](#n4), [N17](#n17), [N7](#n7), [N18](#n18), [2](#o2)</sup>. Bangladesh has no embassy in Cambodia, and the land border with Thailand, where its embassy is, is closed <sup>[N9](#n9), [13](#o13)</sup>. BMET clearances fell from 3,235 in October 2025 to none in October 2026, and on 4 November 2026 the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare told BMET to clear no one for a list of 12 countries, Cambodia among them, without its approval <sup>[22](#o22), [23](#o23), [N12](#n12), [N19](#n19)</sup>.

<a id="is-it-open-now-and-who-can-go"></a>

### 1.2 Is it open now, and who can go?

Closed in practice for workers going through BMET, as of 10 November 2026: BMET cleared 7 workers for Cambodia in September 2026 and none in October or from 1 to 10 November <sup>[23](#o23)</sup>. On 4 November 2026 the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare told BMET not to clear workers for 12 countries, Cambodia among them, without the Ministry's own recruitment permission or approval, giving suspected trafficking, deception and workers ending up in detention as its reasons <sup>[N12](#n12), [N19](#n19), [N20](#n20)</sup>. Every demand letter (the employer's written request for workers) for these countries must now be approved by the Ministry first, and BAIRA (the Bangladesh Association of International Recruiting Agencies) has passed the order to its member agencies by circular <sup>[N21](#n21), [N22](#n22), [N12](#n12)</sup>. In July 2026 the State Minister for Expatriates' Welfare, Nurul Hoque, said there would be no blanket ban on Cambodia, but that workers for Cambodia would need proper work visas and full checks before clearance <sup>[N10](#n10)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is Cambodia on the Ministry's 4 November 2026 list of 12 countries (one Bangla daily lists Croatia in its place), and what does an agency now submit to the Ministry to get a Cambodia demand letter approved?
> - Has the Ministry approved any Cambodia demand letter since 4 November 2026?

<a id="status"></a>

#### 1.2.1 Status

- **September to December 2025:** BMET cleared 1,469 workers for Cambodia in September, 3,235 in October (the highest month on record), 2,469 in November and 2,550 in December <sup>[22](#o22)</sup>.
- **January to June 2026:** clearances fell from 2,223 in January to 674 in February, 607 in March, 102 in April, 52 in May and 2 in June <sup>[23](#o23)</sup>. In June 2026, 583 Bangladeshis came home from Cambodia, 221 of them rescued from scam compounds and flown home in four days <sup>[N23](#n23), [N24](#n24)</sup>.
- **July 2026:** the State Minister said Cambodia, Laos and Brazil lose the exemption that let small batches of workers go without the Bangladesh embassy attesting the job papers (see [The demand letter and the small-batch route](#the-demand-letter-and-the-small-batch-route)) <sup>[N10](#n10)</sup>.
- **July to September 2026:** BMET cleared 17 workers in July, 16 in August and 7 in September <sup>[23](#o23)</sup>.
- **1 September to 31 October 2026:** Cambodia let foreigners who had overstayed leave without paying fines, a waiver due to end on 31 October 2026 (see [Going home without valid papers](#going-home-without-valid-papers)) <sup>[N25](#n25), [N26](#n26)</sup>.
- **October and 1 to 10 November 2026:** no clearances <sup>[23](#o23)</sup>.
- **4 November 2026:** the Ministry's approval order, from its Employment-3 branch, signed by Deputy Secretary Gazi Md. Shahed Anwar <sup>[N12](#n12), [N27](#n27)</sup>.

<a id="the-channels"></a>

#### 1.2.2 The channels

- **A licensed Bangladeshi agency with a BMET permission letter:** the official route. BMET's permission letter names the agency, the Cambodian employer, the job, the number of workers, the pay and the most the agency may take, and the agency must choose the workers from BMET's database of registered jobseekers (see [Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker)) <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.
- **A small batch without embassy attestation:** a demand letter for up to five workers could go to BMET without the embassy's check; syndicates split large orders to use it, and 70-75% of workers were going this way when it was ended in July 2026 <sup>[N10](#n10)</sup>.
- **A tourist or visit visa on a broker's job promise:** the Ministry warns that brokers send people to Cambodia on tourist and visit visas and tells people never to go for work this way <sup>[6](#o6)</sup>. Working on a tourist visa is prohibited (see [Visas not open to Bangladeshis](#visas-not-open-to-bangladeshis)) <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>.
- **Government to government:** Bangladesh and Cambodia signed a memorandum of understanding (MoU) on labour and vocational training on 4 December 2017 for five years, and renewed it in May 2022 <sup>[N28 (2017)](#n28), [N29 (2022)](#n29)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Has BOESL (Bangladesh Overseas Employment and Services Limited, the government's own recruiting company) ever sent workers to Cambodia, and is any recruitment planned under the renewed MoU?

<a id="who-qualifies"></a>

#### 1.2.3 Who qualifies

Men aged 20 to 45, on 2-year contracts, in BMET's permission letters for Cambodia <sup>[1](#o1), [19](#o19), [24](#o24), [25](#o25), [26](#o26), [27](#o27)</sup>. BMET cleared no women for Cambodia in 2022, one each in 2023, 2024 and 2025, and two in 2026 to 10 November <sup>[28](#o28), [29](#o29), [30](#o30), [17](#o17), [31](#o31)</sup>. Other women have gone too: one went in August 2025 through a travel agency for computer work and was held in a scam centre, and a woman returnee says many Bangladeshi women who went for jobs are stuck in Cambodia <sup>[N30](#n30), [N13](#n13)</sup>. The letters are for construction workers <sup>[1](#o1), [19](#o19), [32](#o32), [21](#o21), [33](#o33), [34 (2023)](#o34), [26](#o26), [35](#o35), [27](#o27)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does BMET ask for a construction skill certificate from a TTC (Technical Training Centre, BMET's training institute) before clearing a worker for Cambodia, and does any Cambodian employer test workers before hiring?

<a id="size"></a>

#### 1.2.4 Size

- **Workers cleared by BMET:** 368 in 2022, 1,378 in 2023 (18th destination), 929 in 2024 (23rd), 12,263 in 2025 (8th) and 3,700 from 1 January to 10 November 2026 <sup>[36](#o36), [37](#o37), [38](#o38), [14](#o14), [15](#o15), [16](#o16), [N31](#n31), [39](#o39)</sup>.
- **Job titles on the clearances:** in 2025, out of 35 titles, the largest were Construction Worker (3,428), Construction Labour (2,849), A Builder (1,456), Building / Construction Helper (1,081), Cleaner (678), A Casual Worker (429), Production Manager (388) and Quality Controller (300) <sup>[18](#o18)</sup>. In 2026 to 10 November the largest were A Casual Worker (736), Farm Worker (580), A Builder (390), Building / Construction Helper (349) and Pipe Fitting Labour (335), and 25 were recorded as "Agent" <sup>[40](#o40), [41](#o41)</sup>.
- **Against other high-risk countries:** from January 2025 to September 2026, 15,963 Bangladeshi workers went to Cambodia, more than to any other country on the US State Department's Tier 3 list (its worst rating for fighting human trafficking), against 7,752 to Russia and 7,532 to Laos <sup>[N32](#n32), [N33](#n33)</sup>. The US kept Cambodia on Tier 3 in its 2026 report <sup>[N33](#n33)</sup>. Cambodia, Russia, Laos and Belarus, all on Tier 3, took 27,647 of the 44,122 workers BMET cleared from January 2025 to 30 October 2026 for the 12 countries on the Ministry's 4 November list <sup>[N34](#n34), [N12](#n12)</sup>. Laos suspended recruitment, work permits and entry visas for Bangladeshi workers from 1 September to 31 December 2026 <sup>[N35](#n35)</sup>.
- **Where they are now:** BRAC says there is no way to know where the roughly 16,000 who went with BMET clearance are or what they are doing <sup>[N36](#n36)</sup>. Cambodia's labour minister said about 400 Bangladeshis held Cambodian work permits in 2022 <sup>[N29 (2022)](#n29)</sup>. That year the Bangladeshi ambassador met Bangladeshis working in Cambodia in service, construction, hospitality and banking <sup>[N29 (2022)](#n29)</sup>. The Bangladesh embassy estimated 4,000-4,500 Bangladeshis were overstaying their visas in Cambodia in July 2026 <sup>[N37](#n37)</sup>.
- **Deported, against other nationalities:** of 52,039 foreigners from 87 countries Cambodia deported in January to September 2026, about 23,000 were Chinese, over 8,000 Indonesian, over 4,000 Vietnamese, over 3,000 Bangladeshi and over 2,500 Pakistani <sup>[N38](#n38)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How many of the 12,263 workers cleared for Cambodia in 2025 received an MLVT work permit, and how many Bangladeshis hold one in 2026, and in which jobs? Is any Cambodian employer outside construction (garment factories, hotels, banks) asking for Bangladeshi workers through BMET in 2026?

<a id="what-closed-it-before"></a>

#### 1.2.5 What closed it before

- **2022-2023:** in 2022 the embassy attested requisitions for 210 workers for Cambodia while BMET registered 3,127; BMET's then Director General said stopping registration for lack of checks would hurt remittances <sup>[N39 (2023)](#n39)</sup>. In September 2022 RAB (the Rapid Action Battalion, a Bangladesh police unit) arrested a Cambodia-based ring leader, Nazmul Islam, whose gang it said had trafficked more than 500 Bangladeshis into Cambodian cybercrime <sup>[N40 (2022)](#n40)</sup>.
- **July and December 2025:** fighting on the Thai-Cambodian border displaced Bangladeshi workers; in July the Embassy's labour counsellor went to Phnom Penh to arrange food, shelter, transport and medical care, and in December over 150 construction workers who fled a site near the border after bombing were detained by immigration police and asked for USD 800 (about Tk 1 lakh) each to be sent home <sup>[42](#o42), [N41](#n41), [2](#o2)</sup>. The GDI closed the Doung international checkpoint from 13 June 2025, by 17 September 2025 none of the checkpoints on the Thai border had reopened, and on 12 December 2025 it asked foreign embassies to tell their citizens not to travel to the border conflict areas <sup>[43](#o43), [44](#o44), [45](#o45)</sup>. All land crossings to Thailand were still closed in October 2026 <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>.
- **2026:** clearances fell from 2,223 in January to 674 in February and 2 in June, the small-batch exemption was ended in July, clearances stopped in October, and the Ministry approval order came on 4 November (see [Status](#status)) <sup>[23](#o23), [N10](#n10), [N12](#n12)</sup>.

<a id="how-a-job-reaches-you-and-who-takes-a-cut"></a>

### 1.3 How a job reaches you, and who takes a cut

A legal job starts as a Cambodian employer's demand letter, which the Bangladesh embassy is meant to attest before BMET gives one licensed agency a permission letter and then clears the workers <sup>[N10](#n10), [1](#o1), [5](#o5)</sup>. The choke point is on the Bangladesh side: the embassy's check, which small batches skipped until July 2026, and BMET clearance, which brokers use to make a fake job look real <sup>[N10](#n10), [N42](#n42)</sup>.

<a id="the-demand-letter-and-the-small-batch-route"></a>

#### 1.3.1 The demand letter and the small-batch route

The Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok is meant to verify and attest the employer's appointment or demand letter before workers go to Cambodia <sup>[N39 (2023)](#n39), [N10](#n10), [N9](#n9)</sup>. Until July 2026 a demand letter for up to five workers could go to BMET without that attestation, and syndicates split large orders into small ones to avoid the embassy's scrutiny <sup>[N10](#n10), [N43](#n43)</sup>. Separately, until 4 November 2026 demand letters for up to seven workers needed no approval from the Ministry, and this was allegedly misused to send workers on fake job offers <sup>[N19](#n19), [N22](#n22)</sup>. In a 2022 case, an agent told a worker that work visas were not available, so he would go on a business visa <sup>[N44 (2022)](#n44)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Since July 2026, does the Embassy's labour wing in Bangkok visit a Cambodian employer in Phnom Penh before attesting its demand letter, or check the papers only?

<a id="the-employers"></a>

#### 1.3.2 The employers

The permission letters name these construction firms in Cambodia: North Asian Construction Co. Ltd.; Rafa Construction Co. Ltd.; Chip Hong Insee Ltd., Sim Sia Cambodia, Sok Hang Group, Vain SR Construction and Phanimex Group; China State Construction (Cambodia) Co. Ltd. and Asbic Construction Company Ltd.; Xin Caifu (Cambodia) Co., Ltd., Maliha Jamman M.S Co., Ltd. and Golden Cresent Development Co., Ltd.; Royal Bengol Engineering and Constructors Co. Ltd., Abrar Pvt (Cambodia) Co. Ltd. and Yong Sheng Global Trading Co. Ltd.; Yukon Builders (Cambodia) Co. Ltd.; L.Y.P. Group, Soma Group and Sinohydro (Cambodia) United; and Koh Kong Construction Co. Ltd. <sup>[1](#o1), [34 (2023)](#o34), [19](#o19), [32](#o32), [21](#o21), [24](#o24), [25](#o25), [35](#o35), [26](#o26), [27](#o27)</sup>. A Bangladesh government delegation went to Cambodia in October 2019 to seek jobs for construction workers with Chinese-financed construction firms <sup>[N45 (2019)](#n45)</sup>. BMET's letters give employers' addresses, for example Chip Hong Insee Ltd. at 137 Mao Tse Toung Blvd, Royal Bengol Engineering and Constructors Co. Ltd. in Trapeang Krasang, Posen Chey, and Xin Caifu (Cambodia) Co., Ltd. in Sangkat Chom Chao II, Khan Por Sen Chey <sup>[19](#o19), [33](#o33), [21](#o21)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which of these employers really took the workers named in the permission letters, and are any still hiring Bangladeshis?

<a id="the-bangladeshi-agencies"></a>

#### 1.3.3 The Bangladeshi agencies

BMET's permission letters for Cambodia are all for construction workers, and those that state the pay set USD 420 a month, except one at USD 450 <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34), [1](#o1), [19](#o19), [32](#o32), [21](#o21), [33](#o33), [26](#o26), [35](#o35), [27](#o27)</sup>. The letters (agency, date, workers, employer, cap on what the agency may take), with the licence status on OEP's agency list where it is recorded:

- **T.K. Enterprise (RL-1527):** 20 November 2023, 33 workers for Rafa Construction Co. Ltd. <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>.
- **Sams Company (RL-0696):** January 2025, 18 workers for North Asian Construction Co. Ltd.; cap Tk 80,770; Suspended <sup>[1](#o1), [46](#o46)</sup>. A second Sams letter, 6 February 2025, is for 24 workers for Koh Kong Construction Co. Ltd.; cap Tk 80,770 <sup>[27](#o27)</sup>. Mondol Trade International (RL-2626) got a letter on 26 January 2025 for 27 workers for Royal Bengol Engineering and Constructors Co. Ltd. (9) and Yukon Builders (Cambodia) Co. Ltd. (18); cap Tk 80,770 <sup>[35](#o35)</sup>.
- **Puspita Overseas (RL-2648), Fakirapool, Dhaka:** 24 March 2025, 144 workers for employers including Chip Hong Insee, Sim Sia Cambodia, Sok Hang Group, Vain SR Construction and Phanimex Group; cap Tk 80,770; Active <sup>[19](#o19), [47](#o47), [46](#o46)</sup>. An earlier Puspita letter, 13 March 2025, is for 68 workers for L.Y.P. Group, Soma Group and Sinohydro (Cambodia) United; cap Tk 80,770 <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>. A Sirajganj man told the Daily Star (July 2026) that a broker asked him Tk 7 lakh for a computer-operator job paying Tk 80,000 a month, and that he paid Tk 1 lakh to the broker and the rest to "Pushpita Overseas in Fakirapool"; his visa named him a construction worker with North Asian Construction Company, and in Cambodia he was told he had been bought for USD 2,000 (about Tk 2.5 lakh) <sup>[N37](#n37), [N13](#n13), [2](#o2)</sup>.
- **Al-Grand Sultan Overseas (RL-1582), Savar:** 20 April 2025, 96 workers for Vain SR, Sok Hang, Sim Sia and Phanimex; cap Tk 80,520; Active <sup>[20](#o20), [46](#o46)</sup>.
- **Fahim Recruiting Agency (RL-2594), Naya Paltan:** 25 June 2025, 96 workers for China State Construction (Cambodia) and Asbic Construction; cap Tk 1,05,000; Suspended <sup>[32](#o32), [48](#o48), [46](#o46)</sup>. Its letter says the other terms of employment follow Malaysia's labour law <sup>[48](#o48)</sup>. A Chapainawabganj worker told TBS (July 2026) that he paid Tk 4.8 lakh through a local middleman, went through Fahim with a BMET clearance card and a valid work visa, and endured 10 months of hardship in Cambodia; the agency's owner said only three workers had complained against it at BMET <sup>[N10](#n10)</sup>.
- **Mongol Global Services (RL-2527):** 31 August 2025, 144 workers for three firms including Xin Caifu (Cambodia); cap Tk 1,55,450; Suspended <sup>[21](#o21), [46](#o46)</sup>.
- **SAM International (RL-1203):** 10 September 2025, 100 workers, 25 each for Royal Bengol Engineering and Constructors, Xin Cai Fu (Cambodia), Abrar Pvt (Cambodia) and Yong Sheng Global Trading; cap Tk 80,770 <sup>[33](#o33), [25](#o25)</sup>.

How to find and check these letters: Work visa: company worker, [Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job) <sup>[49](#o49)</sup>. Other licensed agencies have been named: in June 2022 M/S Right Job, a licensed agency in Dhanmondi, advertised a Cambodia tiles-installer job on Facebook at USD 600 (about Tk 74,800) a month and agreed a Tk 3.5 lakh deal, Daily Star Bangla reported in January 2023 <sup>[N46 (2023)](#n46), [2](#o2)</sup>. In 2023 the police counter-terrorism unit said it had found criminal rings linked to about six recruiting agencies trafficking Bangladeshis to Cambodia <sup>[N46 (2023)](#n46)</sup>. Returnees have named two more agencies: two cousins who paid a relative more than Tk 10 lakh went through Mashallah Overseas in the Dainik Bangla area (TBS, 2022), and a Narsingdi man promised computer-operator work went on 30 October 2025 through Alif International Agency and found a scam centre (bdnews24, 2026) <sup>[N44 (2022)](#n44), [N47](#n47), [N48](#n48)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Has BMET issued any permission letter for Cambodia since September 2025, and are any of the 2025 letters still open for recruitment?
> - Why are Sams Company, Fahim Recruiting Agency and Mongol Global Services suspended on OEP, and was it over Cambodia? Is the Mashallah Overseas in these cases the licensed RL-1039 (Dilkusha), and is the Alif International Agency the RL-0049 whose licence BMET lists as cancelled?

<a id="the-middlemen"></a>

#### 1.3.4 The middlemen

A BRAC study found 66% of cyber-scam victims were recruited through middlemen, and the UN Human Rights Office found nearly three in four victims were recruited through relatives, friends, neighbours or community members <sup>[N12](#n12), [N49](#n49)</sup>. The pitch is an office job: computer operator, data entry, customer service or call centre, paying Tk 50,000 to over Tk 1 lakh a month; one friend told a Sirajganj man it could be arranged faster than work in Saudi Arabia <sup>[N11](#n11), [N15](#n15), [N37](#n37)</sup>. Ads run in WhatsApp, Facebook and Telegram groups, and middlemen fill Telegram groups with fake "successful migrants" who belong to the ring <sup>[N50](#n50), [N15](#n15)</sup>. In one case the first meeting with the broker was at a tea stall across the highway from Dhaka airport <sup>[N37](#n37)</sup>. A Bangladeshi businessman in Cambodia said in 2022 that workers are lured with USD 1,000 (about Tk 1.25 lakh) salaries, charged a couple of thousand dollars, then sold to casinos for USD 2,000-3,000 (about Tk 2.5 to 3.7 lakh) <sup>[N44 (2022)](#n44), [2](#o2)</sup>.

<a id="the-bmet-card-as-a-trust-signal"></a>

#### 1.3.5 The BMET card as a trust signal

BRAC says BMET clearance is used as a trust signal, and people who go with BMET clearance and a smart card believe they are following government rules <sup>[N42](#n42), [N13](#n13)</sup>. In 2022 all five scam victims a newspaper asked had BMET smart cards <sup>[N44 (2022)](#n44)</sup>. Victims flew with clearance on visas naming construction work while they had been promised office jobs, and one broker told the worker the construction title was "temporary" <sup>[N37](#n37), [N11](#n11)</sup>. One Lakshmipur returnee with BMET clearance found he had only a one-month visit visa <sup>[N37](#n37)</sup>. One victim's fingerprint did not match at the airport Probashi desk, and an official called his agency and let him board <sup>[N46 (2023)](#n46)</sup>. When checks tightened, the CID (Criminal Investigation Department of the Bangladesh police) says, recruiters used fake BMET cards or sent workers to Thailand as tourists before moving them to Cambodia <sup>[N37](#n37)</sup>. Rights groups say the pattern has grown since 2021, driven by online tourist visas that are easy to get and that traffickers use alongside BMET clearance to make a fake job look legitimate <sup>[N42](#n42)</sup>.

<a id="the-scam-compound-chain"></a>

#### 1.3.6 The scam-compound chain

On arrival a Bangladeshi receiver meets the worker, keeps him in a hotel for a few days, then drives him to a compound "for an interview" and sells him <sup>[N13](#n13)</sup>. The prices reported are USD 1,500 to 5,000 (about Tk 1.9 to 6.2 lakh), and Bangladeshi officials report brokers collect USD 2,000 to 5,000 per person <sup>[N13](#n13), [N14](#n14), [N51](#n51), [2](#o2)</sup>. The compound then tells the worker he owes it a debt to work off, or demands a ransom from the family to free him (see [Scam compounds and trafficking](#scam-compounds-and-trafficking)) <sup>[N52](#n52), [N14](#n14)</sup>.

<a id="what-you-pay-and-what-you-earn-and-keep"></a>

### 1.4 What you pay, and what you earn and keep

On paper the employer pays the costs of getting to Cambodia and the agency may take Tk 80,520 to Tk 1,55,450 <sup>[1](#o1), [20](#o20), [21](#o21)</sup>. In practice people paid Tk 2 to 7 lakh and more for office jobs that did not exist, while the jobs in the permission letters pay USD 420 (about Tk 52,400) a month <sup>[N11](#n11), [N42](#n42), [N16](#n16), [1](#o1), [2](#o2)</sup>.

<a id="the-cap"></a>

#### 1.4.1 The cap

- **The agency's fee:** at most Tk 80,520 to Tk 1,55,450, depending on the permission letter (see [The Bangladeshi agencies](#the-bangladeshi-agencies)), taken only by cheque, bank draft or pay order once the visa and flight are confirmed <sup>[1](#o1), [20](#o20), [32](#o32), [21](#o21), [33](#o33)</sup>.
- **Travel to Cambodia:** the employer bears all costs until arrival, including the levy and airfare <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.
- **Visa and work permit fees:** the employer pays them in advance and then deducts them from the worker's salary <sup>[32](#o32), [21](#o21)</sup>.
- **BMET's fees:** a Tk 5,500 welfare fee to the WEWB (Wage Earners' Welfare Board, the government's welfare board for migrant workers), Tk 1,000 insurance and a Tk 250 clearance fee ([Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), [Step 6: Orientation, BMET clearance and the flight](#step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight)) <sup>[50](#o50), [51](#o51), [5](#o5)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How does BMET set the cap for Cambodia, and why did it rise from Tk 80,770 to Tk 1,55,450 within 2025?

<a id="what-people-really-pay"></a>

#### 1.4.2 What people really pay

- **Tk 2 to 7 lakh:** what many scam-compound victims paid (October 2026); a Narsingdi man paid a broker Tk 2.47 lakh for a receptionist job promised at about Tk 80,000 a month (2023) <sup>[N11](#n11), [N46 (2023)](#n46)</sup>.
- **Tk 4.8 lakh:** through a local middleman and Fahim Recruiting Agency, with a BMET card and a valid work visa (TBS, July 2026) <sup>[N10](#n10)</sup>.
- **Tk 5 lakh:** a construction worker displaced by the border fighting in December 2025 <sup>[N53](#n53)</sup>.
- **Tk 5.3 lakh:** the most recruiters charged for promised IT and computer-operator jobs (2026) <sup>[N10](#n10)</sup>.
- **Tk 5.5 lakh and Tk 5.6 lakh:** paid to brokers by a man who flew with BMET clearance and was let in on a visit visa, and spent by a man who was then sold for USD 2,000 (about Tk 2.5 lakh) and asked for another USD 2,000 to arrange his visa home (2026) <sup>[N24](#n24), [N49](#n49), [2](#o2)</sup>.
- **Tk 7 lakh and Tk 7.7 lakh:** for a computer-operator job promised at Tk 80,000 a month, and for a data-entry job (2026) <sup>[N37](#n37), [N11](#n11)</sup>.
- **Tk 10 lakh:** a Rajbari man promised a work visa and sent on a tourist visa (2026) <sup>[N42](#n42)</sup>.

A Bangla visa-guide site (January 2025) quotes Tk 4 to 6 lakh through an agency, Tk 6 to 8 lakh through a broker and Tk 2 to 3 lakh through a relative in Cambodia <sup>[N54](#n54)</sup>.

<a id="how-it-is-paid"></a>

#### 1.4.3 How it is paid

In parts, to more than one person: one man paid Tk 1 lakh to the broker and the rest to the agency <sup>[N37](#n37)</sup>. At Dhaka airport the broker had him stand by a pillar and send a WhatsApp photo, a man got his boarding pass issued, and a broker's contact handed him USD 2,000 (about Tk 2.5 lakh) in cash before boarding, which was taken from him when he entered the compound <sup>[N37](#n37), [N13](#n13), [2](#o2)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What is the USD 2,000 handed to workers at Dhaka airport for: cash to show Cambodian immigration, or money for the receiver in Phnom Penh?

<a id="how-they-borrow"></a>

#### 1.4.4 How they borrow

Families borrow: one family borrowed much of Tk 6 lakh for a computer job promised at about Tk 1.5 lakh a month, and the father then spent about Tk 13 lakh over 1.5 years supporting his son in Cambodia <sup>[N36](#n36)</sup>. Families borrow again to get a worker out: one family borrowed another Tk 2 lakh to bring a son home through Thailand, and another's ordeal cost nearly Tk 9 lakh in all <sup>[N49](#n49)</sup>. PKB (Probashi Kallyan Bank, the government bank for migrants) lends up to Tk 3 lakh at 9% simple interest ([Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), [Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job)) <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What monthly rate does a village moneylender charge on a Cambodia loan in 2026?

<a id="what-the-job-pays"></a>

#### 1.4.5 What the job pays

- **Construction, on the permission letters:** USD 420 (about Tk 52,400) a month, or USD 450 (about Tk 56,100) in Mondol Trade International's January 2025 letter, for 8 hours a day, 6 days a week, with food, housing, transport and medical care free <sup>[1](#o1), [35](#o35), [2](#o2)</sup>.
- **Garment, textile, footwear, travel-goods and bag factories:** the only jobs with a legal minimum wage, USD 210 (about Tk 26,200) a month in 2026 and USD 212 (about Tk 26,400) from 1 January 2027; construction and other work has none <sup>[N55](#n55), [N56](#n56), [N57](#n57), [2](#o2)</sup>.
- **Construction and farm work, according to a Bangla guide site (June 2026):** USD 300 to 400 (about Tk 37,400 to 49,900) a month <sup>[N58](#n58), [2](#o2)</sup>.
- **The office jobs promised in Bangladesh:** Tk 50,000 to over Tk 1 lakh, up to about Tk 1.5 lakh, a month <sup>[N11](#n11), [N36](#n36)</sup>. Every BMET permission letter for Cambodia is for construction workers (see [The Bangladeshi agencies](#the-bangladeshi-agencies)) <sup>[1](#o1), [19](#o19), [32](#o32), [21](#o21), [33](#o33), [34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>.

Salary tax comes out of pay: 0% for a tax resident on monthly pay up to 1,500,000 riel (about Tk 45,800), but a flat 20% for a non-resident (see [Tax and deductions from your pay](#tax-and-deductions-from-your-pay)) <sup>[N59](#n59), [3](#o3)</sup>.

A worker who pays for his own food and housing in Phnom Penh faces these costs (2026):

- **Food for one, cooking mostly local food:** about USD 150 to 250 (about Tk 18,700 to 31,200) a month <sup>[N1](#n1), [2](#o2)</sup>.
- **Rent:** about USD 7 (about Tk 870) per square metre a month in basic local buildings <sup>[N1](#n1), [2](#o2)</sup>.
- **Electricity:** USD 30 to 60 (about Tk 3,700 to 7,500) a month <sup>[N1](#n1), [2](#o2)</sup>.
- **For comparison:** a 2025 study found Cambodian garment workers took home USD 287 (about Tk 35,800) a month on average, against median household spending of USD 391 (about Tk 48,700) <sup>[N60](#n60), [2](#o2)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What do Bangladeshi construction workers in Phnom Penh earn a month in 2026 after the visa and permit fees are deducted, and do employers really give free food and housing?

<a id="what-goes-unpaid"></a>

#### 1.4.6 What goes unpaid

- **Almost nothing for 1.5 years:** a Pirojpur man sent as a construction worker worked almost unpaid while brokers held the workers' passports and gave them USD 50 (about Tk 6,200) a month for upkeep (2023) <sup>[N46 (2023)](#n46), [2](#o2)</sup>.
- **Five months unpaid:** six Bangladeshis who flew to Sihanoukville in January 2026 worked about five months as labourers without pay, then crawled under the border fence into Thailand <sup>[N61](#n61)</sup>.
- **One month's pay, then nothing:** a man in a scam compound was paid USD 476 (about Tk 59,300) for the first month and nothing after <sup>[N13](#n13), [2](#o2)</sup>.
- **Fees deducted from pay:** the visa and work-permit fees (see [The cap](#the-cap)) <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>. Deducting a recruitment or placement fee from wages is banned <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>.

How to claim: see [If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid).

<a id="money-sent-home"></a>

#### 1.4.7 Money sent home

Bangladesh Bank counts remittances from Cambodia under "Others", so there is no official figure for money sent from Cambodia <sup>[53 (2024)](#o53)</sup>. bKash, a Bangladeshi mobile-money service, does not list Cambodia among the countries it receives remittances from, and hundi (money passed outside the banks through private agents) is illegal in Bangladesh (see [Sending money home](#sending-money-home)) <sup>[N62](#n62), [N63 (2024)](#n63)</sup>. One worker promised Tk 1.5 lakh a month could send nothing home <sup>[N36](#n36)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How much does a Bangladeshi worker in Cambodia send home a month, and through what (bank app, Western Union, hundi)?

<a id="how-long-the-debt-takes"></a>

#### 1.4.8 How long the debt takes

A Tk 4.8 lakh deal equals about 9 months of the full USD 420 (about Tk 52,400) the permission letters set, before any deduction <sup>[N10](#n10), [1](#o1), [2](#o2)</sup>. One returnee who applied for a casino job spent USD 4,047 (about Tk 5 lakh) and earned USD 972 (about Tk 1.2 lakh) <sup>[N50](#n50), [2](#o2)</sup>. A Bangladesh government official warned in 2019 that a worker who pays extra to go to Cambodia is unlikely to earn it back, because higher pay is not available there <sup>[N45 (2019)](#n45)</sup>. The same official put the government costs of sending one worker, the visa fee included, at about USD 1,200 (about Tk 1.5 lakh), and said a worker must pay USD 287 (about Tk 35,800) a year to renew the visa and USD 160 (about Tk 19,900) for the work permit, and spend about USD 100 (about Tk 12,500) a month on food, against pay of USD 400 to 450 (about Tk 49,900 to 56,100) a month <sup>[N45 (2019)](#n45), [2](#o2)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How many months does a construction worker on USD 420 who paid Tk 4.8 lakh take to clear the debt in 2026, after deductions, food and rent?

<a id="what-ties-you-to-the-employer"></a>

### 1.5 What ties you to the employer

Fully: the employer gets the work permit and employment card, and MLVT can cancel the permit if the worker is hired by another employer or out of work for more than a month <sup>[54 (2023)](#o54), [7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>.

- **To enter:** the employer must hold an approved quota of foreign workers before any permit, and invites you on the E-class visa ([Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), [Step 3: The employer gets your work permit](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-work-permit)) <sup>[N5](#n5), [9](#o9)</sup>.
- **To renew:** the work permit lasts one year and is renewed each year between 1 January and 31 March; from the second long-term EB extension, your stay depends on a valid work permit (see [Your residence permit](#your-residence-permit)) <sup>[N4](#n4), [N3 (2024)](#n3)</sup>.
- **To quit:** a fixed-term contract can be ended early only by written agreement in front of a labour inspector, or for serious misconduct or force majeure, and a worker who walks out without good reason can be made to pay the employer damages <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>.
- **To change employer:** a new employer must get you a new permit (see [Changing employer](#changing-employer)) <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7), [N2 (2024)](#n2)</sup>. The owner of Fahim Recruiting Agency says workers who leave for better-paid jobs are often not taken back by their first employers <sup>[N43](#n43)</sup>.
- **To go home on leave:** BMET's January 2025 permission letter makes the employer pay the airfare both ways (see [Going home on leave](#going-home-on-leave)) <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.
- **Which law covers you:** Cambodia's Labour Law (1997) covers every job done in Cambodia, whatever the worker's nationality (see [Your rights at work](#your-rights-at-work)), and the Law on Immigration (1994) covers entry, stay and expulsion <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7), [55 (1994)](#o55)</sup>.
- **Your passport:** brokers have held workers' passports on construction sites, and compounds take them on entry <sup>[N46 (2023)](#n46), [N13](#n13)</sup>.
- **If the visa expires:** an overstay costs USD 10 (about Tk 1,250) a day; people deported for illegal entry or work are barred from Cambodia for at least three to five years, and those who refused a voluntary-departure window and were then detained are barred for life (see [What can get you jailed or deported](#what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported)) <sup>[N17](#n17), [N64](#n64), [N7](#n7), [2](#o2)</sup>.

<a id="what-goes-wrong-most-and-what-people-wrongly-believe"></a>

### 1.6 What goes wrong most, and what people wrongly believe

The danger that defines this corridor is the BMET-cleared route itself: people pay several lakh taka for an office job, fly with BMET clearance and are sold into a scam compound (see [Scam compounds and trafficking](#scam-compounds-and-trafficking)) <sup>[N11](#n11), [N13](#n13), [N15](#n15)</sup>. The commonest outcomes reported:

- **No job:** a BRAC study found 98% of returnees from Cambodia had not got the promised job, 90% were forced into online scams and 86% had their papers confiscated <sup>[N16](#n16)</sup>.
- **Trapped in a compound:** more than 1,000 BMET-cleared Bangladeshis were still in scam compounds in July 2026, by BRAC's count <sup>[N10](#n10)</sup>.
- **The wrong papers:** an invitation letter that named construction work instead of the promised data-entry job, a visit or tourist visa of one or two months instead of a work visa, and a promised work permit that never came <sup>[N11](#n11), [N37](#n37), [N49](#n49)</sup>.
- **Stranded:** the Embassy in Bangkok received requests for help from 1,796 Bangladeshis stranded in Cambodia by October 2026 <sup>[N36](#n36)</sup>.
- **Deported:** 3,364 Bangladeshis between 1 September 2023 and 13 September 2026, 852 of them linked to online scams, according to Cambodia's Ministry of Interior <sup>[N11](#n11)</sup>.
- **Dead:** the bodies of three Bangladeshis came home from Cambodia on 8 October 2026, among them Sohag Ahmed, 23, who went in April 2025 with BMET clearance <sup>[N12](#n12)</sup>.

<a id="what-people-believe-and-the-facts"></a>

#### 1.6.1 What people believe, and the facts

- **"A BMET card means the job is real."** Victims sold into compounds had BMET smart cards, and BRAC says there is no way to know where the 16,000 who went with clearance are <sup>[N44 (2022)](#n44), [N13](#n13), [N36](#n36)</sup>. A Lakshmipur worker paid Tk 5.3 lakh, got clearance, received no work permit in Cambodia and was sold to a scam compound <sup>[N10](#n10)</sup>.
- **"The visa says construction, but that is only temporary."** A broker told a man promised a computer job exactly that; his visa named a construction company, and he ended up in a compound <sup>[N37](#n37), [N13](#n13)</sup>.
- **"It is a two-year work visa."** Many victims travelled on one- or two-month tourist visas while brokers told them they had two-year work visas <sup>[N49](#n49)</sup>.
- **"Go on a tourist visa; the work visa will be fixed in Cambodia."** Working on a tourist visa is prohibited, turning it into an E-class visa after arrival is not an official route, and the Ministry tells people never to go for work this way <sup>[9](#o9), [N6](#n6), [6](#o6)</sup>.
- **"Cambodia is a step to a job in Thailand, Canada, Australia or the UAE."** Some victims were told they were going to those countries and were taken to Cambodia instead, or told to "gain experience" in the compound first <sup>[N65](#n65), [N13](#n13)</sup>. Bangladeshis who crossed on foot from Cambodia into Thailand in 2026 were arrested and charged there (see [Irregular routes](#irregular-routes-and-where-an-irregular-stay-ends), and where an irregular stay ends) <sup>[N66](#n66), [N61](#n61)</sup>.
- **"The compounds are gone now."** On 28 August 2026 the CCOS (Commission for Combating Online Scams, the Cambodian government body leading the crackdown) said Cambodia no longer hosts any scam compounds; an independent anti-trafficking consultant says scam operations and recruitment into them continue, and Amnesty International identified 86 compounds by April 2026 <sup>[N67](#n67), [N68](#n68)</sup>.
- **"Foreigners now get 2 years' jail and a USD 8,000 fine."** That notice, for 1 June 2026, was a forgery, rejected by Cambodia's Ministry of Interior on 29 May 2026; the overstay fine is USD 10 (about Tk 1,250) a day <sup>[N69](#n69), [N17](#n17), [2](#o2)</sup>.

<a id="irregular-routes-and-where-an-irregular-stay-ends"></a>

#### 1.6.2 Irregular routes, and where an irregular stay ends

- **A tourist or visit visa with a job promise: it ends in a compound or an overstay.** One man paid Tk 5.5 lakh, flew with BMET clearance on a visit visa that was never renewed, found no company and was sold to a scam compound; RAB said in 2022 that a gang sent Bangladeshis on Cambodian tourist e-visas arranged with Bangladeshis living there <sup>[N24](#n24), [N70 (2022)](#n70)</sup>.
- **Through Thailand as a tourist: it ends in the same place.** Recruiters sent workers to Thailand as tourists and moved them into Cambodia <sup>[N37](#n37)</sup>. Some traffickers work from Dubai, recruiting Bangladeshi migrant workers there and taking them across the Thai border into Cambodia, Laos or Myanmar <sup>[N51](#n51)</sup>.
- **On overland to Thailand and Malaysia: it ends in a Thai jail.** Since early 2023 Bangladeshis have been smuggled from Cambodia into Thailand, some on their way to Malaysia <sup>[N71 (2024)](#n71)</sup>. In April 2026, 21 who had paid brokers Tk 7 lakh each for passage to Malaysia crossed on foot from Cambodia and were charged with illegal entry and stay in Thailand; in July 2026 seven more, with no passports, paid a smuggling network 8,000 baht each to be walked across by Cambodian guides and were caught <sup>[N66](#n66), [N72](#n72)</sup>. Every land checkpoint into Thailand's Sa Kaeo province stayed closed in June 2026 <sup>[N73](#n73)</sup>.
- **Staying after the visa or permit ends: it ends in detention and deportation.** Cambodia deported more than 3,000 Bangladeshis in January to September 2026 <sup>[N18](#n18)</sup>. On 3 September 2026, 32 Bangladeshis trying to fly home under the fine waiver were detained at Phnom Penh airport, and 30 were held for more than six weeks before reaching Dhaka (see [Going home without valid papers](#going-home-without-valid-papers)) <sup>[N9](#n9), [N74](#n74), [N75](#n75)</sup>. A survivors' group warns that deported Bangladeshis may be lured back into Cambodia illegally through another country <sup>[N76](#n76)</sup>.

<a id="visas-not-open-to-bangladeshis"></a>

## 2 Visas not open to Bangladeshis

- **Visa-free entry:** only for ordinary passports of the other ASEAN countries, the Maldives and Seychelles; Bangladeshis always need a visa <sup>[56](#o56), [N77](#n77)</sup>.
- **Official visa (B):** only for official or service passports on government missions <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>.
- **Courtesy visa (C):** only for members of registered international NGOs <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>.
- **Khmer visa (K):** only for people of Khmer origin or Cambodian ancestry, including children of Cambodians; marrying a Cambodian does not qualify you <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>.
- **Retirement extension (ER):** only for people aged 55 or over <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>.
- **Working on a tourist visa (T):** employment on it is prohibited, and turning it into an E-class visa after arrival is not an official route (see [Changing visa type](#changing-visa-type)) <sup>[9](#o9), [N6](#n6)</sup>.
- **Citizenship by investment:** since 1 December 2025, only for investors of at least USD 1 million (about Tk 12.5 crore) or donors of USD 3 million (about Tk 37.4 crore) to the national budget; ordinary naturalisation is under Permanent residence and citizenship <sup>[N78](#n78), [2](#o2)</sup>.

<a id="work-visa-company-worker"></a>

## 3 Work visa: company worker

A Cambodian employer with an approved foreign-worker quota hires you; you enter on an E-class (ordinary or "business") visa, the employer gets your work permit and employment card from MLVT (the Ministry of Labour and Vocational Training) through FWCMS (the Foreign Workers Centralized Management System, MLVT's online system), and you stay on by extending the visa as an EB (work or business) extension from the GDI (General Department of Immigration) <sup>[N5](#n5), [N3 (2024)](#n3), [10](#o10), [N2 (2024)](#n2), [8](#o8), [N6](#n6), [N8](#n8), [N79](#n79)</sup>. On the Bangladesh side the job must come through a licensed recruiting agency holding a BMET (Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training) permission for a named Cambodian employer, and you need BMET clearance to leave <sup>[5](#o5), [1](#o1), [6](#o6)</sup>. BMET cleared no workers for Cambodia in October 2026 or from 1 to 10 November 2026 (see [Status](#status)) <sup>[23](#o23)</sup>.

<a id="step-1-find-a-real-job"></a>

### 3.1 Step 1: Find a real job

The legal jobs on BMET's permission letters for Cambodia are for men aged 20 to 45, as construction workers for firms in Phnom Penh, at USD 420 a month (about Tk 52,400), or USD 450 (about Tk 56,100) in one letter, on 2-year contracts <sup>[1](#o1), [19](#o19), [21](#o21), [33](#o33), [35](#o35), [2](#o2)</sup>. How a job order reaches a village, and who sells it, is in Background (see [How a job reaches you](#how-a-job-reaches-you-and-who-takes-a-cut), and who takes a cut).

1. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-1"></a>**Register on the OEP (Overseas Employment Platform),** BMET's website for job-seekers <sup>[4](#o4)</sup>. Agencies must pick workers for Cambodia from this database <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[57](#o57), [4](#o4)</sup>:

   - your NID (national identity card);
   - a mobile number;
   - Tk 200 for the government fee.

   1. Open [employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/registration](https://employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/registration) and type your name in English, your mobile and NID numbers, and your date of birth, division and district exactly as on the NID; type the captcha, accept the terms and press Verify <sup>[57](#o57)</sup>.
   2. Print 2 copies of the registration slip and take them to your DEMO (District Employment and Manpower Office, BMET's district office) to give your fingerprints; the DEMO list is on BMET's website <sup>[4](#o4), [58](#o58)</sup>.

   **If it fails:** call the Probashi call centre, 16135 (free), or +8809610102030 from abroad <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>.

2. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-2"></a>**Know what the legal job looks like, and what it doesn't.** BMET's permission letters for Cambodia are for construction workers in Phnom Penh at USD 420 a month, or USD 450 in one <sup>[1](#o1), [19](#o19), [32](#o32), [21](#o21), [33](#o33), [34 (2023)](#o34), [35](#o35)</sup>. The letters promise free food, housing, transport and medical care, an 8-hour day, 6 days a week, and return airfare for leave paid by the employer <sup>[1](#o1), [19](#o19)</sup>. Foreigners may not work in Cambodia as drivers, street vendors, masseurs, hairdressers or beauticians, shoe shiners, tailors, mechanics or tyre repairers, or makers of Khmer souvenirs, musical instruments or Buddhist statues <sup>[N4](#n4)</sup>. Offers of computer-operator, data-entry or customer-service jobs at Tk 50,000 to over Tk 1 lakh a month are the offers behind the scam-compound cases (see [Scam compounds and trafficking](#scam-compounds-and-trafficking)) <sup>[N11](#n11), [N10](#n10), [N80](#n80)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does BMET ask for a TTC (Technical Training Centre) construction skill certificate for Cambodia clearances, and do Cambodian employers test workers before hiring?

3. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-3"></a>**Find the agency's BMET permission letter.** BMET posts every permission for a Cambodia job order on [bmet.gov.bd/pages/miscellaneous-infos](https://bmet.gov.bd/pages/miscellaneous-infos); search the page for "কম্বোডিয়া" (Cambodia) <sup>[49](#o49)</sup>. Each letter names the agency and its RL number (the recruiting licence number every licensed agency has), the Cambodian employer, the job, the number of workers, the salary and the most the agency may take from you <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>. The letters, all for construction workers:

   - **T.K. Enterprise (RL-1527),** 20 November 2023: 33 workers for Rafa Construction Co. Ltd., Phnom Penh <sup>[34 (2023)](#o34)</sup>.
   - **Sams Company (RL-0696),** January 2025: 18 workers for North Asian Construction Co. Ltd.; cap Tk 80,770; OEP licence status Suspended <sup>[1](#o1), [46](#o46)</sup>. A second Sams letter, 6 February 2025: 24 workers for Koh Kong Construction Co. Ltd., Phnom Penh, at USD 420 on a renewable 2-year contract; cap Tk 80,770 <sup>[27](#o27)</sup>. Mondol Trade International (RL-2626), 26 January 2025: 27 workers for Royal Bengol Engineering and Constructors Co. Ltd. (9) and Yukon Builders (Cambodia) Co. Ltd. (18), at USD 450 for 48 hours a week; cap Tk 80,770 <sup>[35](#o35)</sup>.
   - **Puspita Overseas (RL-2648),** Fakirapool, Dhaka, 24 March 2025: 144 workers for Chip Hong Insee Ltd., Sim Sia Cambodia, Sok Hang Group, Vain SR Construction and Phanimex Group; cap Tk 80,770 <sup>[19](#o19), [47](#o47)</sup>. An earlier Puspita letter, 13 March 2025: 68 workers for L.Y.P. Group, Soma Group and Sinohydro (Cambodia) United, at USD 420; cap Tk 80,770 <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>.
   - **Al-Grand Sultan Overseas (RL-1582),** Savar, 20 April 2025: 96 workers for Vain SR, Sok Hang, Sim Sia and Phanimex; cap Tk 80,520 <sup>[20](#o20)</sup>.
   - **Fahim Recruiting Agency (RL-2594),** Naya Paltan, 25 June 2025: 96 workers for China State Construction (Cambodia) Co. Ltd. and Asbic Construction Company Ltd.; cap Tk 1,05,000; OEP licence status Suspended <sup>[32](#o32), [48](#o48), [46](#o46)</sup>.
   - **Mongol Global Services (RL-2527),** 31 August 2025: 144 workers for Xin Caifu (Cambodia) Co., Ltd., Maliha Jamman M.S Co., Ltd. and Golden Cresent Development Co., Ltd.; cap Tk 1,55,450; OEP licence status Suspended <sup>[21](#o21), [24](#o24), [46](#o46)</sup>.
   - **SAM International (RL-1203),** 10 September 2025: 100 workers for Royal Bengol Engineering and Constructors, Xin Cai Fu (Cambodia), Abrar Pvt (Cambodia) and Yong Sheng Global Trading, 25 each; cap Tk 80,770 <sup>[33](#o33), [25](#o25)</sup>.

   **Check it is real:**

   1. On [oep.gov.bd/agencies](https://www.oep.gov.bd/agencies), find the RL number and check that the status says Active, not Expired or Cancelled, and that the validity date has not passed <sup>[60](#o60)</sup>.
   2. Check that the employer and job in your offer are on the agency's permission letter <sup>[49](#o49), [1](#o1)</sup>.
   3. Ask the agency for the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare's approval of the demand letter (the employer's written job order): under the Ministry's directive of 4 November 2026, BMET may not clear workers for Cambodia without it (see [Status](#status)) <sup>[N22](#n22), [N21](#n21), [N12](#n12)</sup>.

   A permission letter is not proof the job is safe: a Chapainawabganj worker told TBS (July 2026) that he paid Tk 4.8 lakh through a local middleman and Fahim Recruiting Agency, left with a BMET clearance card and a valid work visa, and endured 10 months of hardship in Cambodia <sup>[N10](#n10), [N43](#n43)</sup>. Fahim's proprietor told TBS that some workers leave their assigned employers for better pay or become unreachable, and that only three workers had complained against the agency at BMET <sup>[N10](#n10)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Has BMET issued any permission for Cambodia since September 2025, and are any 2025 permissions still open for recruitment?
   > - Can a worker see the Ministry's approval of his own job order, and where?

4. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-4"></a>**Have the papers checked before you pay.** BMET checks job visas free within 3 working days at its DEMOs or at BMET, 89/2 Kakrail, Dhaka, but it cannot check Cambodian visas online (its online check covers only Saudi, Qatari, Bahraini, Singapore and UAE visas) <sup>[51](#o51), [5](#o5), [61 (2021)](#o61)</sup>. The Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare tells people going to Cambodia to verify job offers through the Ministry, BMET or the embassy <sup>[N81 (2024)](#n81)</sup>. Bangladesh has no embassy in Cambodia; the Embassy in Bangkok covers it, and its labour wing's email is fsbangkok@probashi.gov.bd <sup>[62](#o62), [N9](#n9), [63](#o63)</sup>. Check the visa yourself as in [Step 5: The visa, and checking it is real](#step-5-the-visa-and-checking-it-is-real) <sup>[64](#o64)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Can a worker in Bangladesh check that a Cambodian employer is a registered company (Ministry of Commerce online register), and does the Embassy's labour wing check the employer before attesting a demand letter?

5. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-5"></a>**Never go on a tourist or visit visa on a broker's promise of a job, and never without BMET clearance** <sup>[6](#o6)</sup>. Turning a tourist visa into an E-class visa after arrival is not an official route <sup>[N6](#n6)</sup>. What happened to people who went this way is in Scams (see [Done to workers](#done-to-workers)) <sup>[N24](#n24), [N42](#n42)</sup>.

6. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-6"></a>**Know what you should pay.** The employer bears all costs until you arrive in Cambodia, including levy and airfare <sup>[1](#o1), [24](#o24)</sup>. Once your visa and flight are confirmed, the agency may take no more than the cap in its permission letter, only by cheque, bank draft or pay order, and must send BMET's Director General proof of the payment <sup>[1](#o1), [33](#o33)</sup>. Pay only into the agency's bank account and keep every receipt <sup>[51](#o51)</sup>. Two 2025 letters let the employer pay your visa fee (June) or your visa and work-permit fees (August) and then deduct them from your salary <sup>[32](#o32), [21](#o21)</sup>. The official costs on your side, each in the step that owns it:

   - OEP registration: Tk 200 ([Step 1, item 1: Register on the OEP (Overseas Employment Platform)](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-1)) <sup>[4](#o4)</sup>;
   - passport: Tk 4,025 to Tk 10,350 ([Step 4, item 1: Get an e-passport](#step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-1)) <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>;
   - police clearance, if asked: Tk 1,500 ([Step 4, item 2: Get a police clearance if the employer or agency asks for one](#step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-2)) <sup>[66](#o66)</sup>;
   - pre-departure orientation: set by the training centre, Tk 200 at Feni TTC ([Step 6, item 1: Do the compulsory 3-day pre-departure orientation (PDO)](#step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-1)) <sup>[5](#o5), [67](#o67)</sup>;
   - BMET clearance: Tk 5,500 welfare fee, Tk 1,000 insurance, Tk 250 clearance fee and source tax of Tk 250 to Tk 1,200 ([Step 6, item 2: Get BMET clearance (the smart card)](#step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-2)) <sup>[50](#o50), [51](#o51), [5](#o5)</sup>.

   What people really pay, and to whom, is in Background (see ["What you pay, and what you earn and keep"](#what-you-pay-and-what-you-earn-and-keep)).

7. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-7"></a>**If you need to borrow,** PKB (Probashi Kallyan Bank, the government bank for migrant workers) lends up to Tk 3 lakh at 9% simple interest, repaid monthly over up to 3 years, and decides within 7 working days <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>:

   - to be 18 to 60, with you and your guarantors living in the branch's area;
   - a PKB savings account opened with at least Tk 500;
   - the free bank form, 3 recent photos and an NID copy;
   - copies of your passport, visa and BMET card, and the contract if you have one;
   - 3 signed machine-readable (MICR) cheque leaves of an active account in your own name, with a statement;
   - two guarantors: one financially solvent, one a family member or close relative.

   Fees are 0.5% processing plus VAT, 0.5% documentation plus VAT, and Tk 900 per lakh for risk cover <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>. A woman can borrow on the same terms with one guarantor, paying only the documentation fee and risk cover, and must show proof of the migration cost <sup>[68](#o68)</sup>. PKB helpline: 16238, or +880 9677-787878 from abroad <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>.

8. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-8"></a>**If an agency or broker takes your money,** complain in writing to BMET's Director General with your evidence (receipts, passport copy, messages); it is free and meant to be settled within 30 working days <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>. BMET's complaint form asks for the agency's RL number, name and address, the employer and the problem (for example job not provided, salary not paid, stranded at the airport, company does not exist), and you get a receipt with a complaint ID <sup>[69 (2024)](#o69)</sup>. Hand it in at BMET, 89/2 Kakrail, Dhaka-1000 <sup>[61 (2021)](#o61)</sup>. Then, in this order:

   1. BMET's Additional Director General (Employment): 02-48321949, 01720105492, adgemp@bmet.gov.bd <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>.
   2. The Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare's grievance officer, the Joint Secretary (Admin and Finance): 02-41030224, 01712992086, adsadmin@probashi.gov.bd (30 working days) <sup>[70](#o70)</sup>.
   3. The Probashi call centre, 16135 <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>.
   4. The police: in October 2026 a case filed at Paltan Police Station under the Human Trafficking and Migrant Smuggling Prevention and Suppression Act, 2026 led the CID (Criminal Investigation Department) to arrest two men accused of sending two young men to Cambodian scam centres <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>.

<a id="step-2-the-job-offer-and-contract"></a>

### 3.2 Step 2: The job offer and contract

A legal job in Cambodia rests on a written employment contract certified by MLVT; the work permit cannot be applied for without it <sup>[54 (2023)](#o54), [N4](#n4), [N82](#n82)</sup>.

1. <a id="step-2-the-job-offer-and-contract-item-1"></a>**Get the contract before you pay, and read it.** Under BMET's permission conditions the contract is signed with you before clearance, and you get an attested Bangla translation <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>. Get it from the agency at least 2 days before you go and check the job, employer, workplace, length, pay, leave and airfare <sup>[71 (2021)](#o71)</sup>. A fixed-duration contract must be in writing, give exact start and end dates and last at most 2 years, and renewals cannot take it past 4 years in total <sup>[N4](#n4)</sup>. It should state the salary, working hours, duties and other conditions <sup>[N4](#n4)</sup>. Many employers add a probation period, typically 3 months <sup>[N4](#n4)</sup>. Check in particular:

   - **The job title:** the same job you were promised, and the same one that will appear on your visa; a worker promised a computer-operator job told the Daily Star (July 2026) that his visa named him a construction worker with North Asian Construction Company, and the broker said the title was temporary <sup>[N83](#n83)</sup>.
   - **The pay and conditions:** USD 420 a month with free food, housing, transport and medical care, as in the permission letters ([Step 1, item 3: Find the agency's BMET permission letter](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-3)) <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.
   - **Deductions:** an employer may not deduct a placement fee or the cost of your compulsory medical check from your wages <sup>[N4](#n4)</sup>; deductions for the visa fee are allowed under two 2025 letters, and for the work-permit fee under one of them ([Step 1, item 6: Know what you should pay](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-6)) <sup>[32](#o32), [21](#o21)</sup>.

2. <a id="step-2-the-job-offer-and-contract-item-2"></a>**Make sure the employer registers the contract with MLVT.** The employer must register a foreign worker's contract with MLVT; the contract has to be in Khmer and certified by MLVT, which takes about 15 working days <sup>[54 (2023)](#o54), [N82](#n82)</sup>. To ask about it, call the MLVT employment-contract officers listed on FWCMS, 8:00 to 12:00 and 13:00 to 17:00: Mr Chuon Ansangwar 085 778 888 or Mr Neth Sophorn 012 282 250 <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does BMET or the Embassy in Bangkok ask to see the MLVT-certified Khmer contract before clearance, or only the demand letter and an English contract?
   > - Who pays MLVT's contract certification fee, and how much is it?

<a id="step-3-the-employer-gets-your-work-permit"></a>

### 3.3 Step 3: The employer gets your work permit

No foreigner may work in Cambodia without both a work permit and an employment card from the labour ministry (Labour Law 1997, Article 261) <sup>[N3 (2024)](#n3), [7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>. Getting them is the employer's job: they are two documents applied for together, in one application on FWCMS <sup>[54 (2023)](#o54), [N3 (2024)](#n3), [8](#o8)</sup>. Prakas 367/23, MLVT's 2023 order on the duties of employers and foreign workers, makes the employer hold MLVT's yearly permission to use foreign workers, sign a written contract with you and register it with MLVT, and makes you responsible for having legal permission to stay and work <sup>[54 (2023)](#o54)</sup>.

1. <a id="step-3-the-employer-gets-your-work-permit-item-1"></a>**The employer must have a foreign-worker quota.** An approved quota, MLVT's yearly permission to use foreign workers, is the first condition for any work permit; the employer registers on FWCMS (fwcms.mlvt.gov.kh) and requests it there <sup>[N5](#n5), [8](#o8), [54 (2023)](#o54)</sup>. Under an MLVT order (prakas) on the use of foreign workers, foreigners may be at most 10% of a company's Cambodian workforce: 3% office staff, 6% specialised or skilled workers and only 1% non-specialised workers <sup>[N84 (2014)](#n84), [N85](#n85)</sup>. The same order lets an employer hire foreigners only as specialists, technicians or skilled workers for posts Cambodians cannot fill, with MLVT's written permission in advance <sup>[N84 (2014)](#n84)</sup>. A company that cannot find enough qualified Cambodians can ask MLVT to go above 10% under Prakas 277/20 (a ministerial order) <sup>[N85](#n85)</sup>. Quotas for 2027 are requested from 1 September to 30 November 2026 <sup>[N5](#n5), [N84 (2014)](#n84)</sup>. MLVT's quota officers listed on FWCMS are Ms Chea Chandalin, 092 273 217, and Mr Doung Darith, 016 716 668 <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Do the Phnom Penh construction firms hiring Bangladeshis hold enough unskilled-worker quota (1%), or do they apply under Prakas 277/20?

2. <a id="step-3-the-employer-gets-your-work-permit-item-2"></a>**Give the employer your papers.** You'll need <sup>[N2 (2024)](#n2), [N4](#n4)</sup>:

   - your passport, with a valid E-class visa ([Step 5: The visa, and checking it is real](#step-5-the-visa-and-checking-it-is-real));
   - three 4 cm x 6 cm photos;
   - a health certificate ([Step 7, item 3: Get the health check](#step-7-after-arrival-item-3));
   - a residence certificate from the Sangkat (commune) chief where you live, or a rental lease certified by the chief;
   - the date of your last Cambodian exit stamp, if you have been in Cambodia before.

   The employer adds its incorporation certificate, registered address, tax patent, quota approval, Ministry of Commerce approval and articles of incorporation <sup>[N2 (2024)](#n2)</sup>.

3. <a id="step-3-the-employer-gets-your-work-permit-item-3"></a>**The permit is filed and paid for.** Under MLVT's 2026 notice, a foreigner must get the work permit through FWCMS within 90 calendar days of first entering Cambodia <sup>[N5](#n5)</sup>. Processing takes about 15 to 20 working days once all documents are in and paid for <sup>[N3 (2024)](#n3)</sup>. The fees are set by Joint Prakas 335 of 27 March 2020, MLVT and the Ministry of Economy and Finance's order on MLVT's public services <sup>[54 (2023)](#o54), [N86](#n86)</sup>; Guideline 003-15, in force since 26 January 2015, also covers them <sup>[72 (2023)](#o72)</sup>. The permit cost about USD 200 (about Tk 24,900) in 2021, and the FWCMS operator, E-Solution (Cambodia) Co., Ltd., adds USD 30 (about Tk 3,700) per new or renewed permit, paid through a bank <sup>[N3 (2024)](#n3), [N87 (2016)](#n87), [2](#o2)</sup>. Under a June 2025 permission letter the employer pays the visa fee, and under an August 2025 letter the visa and work-permit fees, and deducts them from your wage <sup>[32](#o32), [21](#o21), [24](#o24)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - What do the work permit and employment card cost under Joint Prakas 335/20 in 2026, and does it replace Guideline 003-15?

4. <a id="step-3-the-employer-gets-your-work-permit-item-4"></a>**The agency must report it.** Within 3 months of your departure the agency must submit your Cambodian work permit to BMET <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>. The permit lasts one year and is renewed each year between 1 January and 31 March (see [Renewing](#renewing)) <sup>[N4](#n4)</sup>.

**What you get back:** a work permit and an employment card (the foreign worker's "employment book"), which the employer must hand to you personally with your MLVT-registered contract; carry a copy of them at all times <sup>[54 (2023)](#o54), [N3 (2024)](#n3)</sup>. The employer must also show you legal proof of its business licence <sup>[54 (2023)](#o54)</sup>.

**Check it is real:**

1. <a id="work-visa-company-worker-item-1"></a>**Ask MLVT.** Call its foreign-worker-permit officers, Mr Sim Hong 099 555 339 or Mr Phorn Sovann 012 583 933; for the online system, 085 936 834 or 011 566 686, or email online.fwcms@gmail.com <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.
2. <a id="work-visa-company-worker-item-2"></a>**Ask BMET** whether the agency has submitted your work permit, which it must do within 3 months ([Step 6, item 2: Get BMET clearance (the smart card)](#step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-2) has BMET's emigration contacts) <sup>[21](#o21), [5](#o5)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can a worker check that a work permit number is genuine (an FWCMS lookup, a QR code on the card, or an MLVT phone line that answers in English)?

**If it fails:** an employer using a foreign worker without MLVT's permission or without an employment card can be fined KHR 12.6 million (riel, Cambodia's currency; about Tk 3.8 lakh) per worker, and you face up to 3 months in prison and/or deportation (see [What can get you jailed or deported](#what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported)) <sup>[73](#o73), [N88](#n88), [3](#o3)</sup>. A Lakshmipur worker who paid Tk 5.3 lakh and got BMET clearance received no work permit and was sold to a scam compound (TBS, July 2026) <sup>[N10](#n10)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - If MLVT refuses a work permit, are the worker and the agency told why, and can the employer appeal or reapply?

<a id="step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates"></a>

### 3.4 Step 4: Passport, police clearance and certificates

1. <a id="step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-1"></a>**Get an e-passport.** Over age 20 you need your NID, and you fill in the form exactly as on it <sup>[74](#o74)</sup>.

   1. Fill in the form online and choose the divisional or regional passport office for your present address <sup>[74](#o74)</sup>.
   2. Pay online through A-Challan, DGePay or ShurjoPay, or at any bank through A-Challan <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>. A 48-page passport costs Tk 4,025, Tk 6,325 or Tk 8,625 for 5 years and Tk 5,750, Tk 8,050 or Tk 10,350 for 10 years (regular, express, super express) <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>.
   3. Go to the office with your original NID and any technical certificate <sup>[74](#o74)</sup>. With an online-verified NID there is no police verification, and delivery is within 15, 7 or 2 working days of enrolment <sup>[75](#o75), [65](#o65)</sup>.

   The e-visa needs a passport valid for more than 6 months on the day you enter Cambodia ([Step 5, item 1: Apply for the E-class e-visa online](#step-5-the-visa-and-checking-it-is-real-item-1)) <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.

   **If it is lost or stolen:** in Bangladesh, file a GD (general diary, a police report) at the nearest police station quickly, then re-apply with a copy of the old passport and the GD <sup>[74](#o74)</sup>. Passport helpline: 16445 (09666716445 from abroad), 24 hours <sup>[76 (2023)](#o76)</sup>. In Cambodia, passports are issued by the Embassy in Bangkok (see [The Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok](#the-bangladesh-embassy-in-bangkok)); all land border crossings between Cambodia and Thailand are closed until further notice <sup>[77](#o77), [13](#o13)</sup>.

2. <a id="step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-2"></a>**Get a police clearance if the employer or agency asks for one.**

   You'll need <sup>[66](#o66)</sup>:

   - a passport valid at least 3 more months;
   - a present address that is one of the addresses in your passport;
   - Tk 1,500 by treasury challan, code 1-7301-0001-2681, at Bangladesh Bank or Sonali Bank, or online by card with a service charge.

   1. Register at [pcc.police.gov.bd](https://pcc.police.gov.bd/), log in and click Apply <sup>[66](#o66)</sup>.
   2. Fill in your personal details and your present and permanent addresses; the police check is done where you live now <sup>[66](#o66)</sup>.
   3. Upload scans of your documents <sup>[66](#o66)</sup>.
   4. Track it by texting "PCC S <reference>" to 26969, and collect it with your passport or application copy; someone else can collect it with an attested authorisation letter giving their name and NID number <sup>[66](#o66)</sup>.

   **If it fails:** helpdesk Sunday to Thursday, 9 am to 5 pm, 01320001824, 01320001825, help.pcc@police.gov.bd <sup>[66](#o66)</sup>.

3. <a id="step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-3"></a>**Attest certificates if the employer asks for them.** MoFA (the Ministry of Foreign Affairs) attests (apostilles) education certificates online on mygov.bd at Tk 200 a page in 5 working days, after the Education Board and the Ministry of Education have verified SSC and HSC (secondary and higher secondary) certificates <sup>[78](#o78), [79](#o79)</sup>. Register on mygov.bd, choose the service for your type of document, fill in the form and upload the document; an SMS tells you when to download the apostille <sup>[78](#o78)</sup>. Experience certificates are attested free once BMET or the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare has attested them and they are notarised <sup>[78](#o78)</sup>. For countries outside the online apostille arrangement, you then take the apostille and documents to MoFA's consular counter between 10 am and 1 pm for a consular sticker <sup>[78](#o78)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does FWCMS ask a Bangladeshi construction worker for any attested certificate or a police clearance, or only the passport, photos and health certificate?
   > - Does Cambodia accept MoFA's online apostille, or is the consular sticker needed?

<a id="step-5-the-visa-and-checking-it-is-real"></a>

### 3.5 Step 5: The visa, and checking it is real

To work you enter on the E-class visa, which is for people who have accepted a job offer from a registered Cambodian company, not the T-class tourist visa: only the E-class visa can be extended inside Cambodia as an EB extension <sup>[9](#o9), [N3 (2024)](#n3), [N6](#n6)</sup>. Since April 2025 the E-class e-visa is open to applicants from all countries <sup>[80](#o80)</sup>. (Tourist visas: see Visit visa.)

1. <a id="step-5-the-visa-and-checking-it-is-real-item-1"></a>**Apply for the E-class e-visa online.** The e-visa is run only by the E-Visa Office of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs and International Cooperation, at [evisa.gov.kh](https://www.evisa.gov.kh) <sup>[81](#o81), [10](#o10)</sup>. It costs USD 35 (about Tk 4,400), is single entry, is valid for 3 months from issue and lets you stay 1 month <sup>[10](#o10), [2](#o2)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[10](#o10), [82](#o82)</sup>:

   - a passport valid for more than 6 months on the day you enter;
   - a digital passport photo (JPEG or PNG);
   - a card to pay: Visa, MasterCard, JCB, Diners Club, UnionPay, Alipay or WeChat.

   1. Open the Application menu, type the security code and fill in your personal, passport and travel details <sup>[83](#o83)</sup>.
   2. Review the form and pay <sup>[83](#o83)</sup>.
   3. The approval comes within 3 business days by email, or download it from the "Check & Change" page with your reference number <sup>[82](#o82), [84](#o84)</sup>.

   You show the e-visa certificate to immigration on arrival <sup>[82](#o82)</sup>. A visa got with false information or forged documents can be refused, cancelled or revoked, and you stay responsible for every detail even when an agent applies for you <sup>[85](#o85)</sup>. Fees are not refunded once processing starts <sup>[85](#o85)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Do Cambodian employers or Bangladeshi agencies apply for the E-class e-visa for the worker, and what do agencies charge on top of USD 35?
   > - Does BMET's clearance check that the visa is E-class and not T-class?

2. <a id="step-5-the-visa-and-checking-it-is-real-item-2"></a>**Check it is real before you pay the agency the balance or fly.**

   1. **On the portal.** On [evisa.gov.kh](https://www.evisa.gov.kh) open "Check Certificate" (Check & Change) and enter the application reference number; there is a link if you have lost it <sup>[64](#o64)</sup>.
   2. **The visa type and passport.** The visa type must say E (Business), not T (Tourist), and the passport number must be yours; you must travel on the passport the e-visa was issued for <sup>[10](#o10), [86](#o86)</sup>.
   3. **The job.** The employer and job must match your contract and the permission letter ([Step 2, item 1: Get the contract before you pay, and read it](#step-2-the-job-offer-and-contract-item-1)) <sup>[N83](#n83), [1](#o1)</sup>.
   4. **Ask the e-visa office.** Cambodian embassies cannot check an e-visa's status; the E-Visa Office in Phnom Penh answers in Khmer and English on (855) 23 224 973 and (855) 99 334 973 <sup>[86](#o86), [83](#o83)</sup>.

   **If any check fails:** don't travel; complain as in [Step 1, item 8: If an agency or broker takes your money](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-8) <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - If the E-class e-visa is refused, does the E-Visa Office give a reason, and can the applicant appeal or apply again at once?

3. <a id="step-5-the-visa-and-checking-it-is-real-item-3"></a>**Enter at a port that takes e-visas.** As of 3 October 2026 e-visas were accepted at Techo International Airport (KTI, Phnom Penh), Siem Reap Angkor and Sihanoukville airports, and at the Bavet (Vietnam border) and Tropaeng Kreal (Laos border) land crossings <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>. Cham Yeam (Koh Kong) and Poipet (Banteay Meanchey) are closed to e-visa entry <sup>[82](#o82)</sup>.

4. <a id="step-5-the-visa-and-checking-it-is-real-item-4"></a>**Fill in the e-Arrival form.** Every traveller, even with a visa, fills it in, free, at [arrival.gov.kh](https://arrival.gov.kh), the only official site, or in the Cambodia e-Arrival app, within 7 days before arrival; keep a screenshot or download of its QR code <sup>[87](#o87), [88](#o88)</sup>. The GDI has disowned look-alike sites such as "Cambodia-e-arrival.online" and the fake visa site cambodiaivisa.com <sup>[89](#o89), [88](#o88)</sup>. At the three international airports and the international ports, the GDI is replacing passport entry and exit stamps in 2026 with the Cambodia v-Pass (Visitor Pass), which you get by email, at arrival.gov.kh or in the e-Arrival app <sup>[90](#o90)</sup>. Before leaving the arrival hall, every passenger also hands customs a Passenger's Declaration Form; personal belongings and devices needed for the trip, up to 2 litres of alcohol, 200 cigarettes and 350 ml of perfume come in free, and other goods worth USD 100 (about Tk 12,500) or more pay duty <sup>[N89](#n89), [2](#o2)</sup>.

   Immigration officers may refuse entry to anyone without a clear travel history and purpose, the means to support themselves and a guarantor, the GDI said in July 2025 <sup>[N79](#n79)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What happens to a Bangladeshi worker refused entry at Techo airport: is he held at the airport, and does the agency actually pay his flight back, as BMET's permission conditions require ([If you lose the job, item 4: Hold the agency to its undertaking](#if-you-lose-the-job-item-4))?

**Scams:** workers promised work visas have arrived with one-month visit visas or tourist visas, some after getting BMET clearance (see [Done to workers](#done-to-workers)) <sup>[N83](#n83)</sup>.

<a id="step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight"></a>

### 3.6 Step 6: Orientation, BMET clearance and the flight

1. <a id="step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-1"></a>**Do the compulsory 3-day pre-departure orientation (PDO)** at one of BMET's 70 training institutes; the institute sets the fee, paid against a money receipt, and Feni TTC says its fee is Tk 200 and to pay no more <sup>[5](#o5), [67](#o67)</sup>. Download your certificate at [training.oep.gov.bd/pdo-certificate](https://training.oep.gov.bd/pdo-certificate) with your passport or NID number and the captcha <sup>[91](#o91)</sup>.

2. <a id="step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-2"></a>**Get BMET clearance (the smart card).**

   - **Through an agency (group visa):** the agency applies to BMET's emigration branch with the visa copy, the attested contract, your attested registration, a Tk 300 stamp-paper undertaking that the job is certain, and the pay orders; BMET clears within 3 working days once the visa is issued <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>.
   - **On your own (single visa):** apply at BMET's emigration branch in Dhaka or the Chattogram DEMO with an application, the visa copy, the contract if you have one, an attested copy of your OEP registration, an undertaking on Tk 300 non-judicial stamp paper and the original pay order for the welfare fee and insurance; it takes 3 working days <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>. A woman on a single visa also needs a no-objection letter from her legal guardian on Tk 150 stamp paper <sup>[71 (2021)](#o71)</sup>.

   Your costs:
