   - welfare fee to the WEWB (Wage Earners' Welfare Board, the government's welfare board for migrant workers): Tk 5,500 from 1 October 2026 <sup>[50](#o50)</sup>;
   - insurance: Tk 1,000 once, 5 years' cover, Tk 10 lakh <sup>[51](#o51)</sup>;
   - clearance (smart card) fee: Tk 250 <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>;
   - source tax: Tk 1,200 (skilled) or Tk 800 (unskilled) on a group visa; Tk 400 on an unattested single visa, Tk 250 on an attested one <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>.

   **Check it is real** at [portal.wewb.gov.bd/bmet-wewbm-check](https://portal.wewb.gov.bd/bmet-wewbm-check) with your passport number or membership ID and the captcha <sup>[92](#o92)</sup>. The Welfare Board's desks at Dhaka, Chattogram and Sylhet airports check departing workers' smart cards <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>. Before you fly, check again that your visa is the E-class (business) visa, not a visit or tourist visa ([Step 5: The visa, and checking it is real](#step-5-the-visa-and-checking-it-is-real)): a returnee told the Dhaka Tribune (June 2026) that he left with BMET clearance but was let into Cambodia on a visit visa that was never renewed <sup>[N3 (2024)](#n3), [N24](#n24)</sup>.

   **If it fails:** BMET's Director (Emigration), +8802222222415, 01711-135901, diremi@bmet.gov.bd; next, the Additional Director General (Employment), 02-48321949, 01720105492, adgemp@bmet.gov.bd <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>.

3. <a id="step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-3"></a>**The flight.** Once flights are confirmed, the agency must send the flight schedule and worker list to the Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok and to BMET, and an agency representative must fly with you or meet every flight at the Cambodian airport <sup>[1](#o1), [33](#o33)</sup>. In scam-compound cases reported in 2026, a broker routed one man to Cambodia through Thailand, and another flew via China because there was no direct flight <sup>[N49](#n49), [N13](#n13), [N37](#n37)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Which airlines and transit points do Cambodia-bound workers use in 2026, does any transit airport need a visa or extra papers, and what does a one-way Dhaka-Phnom Penh ticket cost?

4. <a id="step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-4"></a>**Before you fly,** keep one set of copies at home and at least 3 with you, open at least 2 bank accounts (one yours, one a family member's), and never carry a packet whose contents you don't know <sup>[51](#o51)</sup>. Save the Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok's welfare hotline for Bangladeshis in Thailand and Cambodia, +66-818-70-84-43 (see [The Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok](#the-bangladesh-embassy-in-bangkok)) <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>.

<a id="step-7-after-arrival"></a>

### 3.7 Step 7: After arrival

Your first stay on the E-class visa is 30 days <sup>[N2 (2024)](#n2)</sup>.

1. <a id="step-7-after-arrival-item-1"></a>**Know who should meet you.** Under its permission letter the agency's representative must meet you at the airport or fly with you <sup>[1](#o1), [33](#o33)</sup>. In the scam cases, Bangladeshi handlers met workers, kept them in a hotel for a few days and then drove them to a compound "for an interview" (see [Scam compounds and trafficking](#scam-compounds-and-trafficking)) <sup>[N13](#n13), [N37](#n37)</sup>.

2. <a id="step-7-after-arrival-item-2"></a>**Make sure you are registered at your address.** FPCS (the Foreigners Presence in Cambodia System) is the GDI's online register of where foreigners live, run through the "FPCS - GDI" app and website <sup>[N8](#n8)</sup>. Your landlord, owner or guesthouse manager is usually the one who must register you; hotels usually do it automatically <sup>[N8](#n8), [N90](#n90)</sup>. Ask your landlord directly whether they use FPCS and whether you are registered <sup>[N8](#n8)</sup>. Without FPCS registration you may be unable to extend your visa, and some landlords avoid it to hide rental income from tax <sup>[N90](#n90), [N8](#n8)</sup>. For the work permit you also need a residence certificate from the Sangkat chief ([Step 3, item 2: Give the employer your papers](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-work-permit-item-2)) <sup>[N4](#n4)</sup>.

3. <a id="step-7-after-arrival-item-3"></a>**Get the health check.** The health certificate comes from MLVT's Labour Medical Department, or from another clinic with the certificate certified by MLVT; it takes 3 to 7 working days <sup>[N82](#n82)</sup>. Certifying a private clinic's certificate cost USD 5 (KHR 20,400, about Tk 620) in 2016 <sup>[N87 (2016)](#n87), [3](#o3)</sup>. Your employer may not deduct the cost of this check from your wages <sup>[N4](#n4)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - What does the MLVT medical check cost in 2026, where in Phnom Penh is the Labour Medical Department, and who pays in practice?

4. <a id="step-7-after-arrival-item-4"></a>**Extend your visa as an EB extension before the 30 days run out.** The GDI extends the E-class visa for 1, 3, 6 or 12 months at a time; 6- and 12-month extensions allow multiple entries, and 1- and 3-month extensions are single entry <sup>[N2 (2024)](#n2)</sup>. You apply inside Cambodia at the immigration office or through a visa agent <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>. Through an agent an EB extension cost USD 50 for 1 month (about Tk 6,200), USD 80 for 3 months (about Tk 10,000), USD 160 for 6 months (about Tk 19,900) and USD 290 for 1 year (about Tk 36,100) in 2024; going to the Immigration Department directly is supposed to be cheaper, but many foreigners use agents because it is slow <sup>[N3 (2024)](#n3), [2](#o2)</sup>. Your first 6- or 12-month EB extension can be had without a work permit; the second and every later long-term extension needs proof of a valid work permit <sup>[N3 (2024)](#n3)</sup>. An EB extension does not by itself give the right to work: you still need the work permit <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>. After you start work the agency must report your EB extension and regular salary payment to BMET and the Embassy in Bangkok's labour wing <sup>[33](#o33), [24](#o24)</sup>. Later renewals are in Your residence permit (see [Renewing](#renewing)) <sup>[N6](#n6)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - What does an EB extension cost at the GDI counter itself in 2026, and where is the counter in Phnom Penh?
   > - Does the employer arrange the EB extension, or must the worker go alone or through a visa agent?

5. <a id="step-7-after-arrival-item-5"></a>**Collect your work permit and employment card,** and keep a copy with you at all times ([Step 3: The employer gets your work permit](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-work-permit)) <sup>[N3 (2024)](#n3)</sup>.

6. <a id="step-7-after-arrival-item-6"></a>**Check you are registered with the NSSF** (National Social Security Fund, Cambodia's social insurance) <sup>[93 (2019)](#o93)</sup>. Every employer must register its workers, Cambodian and foreign, within 3 days of hiring, using a foreign worker's passport <sup>[93 (2019)](#o93), [94 (2021)](#o94)</sup>. The membership card is free, and you can check on the NSSF smartphone app that your employer pays contributions every month <sup>[94 (2021)](#o94), [N91 (2022)](#n91)</sup>. What it covers is in Your rights at work <sup>[93 (2019)](#o93)</sup>.

7. <a id="step-7-after-arrival-item-7"></a>**Get a SIM card.** Register the SIM of Smart, a Cambodian mobile operator, online or at a Smart Shop, with a scan of your passport and a face scan; approval is usually instant <sup>[N92](#n92)</sup>. Smart's help line: (+855) 10 200 888 or 1204 <sup>[N92](#n92)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Which mobile operator do Bangladeshi workers in Cambodia use, and what do a SIM and a monthly package cost?

8. <a id="step-7-after-arrival-item-8"></a>**Open a bank account.** It lets you send money home from your phone (see [Sending money home](#sending-money-home)) <sup>[N93](#n93), [N94](#n94), [N95](#n95)</sup>.

   - **ACLEDA Bank:** 263 branches in all provinces and towns <sup>[N96](#n96)</sup>. To register for the ACLEDA mobile app, you'll need a passport valid at least 3 more months and a phone, and to be 15 or over <sup>[N97](#n97)</sup>.

     1. Go to any branch, or register in the ACLEDA mobile app <sup>[N97](#n97)</sup>.
     2. Ask for a savings account: no opening deposit, no minimum balance and no fee to open or close (closing within 3 months may be charged), in riel, US dollars, baht or euro <sup>[N96](#n96)</sup>.

     An account with no deposit, withdrawal or transfer for more than 12 months becomes dormant and is charged fees <sup>[N98](#n98)</sup>.
   - **ABA Bank (Advanced Bank of Asia):** its app-based Instant Account is for Cambodian nationals only, so a foreigner opens a standard account at an ABA branch <sup>[N99](#n99)</sup>. You'll need to be 18 or over, your original passport with a valid visa, the application form, and proof of residence: a multiple-entry visa with at least 182 days a year in Cambodia, a rental contract or address certificate of at least 6 months, or an employment contract or certificate showing at least 6 months' work <sup>[N100](#n100)</sup>. A current account needs USD 100 (about Tk 12,500) to open and as its minimum balance, has no monthly fee and is free to close <sup>[N100](#n100), [2](#o2)</sup>. ABA has an interest-free mode for Muslim customers <sup>[N100](#n100)</sup>. Hotline: 1 800 203 203 or 098 203 203 (24 hours) <sup>[N100](#n100), [N93](#n93)</sup>.
   - **Wing Bank:** open the account in the Wing Bank App by scanning your passport; some services need a "Full KYC" (full identity check) upgrade at a Wing branch or agent shop with the same passport <sup>[N101](#n101)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Will ACLEDA or Wing open an account for a Bangladeshi on a passport and E-class visa alone, or do they ask for the work permit?
   > - Do employers of Bangladeshi workers pay wages into a bank account or in cash, and through which bank?

9. <a id="step-7-after-arrival-item-9"></a>**Keep your papers valid and on you.** Working without a permit can bring up to 3 months in prison and/or deportation (see [What can get you jailed or deported](#what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported)) <sup>[N88](#n88)</sup>.

<a id="scams"></a>

### 3.8 Scams

<a id="done-to-workers"></a>

#### 3.8.1 Done to workers

- **The office job on a construction visa.** A Sirajganj man told the Daily Star (July 2026) that a broker asked Tk 7 lakh for a computer-operator job paying Tk 80,000 a month; he paid Tk 1 lakh to the broker and the rest to an agency he named as Pushpita Overseas in Fakirapool <sup>[N37](#n37)</sup>. His visa named him a construction worker with North Asian Construction Company, and the broker said the title was temporary <sup>[N37](#n37)</sup>. In all he paid recruiters Tk 7.70 lakh (Daily Star, October 2026) <sup>[N11](#n11)</sup>.
- **A visit or tourist visa instead of a work visa.** A Lakshmipur man paid Tk 5.3 lakh for a computer-operator job, got BMET clearance and found on arrival he had a one-month visit visa (Daily Star, July 2026) <sup>[N37](#n37), [N83](#n83)</sup>. A Rajbari man told TBS (May 2026) that he paid Tk 10 lakh, was promised a work visa and was sent on a tourist visa <sup>[N42](#n42)</sup>. In 2022 an agent told a man work visas were not available and sent him on a business visa for Tk 4 lakh (TBS, 2022) <sup>[N44 (2022)](#n44)</sup>.
- **The visa and work permit that never come.** Recruiters promised a one-year visa and work permit that never came, leaving the worker without legal status (Daily Star, October 2026) <sup>[N11](#n11), [N80](#n80)</sup>.
- **The licensed agency's Facebook ad.** In June 2022 M/S Right Job, a licensed agency in Dhanmondi, advertised a Cambodia tiles-installer job on Facebook at USD 600 a month (about Tk 74,800) and agreed a Tk 3.5 lakh deal (Daily Star Bangla, January 2023) <sup>[N46 (2023)](#n46), [2](#o2)</sup>. In January 2023 the Daily Star counted 30 Facebook groups and pages offering Cambodia jobs <sup>[N46 (2023)](#n46)</sup>.
- **Unpaid construction work.** A Pirojpur man paid a village broker Tk 3.5 lakh, went as a construction worker and worked almost unpaid for 1.5 years while brokers held the workers' passports and gave them USD 50 (about Tk 6,200) a month for upkeep (Daily Star Bangla, 2023) <sup>[N46 (2023)](#n46), [2](#o2)</sup>. The US State Department reported in 2025 that brokers fraudulently recruit Bangladeshis to Cambodian construction sites, where some are indebted and have their passports confiscated <sup>[N102](#n102)</sup>.
- **Passports handed over and workers sold.** Agents handed one group's passports to a company and took USD 3,600 (about Tk 4.5 lakh) a head (TBS, 2022) <sup>[N44 (2022)](#n44), [2](#o2)</sup>. The airport handover and sale into scam compounds are in Scam compounds and trafficking <sup>[N13](#n13)</sup>.
- **Checks waved through.** A victim said his fingerprint did not match at the airport Probashi desk, and an official took him aside, called his agency and let him board (Daily Star Bangla, 2023) <sup>[N46 (2023)](#n46)</sup>. Since checks tightened, the CID says some recruiters use fake BMET cards, or send workers to Thailand as tourists and move them on to Cambodia <sup>[N37](#n37)</sup>.

Complain in Bangladesh as in [Step 1, item 8: If an agency or broker takes your money](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-8) <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What does a broker in Dhaka charge for a "Cambodia construction visa" in November 2026, after the Ministry's approval rule?

<a id="tried-by-workers"></a>

#### 3.8.2 Tried by workers

- **Working on a tourist visa, or "converting" it.** Turning a tourist visa into an E-class visa after arrival is not an official route <sup>[N6](#n6)</sup>. A foreigner who does business in Cambodia, including self-employed, without a work permit can be fined KHR 50.4 million (about Tk 15.4 lakh) under Joint Prakas 498 of 31 July 2023 (MLVT and the Ministry of Economy and Finance's joint order on fines for breaking the Labour Law), and a foreign employee working without one faces up to 3 months in prison and/or deportation <sup>[N88](#n88), [54 (2023)](#o54), [3](#o3)</sup>.
- **False information or forged documents** in a visa application get the visa refused, cancelled or revoked <sup>[85](#o85)</sup>.
- **Leaving the assigned employer for better pay.** Fahim Recruiting Agency's proprietor told TBS (July 2026) that some workers leave their employers for better-paid jobs and the employers will not take them back <sup>[N43](#n43)</sup>. MLVT can revoke a work permit when the holder is hired by another employer (see [Changing employer](#changing-employer)) <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7), [54 (2023)](#o54)</sup>.
- **Moving on to Thailand or Malaysia without papers.** In July 2026 seven of 11 people caught crossing from Cambodia into Thailand were Bangladeshis carrying no passport or ID, and in 2024 Bangladeshis in Cambodia had fake Thai entry stamps put in 19 men's passports for the trip to Malaysia (see [Irregular routes and undocumented workers](#irregular-routes-and-undocumented-workers)) <sup>[N72](#n72), [N71 (2024)](#n71)</sup>.

<a id="your-rights-at-work"></a>

### 3.9 Your rights at work

Cambodia's Labour Law (1997) covers every job done in Cambodia, whatever the nationality of the worker or the employer and wherever the contract was signed <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>. A foreigner may work only with a work permit and an employment card from MLVT (the Ministry of Labour and Vocational Training), after entering legally, with a valid passport and a valid residence permit ([Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), [Step 3: The employer gets your work permit](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-work-permit)) <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>. Forced labour, and work to pay off a debt, are banned <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>. For any broken right, use the routes under If you aren't paid <sup>[N103](#n103)</sup>.

<a id="your-contract"></a>

#### 3.9.1 Your contract

- **In writing.** The Labour Law lets a contract be written or spoken, and registering one costs nothing, but your employer must have a written contract with you <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7), [54 (2023)](#o54)</sup>. It must be registered with MLVT, in Khmer, and certified by it ([Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), [Step 2: The job offer and contract](#step-2-the-job-offer-and-contract)) <sup>[54 (2023)](#o54), [N82](#n82)</sup>.
- **Fixed-term.** A contract for a set period must be in writing with exact start and end dates, or it counts as permanent ("unlimited-duration"); one contract runs at most 2 years <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7), [N4](#n4)</sup>. BMET's (the Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training's) permission letters for Cambodian jobs set 2-year contracts <sup>[19](#o19), [24](#o24)</sup>.
- **Your Bangla copy.** Under BMET's permission conditions the contract is signed with each worker before clearance, and each worker gets an attested Bangla translation <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.
- **Probation.** At most 3 months for regular staff, 2 for specialised workers and 1 for unskilled workers; it doesn't count toward seniority pay <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7), [N104 (2019)](#n104)</sup>.
- **Pay terms.** The employer must tell you clearly what you will be paid before you start, and again whenever it changes <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>.
- **Employment certificate.** At the end of any contract you can ask for one, and an employer who refuses must pay damages <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can fixed-term renewals run to 4 years in total, or does the contract become permanent once renewals pass 2 years?
> - When the job in Cambodia differs from the BMET-attested contract, does MLVT accept the Dhaka contract as evidence?

<a id="wages-and-the-minimum-wage"></a>

#### 3.9.2 Wages and the minimum wage

- **Minimum wage.** There is a legal minimum only in garment, textile, footwear, travel-goods and bag factories; construction and every other job have none <sup>[N57](#n57)</sup>.
  - In 2026: USD 210 a month (about Tk 26,200) for regular workers and USD 208 (about Tk 25,900) on probation (Prakas 214/25; a prakas is a ministerial order) <sup>[N55](#n55), [2](#o2)</sup>.
  - From 1 January 2027: USD 212 (about Tk 26,400) for regular workers and USD 210 on probation (Prakas 226/26) <sup>[N56](#n56), [2](#o2)</sup>.
  - On top, garment workers get USD 7 (about Tk 870) a month for transport or housing, a USD 10 (about Tk 1,250) attendance bonus, USD 0.50 (about Tk 62) a day for meals on overtime or a free meal, and a seniority bonus of USD 2 to 11 (about Tk 250 to 1,370) <sup>[N55](#n55), [2](#o2)</sup>. Piece-rate workers who fall short are topped up to the minimum <sup>[N56](#n56)</sup>.
- **Your contract wage.** BMET's permission letters for Cambodian construction jobs set USD 420 a month (about Tk 52,400), or USD 450 (about Tk 56,100) in one (see [What the job pays](#what-the-job-pays)) <sup>[1](#o1), [24](#o24), [35](#o35), [2](#o2)</sup>.
- **Payday.** Twice a month: about half in the second week, the rest with benefits in the fourth (Prakas 442, 2018) <sup>[N105 (2018)](#n105)</sup>.
- **How.** Directly to you, in legal currency, and the employer may not tell you how to spend it <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>.
- **Proof.** In a dispute the employer must prove each payment with your signature in its payroll book, or two witnesses' if you can't write <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>. Signing "all wages paid", or taking your pay without protest, doesn't cancel your claim to wages still owed <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What do Bangladeshi construction, factory and casino workers in Phnom Penh and Sihanoukville actually earn a month in 2026, and is it paid in cash, into a bank account, or into an account the employer controls?

<a id="tax-and-deductions-from-your-pay"></a>

#### 3.9.3 Tax and deductions from your pay

**Salary tax.** Cambodia has no income tax as such; employees pay a monthly salary tax that the employer deducts and pays to the tax authorities within 20 days after the month, and you file no return <sup>[N59](#n59), [N106](#n106)</sup>. The rate depends on whether you are a tax resident: someone whose home is in Cambodia, or who is there more than 182 days in 12 months <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>.

- **Residents (since 1 January 2023):** 0% on monthly salary up to KHR 1,500,000 (riel, Cambodia's currency; about USD 367 or Tk 45,800); 5% up to KHR 2,000,000 (about Tk 61,000); 10% up to KHR 8,500,000 (about Tk 2.59 lakh); 15% up to KHR 12,500,000 (about Tk 3.81 lakh); 20% above <sup>[N59](#n59), [N108 (2023)](#n108), [3](#o3)</sup>. Each dependent child or spouse cuts taxable salary by KHR 150,000 (about Tk 4,600) a month <sup>[N109](#n109), [3](#o3)</sup>.
- **Non-residents:** a flat 20% on all salary, as a final tax <sup>[N59](#n59)</sup>.
- **Fringe benefits** (benefits from your employer on top of wages): 20% of the amount paid <sup>[N59](#n59)</sup>.

**What may be deducted.** No one may take a recruitment or placement fee out of your wages, for the employer, its agent or a labour recruiter, nor the cost of your medical check <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7), [N4](#n4)</sup>. Other deductions are limited to unreturned tools, advances and company-shop debts, and may never leave you without enough to live on <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>. BMET's permission letters for Cambodia let the employer pay your work permit and visa fees up front and take them back from your wages <sup>[21](#o21), [24](#o24), [32](#o32)</sup>. Your pension contribution to NSSF (the National Social Security Fund), 2% of your contributory wage, also comes out of your pay ([Pension](#pension)) <sup>[94 (2021)](#o94)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Do employers of Bangladeshi workers apply the resident rate (0% under about USD 367) from the first month, or the 20% non-resident rate until 182 days have passed?
> - How much do employers take back each month for the work permit and visa fees, and for how many months?
> - Do employers in practice give Bangladeshi workers the free food and housing the permission letters promise, or deduct them from pay, and how much?

<a id="hours-overtime-and-rest"></a>

#### 3.9.4 Hours, overtime and rest

- **Hours.** At most 8 a day and 48 a week; BMET's permission letters set 8 hours a day, Monday to Saturday <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7), [19](#o19), [25](#o25)</sup>.
- **Rest.** At most six working days a week; the weekly rest is at least 24 hours in a row, normally Sunday <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>.
- **Overtime.** Voluntary: the employer may not force it by threats or discipline (Prakas 112/25) <sup>[N110](#n110)</sup>. With overtime, hours may not pass 10 a day except for disaster work <sup>[N110](#n110)</sup>.
- **Overtime pay.** 150% of normal pay in the daytime on working days; 200% at night (10 pm to 5 am) and on your weekly day off <sup>[N110](#n110), [7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>. After night overtime the employer must give you a place to sleep or transport <sup>[N110](#n110)</sup>.
- **Check it.** The employer must file overtime at least three working days ahead on LACMS, MLVT's online system, with the agreement of over half the workers concerned or two-thirds of staff representatives <sup>[N110](#n110)</sup>.

<a id="leave-and-public-holidays"></a>

#### 3.9.5 Leave and public holidays

- **Annual leave.** 1.5 working days for each month worked, 18 a year, plus a day for every three years of service <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7), [N111](#n111)</sup>. You can take it after one year; if the contract ends first, you are paid for the leave you earned <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>.
- **Public holidays.** Work on a paid public holiday is voluntary <sup>[N110](#n110)</sup>. The 2026 holidays include Khmer New Year (14-16 April), Pchum Ben (10-12 October) and the Water Festival (23-25 November) <sup>[N112](#n112)</sup>. A holiday on a Sunday gives no extra day off <sup>[N111](#n111)</sup>.

<a id="sick-leave-and-maternity"></a>

#### 3.9.6 Sick leave and maternity

- **Sickness.** A doctor-certified illness suspends your contract for up to six months <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>. The Labour Law gives no sick pay; MLVT policy is full pay in the first month, 60% in the second and third, then unpaid to the sixth <sup>[N113](#n113)</sup>.
- **NSSF sickness pay.** If a doctor signs you off for more than 7 days in a row, NSSF pays 70% of your contributory wage for up to 180 days in 12 months, once you have 2 months of health contributions in a row or 6 in the last 12 <sup>[93 (2019)](#o93)</sup>.
- **Maternity.** 90 days' leave, with no dismissal during it <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>. With a year's service the employer pays half wages, in full before the leave starts; NSSF pays 70% for the 90 days if you have 9 months' contributions in the last 12 <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7), [N114](#n114)</sup>.

<a id="seniority-pay-and-severance"></a>

#### 3.9.7 Seniority pay and severance

- **Permanent contract.** A seniority payment of 7.5 days' wages in June and 7.5 in December, with the second wage payment of those months <sup>[N105 (2018)](#n105), [N104 (2019)](#n104)</sup>. Resign before the end of June or December and you lose that half-year's payment; nothing is due if you are dismissed for serious misconduct <sup>[N104 (2019)](#n104)</sup>.
- **Fixed-term contract.** No seniority payment, but at the end, severance of 5% of all wages paid over the contract <sup>[N105 (2018)](#n105)</sup>.

<a id="social-security-nssf"></a>

#### 3.9.8 Social security (NSSF)

NSSF covers every paid worker, Cambodian or foreign, of any employer with one or more workers <sup>[93 (2019)](#o93)</sup>. Besides the pension (see [Pension](#pension)), the work-injury contribution is 0.8% of your wage as NSSF assesses it, and the health-care contribution 2.6% <sup>[95 (2017)](#o95)</sup>. In 2022 about 400 Bangladeshis held Cambodian work permits with NSSF health and work-injury cover <sup>[N29 (2022)](#n29)</sup>.

1. <a id="social-security-nssf-item-1"></a>**Make sure the employer registers you** within 3 days of your starting work, using your passport details <sup>[94 (2021)](#o94)</sup>. The NSSF card is free <sup>[94 (2021)](#o94)</sup>.
   **Check it is real:** download the NSSF app and check each month that your employer is paying your contributions <sup>[N91 (2022)](#n91)</sup>.
2. <a id="social-security-nssf-item-2"></a>**Use the card for treatment.** Care is free at health facilities with an NSSF agreement, marked with the NSSF logo <sup>[93 (2019)](#o93)</sup>. If a facility asks you to pay, show the card and call 1286, which is also the number for an ambulance <sup>[93 (2019)](#o93)</sup>. Injured at work: see [If a worker dies](#if-a-worker-dies), is injured or loses the job <sup>[93 (2019)](#o93)</sup>.
3. <a id="social-security-nssf-item-3"></a>**Re-register a new passport.** Go to an NSSF office and register the new number with your fingerprints <sup>[N91 (2022)](#n91)</sup>.
4. <a id="social-security-nssf-item-4"></a>**Keep copies.** NSSF keeps the original documents you hand in <sup>[93 (2019)](#o93)</sup>.

**NSSF office:** Building 3, Russian Federation Boulevard, Tuek Laak I, Toul Kork, Phnom Penh (inside the MLVT compound); info@nssf.gov.kh; hotline 1286 <sup>[93 (2019)](#o93)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Are Bangladeshi construction and factory workers in Cambodia actually registered with NSSF in 2026?

<a id="pension"></a>

#### 3.9.9 Pension

- **Cambodia's NSSF pension.** Started on 1 October 2022, and covers foreign workers the same as Cambodians <sup>[N91 (2022)](#n91)</sup>. You and the employer each pay 2% of your contributory wage for the first five years; in the next five years the total rises to 8% <sup>[94 (2021)](#o94)</sup>. It pays from age 60 after at least 12 months of contributions, to people living in Cambodia; a member who doesn't qualify gets an old-age allowance, a lump sum of the contributions paid adjusted for consumer prices, claimed within a month of turning 60 <sup>[94 (2021)](#o94), [N91 (2022)](#n91), [N115 (2021)](#n115)</sup>. In 2022 there was no confirmed way for a foreign worker to take the money as a lump sum on leaving <sup>[N91 (2022)](#n91)</sup>.
- **Bangladesh's Probash pension,** part of the Universal Pension, for Bangladeshis aged 18 to 50 working abroad <sup>[96](#o96), [97](#o97)</sup>:
  1. **Register online in your own name** at upension.gov.bd; no third person is involved <sup>[96](#o96)</sup>.
  2. **Pay Tk 1,000, 2,000, 5,000, 7,500 or 10,000 a month** by debit or credit card into the scheme's Sonali Bank account, 3, 9 or 12 months at a time; 2.5% is added when you pay from abroad in foreign currency <sup>[97](#o97), [96](#o96)</sup>.

  What you get back: after 10 years of unbroken payments, a pension into your bank account from age 60, estimated at Tk 1,530 a month on Tk 1,000 contributions and Tk 15,302 on Tk 10,000; if you die, all you paid plus profit goes to your nominee <sup>[96](#o96), [97](#o97)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can a Bangladeshi leaving Cambodia for good claim back NSSF pension contributions in 2026, and at which NSSF counter?
> - How can a worker in Cambodia pay the Probash pension with only a Cambodian bank card?

<a id="your-passport"></a>

#### 3.9.10 Your passport

Brokers have taken Bangladeshi construction workers' passports in Cambodia <sup>[N102](#n102), [N46 (2023)](#n46)</sup>. In one case reported in 2023, brokers held the passports and paid the workers USD 50 (about Tk 6,200) a month for upkeep <sup>[N46 (2023)](#n46), [2](#o2)</sup>. ABA Bank asks for your original passport with a valid visa to open an account ([Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), [Step 7: After arrival](#step-7-after-arrival)) <sup>[N100](#n100)</sup>. Passports taken in scam compounds: see Scam compounds and trafficking <sup>[N80](#n80)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is it an offence in Cambodia for an employer or broker to keep a worker's passport, and where does a worker complain?

<a id="unions-and-help"></a>

#### 3.9.11 Unions and help

Workers have the right, without distinction and without prior permission, to form and join unions of their choice <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>. CENTRAL (the Center for Alliance of Labor and Human Rights) gives legal aid on labour and human rights: #487 St. 53BT, Sangkat Boeng Tumpun, Khan Meanchey, Phnom Penh; +855 93 556 603 <sup>[N116](#n116)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does CENTRAL, or any union or NGO in Phnom Penh, take cases from Bangladeshi workers, and in which language?

<a id="your-residence-permit"></a>

### 3.10 Your residence permit

Your right to stay is the EB (work or business) extension of your E-class (ordinary, or "business") visa, given by the GDI (General Department of Immigration, part of the Ministry of Interior); your right to work is the work permit and employment card from MLVT <sup>[N2 (2024)](#n2), [N6](#n6), [33](#o33), [N8](#n8), [11](#o11), [N3 (2024)](#n3), [N5](#n5)</sup>. An EB extension alone doesn't let you work <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>. Your address must also be on FPCS (the Foreigners Presence in Cambodia System, the GDI's register of where foreigners live) ([Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), [Step 7: After arrival](#step-7-after-arrival)) <sup>[N8](#n8)</sup>.

<a id="how-long-you-can-stay"></a>

#### 3.10.1 How long you can stay

- **First entry on the E-class visa:** 30 days <sup>[N2 (2024)](#n2)</sup>.
- **EB extension:** 1, 3, 6 or 12 months at a time; 6- and 12-month extensions allow you to leave and come back, 1- and 3-month ones are single entry <sup>[N2 (2024)](#n2), [11](#o11)</sup>.
- **Work permit:** one year, and it can be extended only within the validity of your stay <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7), [N4](#n4)</sup>.
- **Contract:** 2 years under BMET's permission letters <sup>[19](#o19), [24](#o24)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is there a limit on how many years a worker can keep renewing EB extensions and work permits?
> - Since visas went fully digital on 3 October 2026, what does a worker show as proof of an EB extension: a passport sticker, a paper, or a QR code?

<a id="renewing"></a>

#### 3.10.2 Renewing

1. <a id="renewing-item-1"></a>**Extend the EB before it runs out.** It is done inside Cambodia at the immigration office or through a visa agent (prices: Work visa: company worker, [Step 7: After arrival](#step-7-after-arrival)) <sup>[11](#o11), [N3 (2024)](#n3)</sup>. The second and every later 6- or 12-month EB needs proof of a valid work permit <sup>[N3 (2024)](#n3)</sup>. In 2026 extensions increasingly depend on supporting documents and accommodation records, and a worker missing from FPCS may be refused <sup>[N6](#n6), [N8](#n8), [N90](#n90)</sup>.
2. <a id="renewing-item-2"></a>**Renew the work permit every year, between 1 January and 31 March** <sup>[N4](#n4)</sup>. The employer files it on FWCMS (the Foreign Workers Centralized Management System, MLVT's online system) ([Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), [Step 3: The employer gets your work permit](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-work-permit)) <sup>[N2 (2024)](#n2), [N87 (2016)](#n87)</sup>. To renew you need an EB extension with at least 6 months left <sup>[N82](#n82)</sup>.
3. <a id="renewing-item-3"></a>**Keep your passport valid.** Without a valid passport you may not work <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>. The Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok, which covers Cambodia, issues a regular passport for 3,960 baht (Thai currency) in 40 to 45 days, or express for 7,920 baht in 21 to 28 days <sup>[N117](#n117), [77](#o77)</sup>. The Thai land border is closed, so a worker in Cambodia can't reach the Embassy overland (see [The Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok](#the-bangladesh-embassy-in-bangkok)) <sup>[13](#o13), [N73](#n73)</sup>. After a new passport, re-register it with NSSF (Social security (NSSF)) <sup>[N91 (2022)](#n91)</sup>.

**Check it is real:** under BMET's permission conditions the agency must send your work permit to BMET within 3 months of departure, and report your EB extension and regular salary to BMET and the Embassy's labour wing in Bangkok <sup>[21](#o21), [33](#o33)</sup>. The labour wing's email is fsbangkok@probashi.gov.bd <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>.

**If it fails:** without a valid extension you are an overstayer, and without a permit you work illegally ([What can get you jailed or deported](#what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported)) <sup>[N17](#n17), [N118](#n118)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can a worker in Cambodia apply for a new Bangladeshi passport at the Bangkok Embassy without travelling there, and how?
> - Does a new passport need a new work permit or EB extension, and who pays?
> - Can a worker check with the GDI or on FWCMS that his EB extension or permit renewal was actually filed?
> - Will the Embassy's labour wing tell a worker whether his agency reported his work permit and EB extension?

<a id="if-you-lose-your-job"></a>

#### 3.10.3 If you lose your job

**Your permit can go with the job.** MLVT can cancel your work permit once you have been out of work for more than a month, or are hired by another employer <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7), [54 (2023)](#o54)</sup>. The other grounds on which a labour inspector can withdraw it are under Working without a permit <sup>[54 (2023)](#o54)</sup>. Without a valid permit you can't get a second long EB extension ([Renewing](#renewing)) <sup>[N3 (2024)](#n3)</sup>.

**If you are dismissed:**

- The employer needs a valid reason linked to your ability, behaviour or the business's needs <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>. Serious misconduct by you, such as giving false documents when hired, lets the employer dismiss you without notice <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>.
- Without proper notice the employer owes your wages and benefits for the notice period ([Changing employer](#changing-employer)) <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>. During notice you get two paid days off a week to look for work <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>.
- If the employer ends a fixed-term contract early without serious misconduct or an act of God, it owes you at least the wages up to the contract's end date <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>.
- Closure or bankruptcy doesn't cancel what the employer owes <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - When a worker's job ends, how many days does he have to find a new employer or leave before he is out of status?
> - Is the EB extension cancelled when the work permit is, or does it run to its end date?

<a id="permanent-residence-and-citizenship"></a>

#### 3.10.4 Permanent residence and citizenship

- **Naturalisation:** the 1996 Nationality Law required seven years' continuous residence after receiving a residence card, and naturalisation is a favour, not a right, so it can be refused <sup>[98 (1996)](#o98)</sup>. An amended Nationality Law in force from 1 December 2025 tightens the residence needed, and offers citizenship to investors of at least USD 1 million (about Tk 12.5 crore) or donors of USD 3 million (about Tk 37.4 crore) to the national budget <sup>[N78](#n78), [2](#o2)</sup>.
- **Marriage to a Cambodian:** gives no automatic nationality ([Family](#family)) <sup>[99](#o99)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is there any permanent residence card for foreign workers, or is stay always renewed through EB extensions?
> - How many years of residence does ordinary naturalisation need since 1 December 2025?

<a id="what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported"></a>

### 3.11 What can get you jailed or deported

<a id="working-without-a-permit"></a>

#### 3.11.1 Working without a permit

- **You:** up to 3 months' prison and/or deportation under the Law on Immigration; the Minister of Interior may expel any foreigner working without a work card <sup>[N118](#n118), [N88](#n88), [100 (1994)](#o100)</sup>. Under Prakas 367/23 a labour inspector can withdraw your work permit if you entered Cambodia illegally, use an improper passport, have no proper residence visa, are physically unfit for the job or have a contagious disease, have had no work for more than a month or have gone to work for another employer, or gave false information about your background, job, workplace, home or documents <sup>[54 (2023)](#o54)</sup>.
- **The employer:** using foreign workers without MLVT's permission, the self-employed included, or without employment cards is fined KHR 12.6 million (about Tk 3.84 lakh) per worker, up to KHR 63 million (about Tk 19.2 lakh) where five or more lack permits (Joint Prakas 498) <sup>[54 (2023)](#o54), [73](#o73), [N118](#n118), [3](#o3)</sup>. A Joint Foreign Workforce Inspection Team (JFWIT) inspects companies' work permits and employment cards <sup>[N86](#n86)</sup>.

<a id="overstaying-your-visa"></a>

#### 3.11.2 Overstaying your visa

- **The fine:** USD 10 (about Tk 1,250) a day, settled at the airport when you leave; it can add up to thousands <sup>[N17](#n17), [N69](#n69), [2](#o2)</sup>. Breaking the registration and residence rules of the Law on Immigration is fined KHR 50,000 to 1,000,000 (about Tk 1,500 to 30,500), and up to KHR 2,000,000 (about Tk 61,000) for a repeat <sup>[55 (1994)](#o55), [3](#o3)</sup>.
- **Arrest:** Cambodia's new provisions allow foreigners who fail to leave within one month to be arrested, fined and jailed <sup>[N119](#n119), [N120](#n120)</sup>. Deportation through the normal procedure can take more than six months <sup>[N121](#n121)</sup>.
- **The waiver:** in 2026 Cambodia let overstayers who registered leave without the fine; for Bangladeshis that waiver was due to end on 31 October 2026 ([Going home without valid papers](#going-home-without-valid-papers)) <sup>[N122](#n122), [N123](#n123), [N124](#n124)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Was the waiver for Bangladeshis extended past 31 October 2026, and what happens now to those who missed it?
> - Which law sets the 2026 arrest-and-prison rule for overstayers, and is the USD 10-a-day fine capped?

<a id="false-papers-and-illegal-entry"></a>

#### 3.11.3 False papers and illegal entry

- **Entering secretly or by fraud:** 3 to 6 months' prison, then expulsion; anyone who helps gets the same <sup>[100 (1994)](#o100), [55 (1994)](#o55)</sup>.
- **Faking, altering, lending or using in another name** a resident card, work card or employment contract, or applying with false papers: 5 to 15 years' prison <sup>[100 (1994)](#o100)</sup>.
- **Holding or using a Khmer identity card or Cambodian passport:** 5 to 10 years' prison <sup>[98 (1996)](#o98)</sup>.

<a id="online-scams"></a>

#### 3.11.4 Online scams

Cambodia's Law on Combating Online Scams was promulgated by Royal Decree on 6 April 2026 <sup>[N125](#n125)</sup>:

- carrying out an online scam: 2 to 5 years' prison and KHR 200 to 500 million (about Tk 61 lakh to 1.53 crore); 5 to 10 years and KHR 500 million to 1 billion (about Tk 1.53 to 3.05 crore) when done by a group or against many victims <sup>[N125](#n125), [3](#o3)</sup>;
- recruiting or training people for scam work: 2 to 5 years <sup>[N125](#n125)</sup>;
- collecting or using other people's identity cards or passports for scams: 1 to 3 years and KHR 100 to 300 million (about Tk 30.5 to 91.5 lakh) <sup>[N125](#n125), [3](#o3)</sup>;
- running a scam centre with violence, detention or exploitation: 10 to 20 years; 15 years to life if someone dies <sup>[N125](#n125)</sup>.

Cambodian officers interview Bangladeshis applying to go home to screen for trafficking victims, and those found to be victims have been let go home (see [Scam compounds and trafficking](#scam-compounds-and-trafficking)) <sup>[N117](#n117), [N126](#n126)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does the 2026 anti-scam law spare trafficking victims forced to scam from prosecution, and has any Bangladeshi been charged under it?

<a id="drugs"></a>

#### 3.11.5 Drugs

- Keeping, carrying or trafficking drugs such as heroin, methamphetamine or cannabis: 2 to 5 years' prison and KHR 4 to 10 million (about Tk 1.22 to 3.05 lakh); larger amounts, for example 15 to 80 grams of methamphetamine, 5 to 10 years <sup>[101 (2012)](#o101), [3](#o3)</sup>.
- Using drugs: 1 to 6 months' prison and KHR 100,000 to 1,000,000 (about Tk 3,050 to 30,500) <sup>[101 (2012)](#o101), [3](#o3)</sup>.

<a id="where-you-live"></a>

#### 3.11.6 Where you live

- Whoever houses a foreigner must report it to the authorities within 24 hours, and you must report a change of address to the local police within 24 hours <sup>[100 (1994)](#o100), [N127](#n127)</sup>.
- Since January 2026, guesthouses, apartment blocks, dormitories, factories and similar places must see papers proving your right to stay before letting you stay, and are fined KHR 4 million (about Tk 1.22 lakh) for each foreigner staying illegally <sup>[N128](#n128), [3](#o3)</sup>.

<a id="detention-and-deportation"></a>

#### 3.11.7 Detention and deportation

1. <a id="detention-and-deportation-item-1"></a>**The order.** The Minister of Interior orders expulsion; it takes effect at once and is carried out within 7 days unless a court suspends it, and it can be added to any penalty under the Law on Immigration <sup>[100 (1994)](#o100)</sup>.
2. <a id="detention-and-deportation-item-2"></a>**Appeal** to a court within two months of the order <sup>[100 (1994)](#o100)</sup>.
3. <a id="detention-and-deportation-item-3"></a>**Detention.** Cambodia runs immigration detention centres in Phnom Penh and Sihanoukville, and in 2026 moved foreigners suspected of scam work, Bangladeshis among them, to the Bati Pre-Deportation Centre in Takeo province <sup>[N129 (2023)](#n129), [N130](#n130)</sup>. How long you stay there depends on your embassy issuing travel papers if you have no passport <sup>[N130](#n130)</sup>. Detainees have reported officers demanding bribes for phones, beds, food deliveries and release (2023) <sup>[N129 (2023)](#n129)</sup>.
4. <a id="detention-and-deportation-item-4"></a>**The ban.** Deportees for illegal entry and work are blacklisted for at least three to five years, and those who refused the 2026 voluntary-departure window and were detained are barred for life once deported <sup>[N64](#n64), [N7](#n7)</sup>.
5. <a id="detention-and-deportation-item-5"></a>**Who helps.** Bangladesh has no embassy in Cambodia; the Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok handles it ([The Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok](#the-bangladesh-embassy-in-bangkok)) <sup>[N117](#n117)</sup>. In Bangladesh the family can apply to the Wage Earners' Welfare Board (WEWB) to bring home a worker jailed abroad, and for legal aid abroad, through the DC office's expatriate welfare branch, the DEMO (District Employment and Manpower Office) or the Board directly <sup>[102](#o102), [103](#o103)</sup>.

**In practice:** on 3 September 2026 immigration police detained 32 Bangladeshis at Phnom Penh airport as they tried to fly home under the fine waiver; held without phones, 30 reached Dhaka on 20 October 2026 after more than six weeks, on tickets they paid for themselves <sup>[N117](#n117), [N74](#n74), [N122](#n122), [N123](#n123)</sup>. In December 2025 Bangladeshi construction workers fleeing the Thai border fighting were detained though most said they had valid visas and work permits, and were asked for USD 800 (about Tk 1 lakh) each to be sent home <sup>[N41](#n41), [2](#o2)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can relatives or the Embassy visit Bangladeshis held at the Bati centre or the Phnom Penh detention centre, and how?

<a id="if-you-arent-paid"></a>

### 3.12 If you aren't paid

**Deadlines:** a wage claim lapses three years after the wage was due; once conciliation fails you have two months to go to court <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>.

**Your position:** refusing to pay all or part of your wages, or paying late again and again, is a serious offence by the employer; you can then leave without notice and still claim indemnity and damages <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>. The employer must prove every payment with your signature in its payroll book, and signing that all wages are paid, or taking pay without protest, does not give up your right to unpaid wages <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>.

**Act in this order:**

1. <a id="if-you-arent-paid-item-1"></a>**Keep proof:** your contract and the attested Bangla translation of it that every worker must be given <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-you-arent-paid-item-2"></a>**Complain to MLVT.** File at MLVT's Labour Dispute Department in Phnom Penh or at the Department of Labour of your province, under Prakas 073/25 <sup>[N103](#n103)</sup>. MLVT's compound is on Russian Federation Boulevard, Toul Kork, Phnom Penh, where the NSSF office also is <sup>[93 (2019)](#o93)</sup>. Call MLVT first on 1297 or 023 884 375; its website, mlvt.gov.kh, also has an anonymous complaint box <sup>[104](#o104)</sup>.
   - The labour inspector must call you and the employer to a joint conciliation meeting within three weeks <sup>[N103](#n103)</sup>.
   - An agreement signed before the inspector is legally binding, and you may bring someone to help or represent you <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>. Conciliation and arbitration under the Labour Law are free <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>.
3. <a id="if-you-arent-paid-item-3"></a>**Ask for a second conciliation, or the Arbitration Council.** If conciliation fails, you and the employer can jointly ask MLVT for one more within two working days <sup>[N103](#n103)</sup>. During conciliation either side can ask for the case to go to the Arbitration Council, an independent body set up under the Labour Law <sup>[N103](#n103), [105](#o105)</sup>.
4. <a id="if-you-arent-paid-item-4"></a>**Go to court** within two months of failed conciliation <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>.
5. <a id="if-you-arent-paid-item-5"></a>**Get legal aid** from CENTRAL ([Unions and help](#unions-and-help)) <sup>[N116](#n116)</sup>.
6. <a id="if-you-arent-paid-item-6"></a>**Tell the Bangladesh side.**
   1. The Embassy's labour wing in Bangkok: fsbangkok@probashi.gov.bd, welfare hotline +66-818-70-84-43 ([The Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok](#the-bangladesh-embassy-in-bangkok)) <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>. The agency must report your regular salary to it and to BMET <sup>[33](#o33)</sup>.
   2. BMET, against the agency: a written complaint with evidence to BMET's Director General, free, answered in 30 working days; BMET's complaint form lists non-payment of salary and non-existence of the company, and you get a receipt with a complaint ID <sup>[5](#o5), [69 (2024)](#o69)</sup>.
   3. The Probashi call centre: 16135 (free in Bangladesh) or +8809610102030 from abroad <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>.
   4. Your family can ask WEWB for legal aid abroad ([Detention and deportation](#detention-and-deportation)) <sup>[103](#o103)</sup>.

In one 2022 case, a Bangladeshi sent on a business visa for a USD 1,000 (about Tk 1.25 lakh) data-entry job was told he would get no salary unless he found customers for the scam (see [Scam compounds and trafficking](#scam-compounds-and-trafficking)) <sup>[N131 (2022)](#n131), [2](#o2)</sup>.

**In practice:** six Bangladeshis who said they had worked about five months as labourers without pay crawled under the Thai border fence from Poipet in May 2026, trying to reach the Embassy in Bangkok, and were handed to Thai police for prosecution <sup>[N61](#n61)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Will MLVT's Labour Dispute Department take a complaint from a Bangladeshi without a Khmer interpreter?
> - Has any Bangladeshi recovered unpaid wages through MLVT, the Arbitration Council or the Embassy in Bangkok?
> - What are the Arbitration Council's current address and phone?
> - Where is the Preah Sihanouk (Sihanoukville) provincial Department of Labour, and its phone?

<a id="changing-employer-or-leaving"></a>

### 3.13 Changing employer or leaving

<a id="changing-employer"></a>

#### 3.13.1 Changing employer

**The permit doesn't move with you.** MLVT can cancel your work permit when another employer hires you, and a new employer needs an approved foreign-worker quota and a new permit for you ([Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), [Step 3: The employer gets your work permit](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-work-permit)) <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7), [54 (2023)](#o54), [N5](#n5)</sup>. A recruiting agency owner said in July 2026 that some workers leave for better-paid jobs and their first employers then refuse to take them back <sup>[N132](#n132)</sup>. Each employer must write your start or departure, with the job, wage and dates, in your employment card within 15 working days <sup>[54 (2023)](#o54)</sup>.

**Leaving a permanent contract:** give notice of 7 days with under 6 months' service, 15 days for 6 months to 2 years, 1 month for 2 to 5 years, 2 months for 5 to 10 years, 3 months above 10 <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>. Resigning before the end of June or December loses that half-year's seniority payment <sup>[N104 (2019)](#n104)</sup>.

**Leaving a fixed-term contract early:** only by written agreement of both sides in front of a labour inspector, or for serious misconduct or an act of God <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>. Walk out without good reason and the employer can claim damages <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>.

**If the employer cheats or abuses you:** fraud to get you to sign, unpaid or repeatedly late wages, abuse, threats or violence let you leave without notice and still claim indemnity and damages <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>.

**Social security:** you stay covered for work injury only if the new employer registers you with NSSF <sup>[93 (2019)](#o93)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Besides the first employer's entry in the employment card, does a Bangladeshi worker need a release letter to get a new work permit, and do employers charge for it?

<a id="going-home-on-leave"></a>

#### 3.13.2 Going home on leave

You earn 18 days' paid leave a year and can take it after one year <sup>[N111](#n111), [7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>. A 6- or 12-month EB extension allows multiple entries; 1- and 3-month extensions are single entry <sup>[N2 (2024)](#n2), [11](#o11)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Must a worker returning to Cambodia from leave get a new BMET clearance or re-entry card in Dhaka?

<a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job"></a>

#### 3.13.3 Leaving at the end of the job

1. <a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-1"></a>**Watch for the notice.** On a fixed-term contract over 6 months the employer must tell you 10 days ahead that it won't renew (15 days if over a year), or the contract is extended <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>.
2. <a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-2"></a>**Collect what you are owed within 48 hours** of the end <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>:
   - unpaid wages and overtime <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>;
   - pay for leave not taken <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>;
   - on a fixed-term contract, severance of 5% of all wages paid <sup>[N105 (2018)](#n105)</sup>;
   - on a permanent contract, any seniority payment due <sup>[N105 (2018)](#n105)</sup>.
3. <a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-3"></a>**Ask for your employment certificate** <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>.
4. <a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-4"></a>**Settle your bank account.** An ACLEDA account with no activity for over 12 months becomes dormant and is charged fees; ABA charges USD 50 (about Tk 6,200) after 4 years without a transaction, and closing an ABA current account is free <sup>[N98](#n98), [N100](#n100), [2](#o2)</sup>.
5. <a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-5"></a>**Leave before your EB extension ends;** each day over costs USD 10 (about Tk 1,250) ([What can get you jailed or deported](#what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported)) <sup>[N17](#n17), [2](#o2)</sup>.
6. <a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-6"></a>**Cash and goods.** What you may take out of Cambodia and bring into Bangladesh: Coming home <sup>[106 (1997)](#o106)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Who pays the flight home at the end of a Cambodian contract, and does the BMET-attested contract say?

<a id="bringing-your-family"></a>

### 3.14 Bringing your family

Your husband, wife or child enters on the E-class visa, since only it can be extended as EB, and then gets an EB dependant extension with proof of the relationship and a letter from you <sup>[N3 (2024)](#n3), [N6](#n6), [11](#o11)</sup>. They can't work without their own work permit <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>. They apply for the extension inside Cambodia, at the immigration office or through a visa agent; the papers and steps are in Family <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>.

<a id="scam-compounds-and-trafficking"></a>

## 4 Scam compounds and trafficking

A scam compound is a guarded building or estate, mostly run by Chinese gangs, where trafficked foreigners are forced to cheat people online through fake romances, fake investments and cryptocurrency schemes, many of them aimed at people in the United States and other rich countries <sup>[N52](#n52), [N15](#n15), [107](#o107), [N133](#n133), [N13](#n13)</sup>. Many compounds were casinos and hotels until Cambodia banned online gambling in 2019; they have surveillance cameras, barbed wire and many guards, often with electric-shock batons and sometimes guns <sup>[N52](#n52)</sup>. For Bangladeshis the main way in is the legal route itself: many of those sold into compounds left with BMET (Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training) emigration clearance and a BMET smart card (BMET's emigration clearance card), believing they were following government rules <sup>[N13](#n13), [N23](#n23), [N26](#n26), [N80](#n80)</sup>. BRAC (the Bangladeshi development organisation, which runs a migration programme) counts more than 1,000 of the 15,921 workers BMET cleared for Cambodia in 18 months as still trapped in compounds <sup>[N134](#n134)</sup>. Bangladeshis are held there alongside Pakistanis, South Indians, Nepalis and Bhutanese <sup>[N13](#n13)</sup>.

<a id="how-people-are-recruited"></a>

### 4.1 How people are recruited

The same chain recurs in the cases reported: a promised computer, data-entry, factory or customer-service job <sup>[N13](#n13)</sup>, a payment of several lakh, papers that don't match the job, a Bangladeshi receiver on arrival, and a sale <sup>[N13](#n13), [N15](#n15), [N80](#n80)</sup>.

1. <a id="how-people-are-recruited-item-1"></a>**The offer.** The job is computer operator, data entry, typist, call-centre or customer-service work, sometimes factory, restaurant or casino work, at Tk 50,000 to over Tk 1 lakh a month; one man was promised USD 800 (about Tk 1 lakh) <sup>[N80](#n80), [N13](#n13), [N15](#n15), [N135](#n135), [N136](#n136), [N49](#n49), [2](#o2)</sup>. A Sirajganj man was told by a friend it could be arranged faster than a job in Saudi Arabia <sup>[N37](#n37)</sup>. The ads run on fake websites, email, Facebook, Instagram, WhatsApp and Telegram, and some Telegram groups hold fake "successful migrants" who belong to the ring <sup>[N15](#n15), [N52](#n52), [N135](#n135)</sup>.
2. <a id="how-people-are-recruited-item-2"></a>**The person you know.** The UN Human Rights Office found nearly three in four victims were recruited by relatives, friends, neighbours or community members <sup>[N49](#n49)</sup>. A BRAC study found 66% of cyber-scam victims were recruited through middlemen <sup>[N12](#n12)</sup>. Compounds make people inside recruit others, often people they know, to win their own release, and one Bangladeshi was made to film a recruitment video later used to lure other Bangladeshis <sup>[N102](#n102), [N51](#n51)</sup>.
3. <a id="how-people-are-recruited-item-3"></a>**The test.** Some rings first give a computer literacy test, then arrange a Cambodian tourist e-visa <sup>[N40 (2022)](#n40)</sup>; one man had his typing and spoken English tested at the compound itself <sup>[N13](#n13)</sup>. UNODC (the UN Office on Drugs and Crime) says the networks pick people with computer, digital or language skills, and most people found at scam sites are men aged 18 to 40 <sup>[107](#o107)</sup>.
4. <a id="how-people-are-recruited-item-4"></a>**The payment.** Families paid Tk 2 lakh to Tk 7.7 lakh; in 2026 cases reported, victims paid Tk 4.5 lakh, Tk 5.3 lakh, Tk 5.5 lakh and Tk 6 lakh <sup>[N80](#n80), [N135](#n135), [N23](#n23), [N133](#n133), [N14](#n14)</sup>. A Sirajganj man told The Daily Star (July 2026) that a broker asked Tk 7 lakh for a computer-operator job at Tk 80,000 a month, and that he paid Tk 1 lakh to the broker and the rest to an agency he named as Pushpita Overseas in Fakirapool <sup>[N37](#n37)</sup>. A Chapainawabganj returnee told TBS (The Business Standard, a Dhaka newspaper; July 2026) he paid Tk 4.8 lakh through a local middleman and went through Fahim Recruiting Agency <sup>[N43](#n43)</sup>. BMET gave Puspita Overseas (RL-2648) of Fakirapool and Fahim Recruiting Agency (RL-2594) permission letters in 2025 to send construction workers to Cambodia (see [How a job reaches you](#how-a-job-reaches-you-and-who-takes-a-cut), and who takes a cut) <sup>[47](#o47), [48](#o48)</sup>.
5. <a id="how-people-are-recruited-item-5"></a>**The papers.** The visa and papers don't match the promised job: invitation letters named a construction company, the broker called the construction title "temporary", or the worker found on arrival he held a tourist visa or a one-month visit visa <sup>[N13](#n13), [N37](#n37), [N23](#n23)</sup>. The State Minister for Expatriates' Welfare says many victims travel on one- or two-month tourist visas while brokers tell them they hold two-year work visas <sup>[N49](#n49)</sup>. In two cases reported, the promised visa and work permit never came <sup>[N80](#n80), [N134](#n134)</sup>. Since checks tightened, some traffickers use fake BMET cards, or send people to Thailand as tourists first and on to Cambodia from there <sup>[N13](#n13)</sup>.
6. <a id="how-people-are-recruited-item-6"></a>**The trip.** Brokers routed people through Thailand or, when there was no direct flight, through China <sup>[N49](#n49), [N13](#n13)</sup>. At Dhaka airport one man, told at check-in he needed a Thai transit visa, was told by the broker to stand by a pillar and send a WhatsApp photo; a man came and got him a boarding pass within 10 minutes, and another of the broker's men handed him USD 2,000 (about Tk 2.5 lakh) before boarding <sup>[N13](#n13), [2](#o2)</sup>. In a 2023 case a victim's fingerprint didn't match at the airport Probashi Kallyan desk (the airport desk that checks departing workers' BMET smart cards), and an official called his agency and let him board <sup>[N46 (2023)](#n46), [5](#o5)</sup>.
7. <a id="how-people-are-recruited-item-7"></a>**The handover.** A Bangladeshi receiver meets the worker at the airport, keeps him in a hotel for two or three days, then takes him "for an interview" to a compound, three and a half hours' drive away in one case and 120 km away near the border in another <sup>[N13](#n13), [N23](#n23)</sup>. There he is told he has been bought, for USD 1,500 to 5,000 (about Tk 1.9 lakh to Tk 6.2 lakh) <sup>[N13](#n13), [N14](#n14), [2](#o2)</sup>. Bangladeshi officials say brokers collect USD 2,000 to 5,000 a person <sup>[N51](#n51)</sup>.

Other ways in:

- **"Cambodia first, then a richer country."** Some victims were told they were going to Thailand, Canada, Australia or the UAE and were taken to Cambodia, or were told to "gain some experience" in the compound before being sent on <sup>[N65](#n65), [N137](#n137), [N13](#n13)</sup>.
- **From the Gulf.** Some traffickers work out of Dubai, recruiting Bangladeshis already working in West Asia with promises of higher pay and taking them across the Thai border into Cambodia, Laos or Myanmar <sup>[N51](#n51), [N65](#n65)</sup>.
- **Construction sites.** Brokers also recruit Bangladeshis to Cambodian building sites under false promises, where some end up in debt with their passports taken <sup>[N102](#n102)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which licensed agencies (licence numbers) processed the BMET clearances of workers later found in compounds, and has BMET suspended any of them for it?
> - What does a broker in Dhaka charge for a "Cambodia computer job" in November 2026, after the end of the attestation exemption?

<a id="where-the-compounds-are"></a>

### 4.2 Where the compounds are

Amnesty International counted at least 53 compounds in 16 towns and cities in June 2025, and 86 by April 2026 <sup>[N52](#n52), [N68](#n68)</sup>. They cluster in Sihanoukville, along the Thai and Lao borders, in border towns such as Bavet and, more and more, in rural areas <sup>[N102](#n102), [N138](#n138)</sup>. One Bangladeshi was taken from Phnom Penh to the Long Bay compound in Dara Sakor, a special economic zone 350 km away <sup>[N51](#n51)</sup>. After more than 250 raids from July 2025 to March 2026, Cambodia's Commission for Combating Online Scams (CCOS, the government body leading the crackdown) says scam groups hide in smaller groups in gated housing estates (called borey in Khmer), apartments and condominiums, and that many suspected sites are mixed-use buildings with condominiums, hotels and legitimate businesses <sup>[108](#o108), [N67](#n67), [N68](#n68)</sup>. Managers sometimes knew of raids in advance and moved workers, in one case by vehicle into the mountains <sup>[N139](#n139), [N68](#n68)</sup>. The US State Department reported in 2025 that NGOs estimate about 150,000 people are held in forced labour in about 350 compounds in Cambodia <sup>[N102](#n102)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - In which towns or compounds are Bangladeshis held in November 2026, according to the Bangkok embassy or BRAC caseworkers?

<a id="what-happens-inside"></a>

### 4.3 What happens inside

- **Passport, phone and cash taken** on entry; one man was allowed his phone for 10 minutes <sup>[N80](#n80), [N13](#n13)</sup>. One compound had a spa and a supermarket inside, so there was no chance to go outside <sup>[N13](#n13)</sup>.
- **Hours:** the promised eight-hour day becomes 12 to 16 hours, sometimes at night (one man worked 10 pm to 10 am) <sup>[N13](#n13), [N65](#n65), [N80](#n80)</sup>.
- **The work:** posing online as a young woman or a romantic partner, building trust, then getting the target to "invest"; teams split the stages, one collecting phone numbers, another building the relationship <sup>[N13](#n13), [N49](#n49)</sup>.
- **Pay:** one man got USD 476 (about Tk 59,300) for the first month and nothing after; another was fed only dry noodles, sometimes once a day <sup>[N13](#n13), [2](#o2)</sup>. In a 2022 case, a man sent for a USD 1,000 data-entry job was told he would get no salary unless he found scam customers <sup>[N131 (2022)](#n131)</sup>.
- **Punishment:** people who miss targets, refuse or try to contact the police are taken to "dark rooms", given electric shocks, beaten with hockey sticks and denied food; one man was locked up for 17 days <sup>[N13](#n13), [N52](#n52), [N49](#n49)</sup>.
- **Resale:** people who miss quotas or can't repay the "debt" are sold on to other compounds, sometimes at a higher price; one Bangladeshi was sold to three compounds in 2022 for USD 3,500 to 10,000 (about Tk 4.4 lakh to Tk 12.5 lakh) <sup>[N102](#n102), [N13](#n13), [N51](#n51), [2](#o2)</sup>. Another, whose broker refused to renew his expired visa, was held in a building waiting to be sold <sup>[N135](#n135)</sup>.
- **The price of leaving:** the purchase price becomes a debt to work off, and people who ask to leave are told to repay it, sometimes double, and to promise not to go to the police <sup>[N52](#n52), [N13](#n13), [N40 (2022)](#n40)</sup>. Families are phoned directly, sometimes by the victim made to call, and asked for up to USD 3,000 (about Tk 3.7 lakh) under threat of resale <sup>[N135](#n135), [N48](#n48), [2](#o2)</sup>.
- **No legal status:** without the promised visa and work permit the worker is left with no legal status, and overstaying costs USD 10 (about Tk 1,250) a day <sup>[N80](#n80), [N134](#n134), [N13](#n13), [2](#o2)</sup>.

<a id="if-you-or-a-relative-is-trapped"></a>

### 4.4 If you or a relative is trapped

Bangladesh has no embassy in Cambodia; the Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok covers it <sup>[N135](#n135), [N140](#n140)</sup>. All land crossings between Cambodia and Thailand are closed, so a worker in Cambodia can't reach the embassy overland <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>. Rescue was more likely when a foreign embassy was involved than through the Cambodian hotline alone <sup>[N102](#n102)</sup>.

You'll need the compound's exact location and a photo of him; the police hotline asks for both before it acts <sup>[N102](#n102)</sup>.

Ask for help in this order:

1. <a id="if-you-or-a-relative-is-trapped-item-1"></a>**Get the location out.** One victim shared his live location with his wife before he was locked up, and his brother then reached Bangladeshis in Cambodia who traced him <sup>[N49](#n49)</sup>. Several survivors were beaten after bosses found they had secretly contacted the police <sup>[N52](#n52)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-you-or-a-relative-is-trapped-item-2"></a>**Contact the embassy's labour and welfare wing.**
   - **Welfare hotline** for Bangladeshis in Thailand and Cambodia: +66-818-70-84-43 <sup>[109](#o109)</sup>.
   - **Labour and welfare email:** fsbangkok@probashi.gov.bd; the First Secretary (Labour) in 2026 is Jahir Emam <sup>[109](#o109), [110](#o110)</sup>.
   - **Main line:** +66 2 390 5107 to 8; mission.bangkok@mofa.gov.bd and consular.bangkok@mofa.gov.bd <sup>[109](#o109)</sup>.
   Ready wording: "My [son/husband] [name], passport [number], went to Cambodia on [date] through [broker or agency]. He is held at [compound name / live-location link] in [town]. His phone: [number]. Photo attached. Please help him." <sup>[N102](#n102), [109](#o109)</sup>
   **What it has done:** embassy officials confirmed one escaped woman's location by video call and, with help from Bangladeshis living in Cambodia, rescued her from Phnom Penh, then began arranging her return on a travel pass with the Cambodian government's help <sup>[N30](#n30)</sup>. In July 2025, during the Thailand-Cambodia fighting, its Counsellor (Labour) was in Phnom Penh arranging food, shelter, transport and medical care for Bangladeshis who had left the border areas <sup>[42](#o42)</sup>.
3. <a id="if-you-or-a-relative-is-trapped-item-3"></a>**From Bangladesh, apply to the Welfare Board.** The family applies to the Wage Earners' Welfare Board (WEWB, the government board for migrant workers' welfare), directly, through the expatriate welfare branch of the district DC (Deputy Commissioner's) office or through the DEMO (District Employment and Manpower Office); the Board writes to the embassy and tells the family <sup>[103](#o103), [58](#o58)</sup>. The Board's services include getting a worker jailed abroad brought home, and legal aid abroad <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>. Probashi call centre: 16135 (toll-free in Bangladesh), +8809610102030 from abroad <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>. After a survivors' group, the Anirban Survivor Voice Foundation, applied, the Board wrote to the embassy's labour wing to free 30 Bangladeshis held in Cambodian detention, and they were brought home in October 2026 <sup>[N9](#n9), [N141](#n141), [N123](#n123)</sup>. The foundation's president is Al Amin Nayan <sup>[N142](#n142)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What phone number or Facebook page does Anirban Survivor Voice Foundation give families of Bangladeshis held in Cambodia?
4. <a id="if-you-or-a-relative-is-trapped-item-4"></a>**Contact BRAC's Migration Programme in Dhaka.** Two young men trafficked to a Cambodian scam centre contacted BRAC's Migration Centre online, and it helped arrange their return <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>.
5. <a id="if-you-or-a-relative-is-trapped-item-5"></a>**Call the Cambodian police.** The police's Anti-Human Trafficking and Juvenile Protection Office hotline is 1288; the anti-trafficking hotline takes Chinese, English, Khmer and Vietnamese <sup>[111 (2022)](#o111), [N102](#n102)</sup>. Since August 2024 the police pass scam-related cases to a separate hotline run by the Central Security Department <sup>[N102](#n102)</sup>. The National Police also run a 24-hour online scam-reporting site, hotline.police.gov.kh, with a complaint form (ដាក់ពាក្យបណ្ដឹង, "file a complaint") <sup>[112](#o112)</sup>. In many police rescues officers met a manager at the gate and were handed only the person who had called <sup>[N52](#n52)</sup>.
6. <a id="if-you-or-a-relative-is-trapped-item-6"></a>**Ask about IOM.** IOM (the International Organization for Migration, the UN migration agency) helped more than 3,500 victims of forced criminality in Southeast Asia from 2022 to 2025, Bangladeshis among the largest groups, but only people given victim status can get its help, including the ticket home <sup>[N143](#n143), [N144](#n144)</sup>.

**If someone demands money for release:** compounds have demanded USD 1,500 to 5,000 (about Tk 1.9 lakh to Tk 6.2 lakh); one family paid about USD 2,900 (about Tk 3.6 lakh) and spent nearly Tk 9 lakh in all <sup>[N13](#n13), [N14](#n14), [N49](#n49), [2](#o2)</sup>. After those two young men were released, the traffickers took control of them again and took more money from their families for return tickets <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>. Bangladeshis involved in the trafficking intimidate victims trying to go home, and one returnee said brokers' interference delayed his return by two months <sup>[N137](#n137), [N145](#n145), [N122](#n122)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is 1288 still the anti-trafficking hotline, what is the Central Security Department's scam-victim number, and does either answer in English?
> - Does the embassy's +66-818-70-84-43 hotline take WhatsApp messages, and in which hours is it staffed?
> - Which phone number or WhatsApp should a family use to reach BRAC's Migration Programme about a relative held in Cambodia?

<a id="after-you-get-out"></a>

### 4.5 After you get out

- **Victim or offender:** Cambodian authorities rarely give victim status; people freed from compounds are often detained and deported without screening <sup>[N138](#n138), [N102](#n102), [N33](#n33)</sup>. None of the 73 survivors Amnesty interviewed for its 2026 report, Bangladeshis among them, had been recognised as a trafficking victim <sup>[N139](#n139)</sup>. Some were held indefinitely, asked for bribes or ransom by police, or resold to compounds <sup>[N102](#n102), [N139](#n139)</sup>.
- **Screening for the return list:** Cambodian officers interview the Bangladeshis on the embassy's return list, question those who entered irregularly in more detail, and have let those found to be trafficking victims go home <sup>[N48](#n48), [N126](#n126)</sup>. In October 2026 the screening was paused, and the Bangladesh mission asked for it to restart <sup>[N126](#n126)</sup>.
- **Detention:** people are often held about three months, in overcrowded centres, without phones <sup>[N138](#n138), [N139](#n139), [N48](#n48)</sup>. On 3 September 2026, 32 Bangladeshis with tickets home were turned back at Phnom Penh airport and taken to an immigration detention centre; 30 reached Dhaka on 20 October 2026 <sup>[N48](#n48), [N123](#n123)</sup>. A Bangladeshi held in a detention centre reported being attacked by other inmates <sup>[N138](#n138)</sup>.
- **Shelter:** Cambodia has one shelter for scam-compound victims, with room for 100; in June 2026 it was full, with a waiting list of hundreds <sup>[N138](#n138), [N17](#n17)</sup>. Many people leaving compounds are stranded in Phnom Penh with no passport and no money for a flight <sup>[N146](#n146)</sup>.
- **Leaving:** the fine waiver, the embassy's return list, the Travel Permit for people without a passport and the ticket are covered in Going home without valid papers <sup>[N48](#n48), [113 (2023)](#o113)</sup>.

<a id="coming-home-and-taking-action"></a>

### 4.6 Coming home and taking action

1. <a id="coming-home-and-taking-action-item-1"></a>**At Dhaka airport,** returnees from Cambodia have got emergency help, counselling and money to reach home from Civil Aviation Security, the Expatriate Welfare Desk and BRAC's Migration Programme <sup>[N23](#n23)</sup>. The CID (Criminal Investigation Department of the Bangladesh Police) has run forensic checks on returnees and their phones, and seized one returnee's phone <sup>[N123](#n123), [N48](#n48)</sup>.
2. <a id="coming-home-and-taking-action-item-2"></a>**Keep the evidence.** Most victims who seek legal help can't produce documents, payment records or receipts to file a case, while one survivor kept the bank transaction records linked to his broker <sup>[N135](#n135), [N49](#n49)</sup>.
3. <a id="coming-home-and-taking-action-item-3"></a>**File a criminal case** at a police station against the broker and agency; several returnees from Cambodia have <sup>[N133](#n133)</sup>. Bangladesh's Prevention and Suppression of Trafficking in Persons and Smuggling of Migrants Act, 2026 was adopted on 9 April 2026 <sup>[N51](#n51)</sup>. On 26 October 2026 a case filed at Paltan Police Station, Dhaka, under sections 6, 7, 8, 9 and 22 of the Act led the CID to arrest two men accused of trafficking two young men to Cambodian scam centres <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>. Under section 54 of the Act, a trafficking victim is not criminally liable for illegal entry or stay, or a false travel or identity document, where it resulted directly from the trafficking <sup>[N51](#n51)</sup>.
4. <a id="coming-home-and-taking-action-item-4"></a>**Complain against the agency at BMET.** Send a written application with your evidence to BMET's Director General; it is free and is to be settled in 30 working days <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>. BMET's complaint form lists "job not provided", "non-payment of salary", "stranded at airport" and "non-existence of company", and BMET gives an acknowledgement receipt with a complaint ID <sup>[69 (2024)](#o69)</sup>. BMET's permission letters for Cambodia require the agency to pay the worker's food and lodging, return airfare and compensation if anything goes wrong, under a Tk 300 non-judicial stamp undertaking <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.
5. <a id="coming-home-and-taking-action-item-5"></a>**Get legal advice.** Returnees have asked BRAC's Migration Service Centres for help, including legal support <sup>[N49](#n49)</sup>.

In practice, the CID's trafficking unit says cases are few compared with the number of returnees, and many returnees settle with the broker to get money back instead <sup>[N13](#n13)</sup>. Two survivors who didn't file cases said they couldn't afford to <sup>[N49](#n49)</sup>. A survivors' group warns that deported Bangladeshis may be lured back into Cambodia illegally through another country <sup>[N76](#n76)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Has any Bangladeshi trafficked into a Cambodian compound got money back from an agency through a BMET complaint, or compensation through the courts?

<a id="the-crackdown"></a>

### 4.7 The crackdown

- **Cambodia's numbers:** CCOS says Cambodia raided 972 scam operations and repatriated 23,760 foreigners between July 2025 and October 2026 <sup>[N147](#n147)</sup>. From January to September 2026 Cambodia's General Department of Immigration (GDI) deported 52,039 foreigners, including more than 3,000 Bangladeshis, and identified more than 18,000 of the deportees as directly involved in online fraud; no figure shows how many of the Bangladeshis were trafficking victims <sup>[N148](#n148), [N76](#n76)</sup>.
- **"No compounds left":** Prime Minister Hun Manet vowed to clear Cambodia of scam operations by April 2026 <sup>[108](#o108)</sup>. On 28 August 2026 CCOS said Cambodia no longer hosts any scam compounds <sup>[N67](#n67)</sup>. An anti-trafficking consultant says scam operations and recruitment of foreign workers into them continue, and the anti-trafficking group Blue Dragon says calls from trafficked people have dropped sharply but not stopped <sup>[N67](#n67)</sup>. Amnesty found evidence of state intervention at only 24 of the 86 compounds it identified <sup>[N139](#n139)</sup>.
- **The law:** Cambodia's Law on Combating Online Scams came into force on 7 April 2026; scammers face 2 to 5 years in prison and a fine of up to 500 million riel (Cambodia's currency; about Tk 1.5 crore), and bosses 15 years to life if their operations cause death (see [What can get you jailed or deported](#what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported)) <sup>[N149](#n149), [N150](#n150), [N125](#n125), [3](#o3)</sup>. Recruiting or training people for scam work carries 2 to 5 years <sup>[N125](#n125)</sup>.
- **The bosses:** two alleged kingpins, Chen Zhi and Li Xiong, Chinese-born businessmen with Cambodian citizenship, were arrested and extradited in 2026 <sup>[N67](#n67)</sup>. The US State Department's 2026 Trafficking in Persons report kept Cambodia in Tier 3, its lowest ranking, and says some senior officials and police profited from and protected trafficking in scam centres <sup>[N33](#n33)</sup>.
- **In Bangladesh:** in September 2022 RAB (the Rapid Action Battalion, an elite police unit) arrested a ring leader living in Cambodia, Nazmul Islam, and two others in Paltan, and said the gang had trafficked more than 500 Bangladeshis to Cambodia <sup>[N40 (2022)](#n40)</sup>. In July 2026 the State Minister for Expatriates' Welfare said visa checks for Cambodia had been tightened and that workers to Cambodia would no longer go under the attestation exemption, which let batches of up to five workers go without the embassy attesting the job papers (see ["Is it open now, and who can go?"](#is-it-open-now-and-who-can-go)) <sup>[N13](#n13), [N134](#n134)</sup>.

<a id="other-work-routes"></a>

## 5 Other work routes

Besides a job with one employer (see [Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker)), a foreigner can work on his own permit as a self-employed person or as an owner named on a company's tax registration <sup>[N151 (2024)](#n151)</sup>.

<a id="self-employed-and-business-owners"></a>

### 5.1 Self-employed and business owners
