A self-employed foreigner needs his own work permit and employment card from MLVT (the Ministry of Labour and Vocational Training), under MLVT Notification 110/23 of 28 December 2023; the work permit and employment card are the two papers that together let a foreigner work in Cambodia <sup>[N151 (2024)](#n151), [N3 (2024)](#n3)</sup>. A foreign owner named on a company's patent tax certificate (the company's yearly business-tax registration) needs a work permit too <sup>[N151 (2024)](#n151)</sup>.

1. <a id="self-employed-and-business-owners-item-1"></a>**Apply on FWCMS** (the Foreign Workers Centralized Management System, fwcms.mlvt.gov.kh, MLVT's online system for foreign workers). A self-employed person uses its "Self-Employed" function <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. A company uses its "Enterprise" function to register, request a quota to use foreign workers and monitor them <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. The work permit and employment card are applied for together in one application ([Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), [Step 3: The employer gets your work permit](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-work-permit)) <sup>[N3 (2024)](#n3)</sup>.
2. <a id="self-employed-and-business-owners-item-2"></a>**Stay on the EB extension.** The EB (business) extension of the E-class (ordinary) visa covers employees and freelancers, for 1, 3, 6 or 12 months; it does not by itself give the right to work <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>. How to get it is in Work visa: company worker, [Step 7: After arrival](#step-7-after-arrival) <sup>[N2 (2024)](#n2)</sup>.
3. <a id="self-employed-and-business-owners-item-3"></a>**Keep out of the closed trades.** Foreigners may not work as drivers, street vendors, masseurs, hairdressers or beauticians, shoe shiners or tailors, mechanics or tyre repairers, or makers of Khmer souvenirs, musical instruments or Buddhist statues <sup>[N4](#n4)</sup>.

**If it fails:** A foreigner doing business in Cambodia without a work permit, the self-employed included, can be fined KHR 50.4 million (riel, Cambodia's currency; about Tk 15.4 lakh) under Joint Prakas 498 of 31 July 2023 (a joint ministerial order) <sup>[N88](#n88), [54 (2023)](#o54), [3](#o3)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Do Bangladeshis in Phnom Penh run registered shops or restaurants, and how does a Bangladeshi owner get a quota and work permit for himself?
> - What does a self-employed work permit cost in 2026, and can it be applied for from Bangladesh or only after arrival?

<a id="student-visa"></a>

## 6 Student visa

Cambodia has no student entry visa of its own: a student enters on the E-class (ordinary) visa, which also covers trainees, interns and students, and then extends it inside Cambodia as an ES (student) extension <sup>[9](#o9), [N152](#n152), [11](#o11)</sup>. The E-class e-visa costs USD 35 (about Tk 4,400) <sup>[10](#o10), [2](#o2)</sup>.

1. <a id="student-visa-item-1"></a>**Get an acceptance letter** from a registered Cambodian school or university, and proof that you can pay for your living costs and fees <sup>[N152](#n152), [11](#o11)</sup>.
2. <a id="student-visa-item-2"></a>**Get the E-class e-visa** on www.evisa.gov.kh, as in Work visa: company worker, [Step 5: The visa, and checking it is real](#step-5-the-visa-and-checking-it-is-real); the E-class visa needs a letter of invitation, job offer or contract from a company or organisation in Cambodia <sup>[9](#o9), [10](#o10)</sup>.
3. <a id="student-visa-item-3"></a>**Apply for the ES extension before your first month ends,** at the immigration office or through a visa agent <sup>[11](#o11), [9](#o9)</sup>. You'll need the enrolment letter from a registered school and proof of funds <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>. It runs for 1, 3, 6 or 12 months; 1- and 3-month extensions are single entry, and 6- and 12-month ones let you leave and come back <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>. Through an agent, a 6-month extension costs around USD 160 to 170 (about Tk 20,000 to 21,200) <sup>[N152](#n152), [2](#o2)</sup>.
4. <a id="student-visa-item-4"></a>**Renew your passport at the reduced student fee.** Bangladeshi students in Cambodia pay 1,190 baht (Thai currency) for a regular passport at the Embassy of Bangladesh in Bangkok, which covers Cambodia because Bangladesh has no embassy there (see [The Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok](#the-bangladesh-embassy-in-bangkok)) <sup>[114](#o114), [115 (2021)](#o115), [N9](#n9)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which Cambodian universities enrol Bangladeshi students, and about how many Bangladeshis study in Cambodia?
> - Does the school's acceptance letter count as the "letter of invitation" for the E-class e-visa, or must the school send a separate one?
> - What does the General Department of Immigration (GDI, the Ministry of Interior's immigration department) charge at its own counter for an ES extension?

<a id="can-you-work"></a>

### 6.1 Can you work?

No foreigner may work without an MLVT work permit and employment card, and the ES extension is issued for study <sup>[N3 (2024)](#n3), [11](#o11)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can an ES holder get a work permit for part-time work, or is any work on an ES extension illegal?
> - Are Dhaka agents selling Cambodian "student visas" as a route to jobs?

<a id="visit-visa"></a>

## 7 Visit visa

The tourist visa (T-class) is for holidays, and working on it is prohibited <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>. It costs USD 30 (about Tk 3,700), is single entry, is valid for 3 months from issue and lets you stay 1 month; it can be extended once <sup>[56](#o56), [9](#o9), [2](#o2)</sup>. Bangladesh's Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare tells people not to go abroad for work on a tourist or visit visa on a broker's promise <sup>[6](#o6)</sup>. A job needs the E-class visa and a work permit (see [Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker)) <sup>[N3 (2024)](#n3)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - In November 2026, can a Bangladeshi passport holder apply for the tourist (T) e-visa or get a visa on arrival, or only the E-class e-visa?

<a id="step-1-choose-how-to-apply"></a>

### 7.1 Step 1: Choose how to apply

1. <a id="step-1-choose-how-to-apply-item-1"></a>**Online:** www.evisa.gov.kh is run only by the E-Visa Office of Cambodia's MFAIC (Ministry of Foreign Affairs and International Cooperation); embassies don't run it <sup>[81](#o81)</sup>. The steps are in [Step 2: Apply for the e-visa](#step-2-apply-for-the-e-visa): Apply for the e-visa.
2. <a id="step-1-choose-how-to-apply-item-2"></a>**Through an embassy, by email:** Cambodia's embassy for Bangladesh is the Royal Embassy of Cambodia in New Delhi (A-1/309, Safdarjung Enclave, New Delhi 110029; +91 11 41717136 or 41717143; camemb.ind@mfaic.gov.kh) <sup>[84](#o84), [116 (2024)](#o116)</sup>. Tourist and business visas can typically be requested from any Cambodian embassy, wherever the applicant lives <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-1-choose-how-to-apply-item-3"></a>**On arrival:** a visa on arrival is offered at international airports and border checkpoints, but not every nationality is eligible <sup>[84](#o84), [117](#o117)</sup>. It is now processed electronically, with a digital visitor pass sent by email, and since 1 September 2026 its fee can be paid online in advance <sup>[N90](#n90), [N153](#n153)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does the New Delhi embassy accept visa applications by email from people living in Bangladesh, and what does it charge?

<a id="step-2-apply-for-the-e-visa"></a>

### 7.2 Step 2: Apply for the e-visa

You'll need:
- a passport valid for more than 6 months on the day you arrive <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>;
- a digital passport photo (JPEG or PNG) <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>;
- a Visa or MasterCard credit card <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>;
- a flight, bus or boat ticket and a hotel or other accommodation booking <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>.

1. <a id="step-2-apply-for-the-e-visa-item-1"></a>**Open www.evisa.gov.kh** and click "Application" <sup>[83](#o83)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-2-apply-for-the-e-visa-item-2"></a>**Type the security code** shown in the box <sup>[83](#o83)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-2-apply-for-the-e-visa-item-3"></a>**Fill in your personal, passport and travel details,** then review them <sup>[83](#o83)</sup>.
4. <a id="step-2-apply-for-the-e-visa-item-4"></a>**Pay by card** <sup>[83](#o83)</sup>. The fee is not refunded once processing has started <sup>[85](#o85)</sup>.
5. <a id="step-2-apply-for-the-e-visa-item-5"></a>**Save the reference number.** You need it to track the application, change pending details or download the visa under "Check and Change" <sup>[84](#o84)</sup>.
6. <a id="step-2-apply-for-the-e-visa-item-6"></a>**Wait about 3 business days** for the visa by email; there is no express service <sup>[84](#o84), [56](#o56)</sup>.
7. <a id="step-2-apply-for-the-e-visa-item-7"></a>**If your photo was wrong,** email the new one with your reference number to info@evisa.gov.kh <sup>[83](#o83)</sup>. The e-visa office answers on (855) 23 224 973 and (855) 99 334 973, in Khmer and English <sup>[83](#o83)</sup>.

**Check it is real:** On www.evisa.gov.kh, open "Check Certificate" (Check & Change) and enter the reference number; there is a link if you have lost it <sup>[64](#o64)</sup>. Check that the visa type is T (Tourist) and that the passport is the one you will travel on <sup>[56](#o56), [86](#o86)</sup>. Cambodian embassies can't check an e-visa for you <sup>[86](#o86)</sup>. Since 3 October 2026 every Cambodian visa is digital, so no sticker goes in the passport <sup>[84](#o84)</sup>.

**If it fails:** The Ministry may refuse, cancel or revoke a visa given on false information or forged documents, and you stay responsible for every detail even when an agent applies for you <sup>[85](#o85)</sup>.

**What agencies charge:** A Dhaka travel agency charges Tk 7,500 for a Cambodia e-visa, non-refundable, delivered in 5 to 7 working days, against the official USD 30 (about Tk 3,700) <sup>[N154 (2024)](#n154), [56](#o56), [2](#o2)</sup>. It asks for 6 months' bank statements and a bank solvency certificate showing at least Tk 60,000 per applicant <sup>[N154 (2024)](#n154)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What do Dhaka agencies charge in 2026 for a Cambodia tourist visa sold with a promise of a job after arrival?

<a id="step-3-fill-in-the-e-arrival-before-you-fly"></a>

### 7.3 Step 3: Fill in the e-Arrival before you fly

1. <a id="step-3-fill-in-the-e-arrival-before-you-fly-item-1"></a>**Within 7 days before you arrive,** fill in the e-Arrival (Cambodia's online immigration, health and customs form) at https://arrival.gov.kh, the only official site, or in the Cambodia e-Arrival app <sup>[87](#o87), [82](#o82), [88](#o88)</sup>. Every traveller must submit it, even with a visa; it is free, and it is not a visa <sup>[87](#o87), [117](#o117)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-3-fill-in-the-e-arrival-before-you-fly-item-2"></a>**Screenshot or download the QR code** before you close the page <sup>[87](#o87)</sup>.

<a id="step-4-at-the-border"></a>

### 7.4 Step 4: At the border

1. <a id="step-4-at-the-border-item-1"></a>**Enter at a port that accepts e-visas:** Techo International Airport (Phnom Penh), Siem Reap Angkor or Sihanoukville airport, Bavet on the Vietnam border or Tropaeng Kreal on the Laos border <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>. All land crossings from Thailand, Poipet, Cham Yeam and O Smach included, are closed until further notice <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>. At airports and international ports your entry and exit go on a Cambodia v-Pass instead of a passport stamp ([Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), [Step 5, item 4: Fill in the e-Arrival form](#step-5-the-visa-and-checking-it-is-real-item-4)) <sup>[90](#o90)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-4-at-the-border-item-2"></a>**Be ready to show why you came.** Officers may refuse entry to anyone without a clear travel history and purpose, money to support themselves and a guarantor in Cambodia <sup>[N79](#n79)</sup>. The GDI said in July 2025 that it has no policy of refusing any nationality, but applies stricter checks <sup>[N79](#n79)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What do immigration officers at Techo airport ask Bangladeshi visitors to show (return ticket, cash, hotel booking), and how many are refused entry?

<a id="step-5-extend-once-or-leave"></a>

### 7.5 Step 5: Extend once, or leave

1. <a id="step-5-extend-once-or-leave-item-1"></a>**Register your address.** FPCS (Foreigners Presence in Cambodia System) is the system the authorities use to record where foreigners stay; hotels usually register guests, and someone not registered may be unable to extend a visa <sup>[N90](#n90)</sup>. Report a change of address to the local police within 24 hours <sup>[N127](#n127)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-5-extend-once-or-leave-item-2"></a>**To extend,** go to the immigration office in Phnom Penh or use a licensed visa agent; it can't be done online <sup>[N155](#n155)</sup>. A tourist visa can be extended once, for one month, for about USD 45 (about Tk 5,600) <sup>[N156](#n156), [9](#o9), [2](#o2)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-5-extend-once-or-leave-item-3"></a>**Leave after about 60 days in all** (30 on entry and one 30-day extension) <sup>[N155](#n155)</sup>. Overstaying costs USD 10 (about Tk 1,250) a day (see [What can get you jailed or deported](#what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported)) <sup>[N156](#n156), [2](#o2)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What is the GDI's own fee for a one-month tourist extension in 2026, and what do Phnom Penh agents charge Bangladeshis?

<a id="can-you-work-1"></a>

### 7.6 Can you work?

No: employment on a tourist visa is prohibited <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>. Turning a tourist visa into an E-class visa after arrival is not an official route (see [Changing visa type](#changing-visa-type)) <sup>[N6](#n6)</sup>.

<a id="scams-1"></a>

### 7.7 Scams

- **Tourist visa sold as a work visa:** in July 2026 the State Minister said many victims travel on one- or two-month tourist visas while brokers tell them they have two-year work visas <sup>[N49](#n49)</sup>. A returnee promised a computer-operator job for Tk 5.3 lakh got BMET (Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training) clearance, then found on arrival he had only a one-month visit visa <sup>[N83](#n83)</sup>. Many trafficked Bangladeshis travelled on visas that did not match the job promised (see [Scam compounds and trafficking](#scam-compounds-and-trafficking)) <sup>[N83](#n83)</sup>.
- **The Thailand detour:** after Bangladesh tightened checks on Cambodia, some recruiters allegedly sent workers to Thailand as tourists before moving them to Cambodia <sup>[N157](#n157)</sup>.
- **Look-alike websites:** the GDI has disowned sites such as "Cambodia-e-arrival.online" and cambodiaivisa.com; the only official sites are www.evisa.gov.kh and arrival.gov.kh <sup>[89](#o89), [88](#o88), [81](#o81), [87](#o87)</sup>. The tourist e-visa fee was cut from USD 36 to USD 30 <sup>[N158](#n158), [56](#o56)</sup>.

<a id="family"></a>

## 8 Family

A worker's husband, wife or child stays on an EB dependant extension: EB extensions cover dependants of EB holders, for 1, 3, 6 or 12 months <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>. Only the E-class visa can be extended as EB, so the family member enters on it <sup>[N3 (2024)](#n3), [N6](#n6)</sup>.

1. <a id="family-item-1"></a>**Get your marriage and relationship papers certified** at the Embassy of Bangladesh in Bangkok, which covers Cambodia: it attests a marriage certificate for 275 baht and issues a relationship certificate for 205 baht <sup>[115 (2021)](#o115), [114](#o114)</sup>. The Thai land border is closed, so a worker in Cambodia can't reach Bangkok overland <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>.
2. <a id="family-item-2"></a>**The family member gets the E-class e-visa** on www.evisa.gov.kh, as in Work visa: company worker, [Step 5: The visa, and checking it is real](#step-5-the-visa-and-checking-it-is-real) <sup>[10](#o10), [N3 (2024)](#n3)</sup>.
3. <a id="family-item-3"></a>**After arrival, apply for the dependant EB extension** at the immigration office or through a visa agent, with proof of the relationship and a letter from you, the EB holder <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>. Agents' EB prices are in Work visa: company worker, [Step 7: After arrival](#step-7-after-arrival) <sup>[N3 (2024)](#n3)</sup>.
4. <a id="family-item-4"></a>**Register the family's address on FPCS** ([Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), [Step 7: After arrival](#step-7-after-arrival)) <sup>[N8](#n8)</sup>.

A family member on an EB extension may not work without his or her own work permit <sup>[11](#o11), [N3 (2024)](#n3)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What invitation letter does a spouse or child use for the E-class e-visa: the worker's employer's, or the worker's own?
> - Does the GDI accept a Bangladeshi marriage certificate attested by the Bangkok embassy, or must it also be legalised by MFAIC?
> - Do any Bangladeshi workers in Cambodia bring their families, and is there a minimum salary for it?

<a id="marrying-in-cambodia"></a>

### 8.1 Marrying in Cambodia

A marriage is legal only if registered before the civil registrar at the commune or sangkat (urban ward) office <sup>[N159 (2024)](#n159)</sup>. Two foreigners living legally in Cambodia may marry there under Cambodian law <sup>[N159 (2024)](#n159)</sup>. Marriage between a Cambodian and a foreigner follows Sub-Decree 183 of 3 November 2008 <sup>[N159 (2024)](#n159)</sup>. The Bangkok embassy issues a marital status certificate for 275 baht <sup>[114](#o114)</sup>.

Marrying a Cambodian gives no Khmer visa (K-class) and no automatic nationality: the foreign spouse still needs a T or E visa, and may apply for nationality only after living together for three years from registration of the marriage <sup>[9](#o9), [99](#o99), [98 (1996)](#o98)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What does a commune office ask a Bangladeshi to show before registering a marriage (single-status certificate, police clearance, MFAIC approval)?

<a id="changing-visa-type"></a>

## 9 Changing visa type

- **Tourist to E-class:** Not an official route inside Cambodia <sup>[N6](#n6), [N90](#n90)</sup>. After the one tourist extension, a T-class holder must leave; anyone staying more than 60 days is told to enter on the E-class visa <sup>[N155](#n155)</sup>.
- **E-class to EB (work or business):** The extension a worker lives on ([Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), [Step 7: After arrival](#step-7-after-arrival)) <sup>[N2 (2024)](#n2), [N6](#n6)</sup>. It does not give the right to work; a work permit is still needed <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>.
- **E-class to EG (job-seeker):** For people looking for work, for 1, 3 or 6 months, generally single entry <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>.
- **E-class to ES (student):** See Student visa <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>.
- **EB to residence or citizenship:** See Permanent residence and citizenship <sup>[N78](#n78), [98 (1996)](#o98)</sup>.

All E-class extensions are applied for inside Cambodia at the immigration office or through a visa agent <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>. To leave and come back on a new visa, the Thai land crossings are closed; Bavet (Vietnam) and Tropaeng Kreal (Laos) are open and accept e-visas <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>.

**Scams:** Brokers promised one Bangladeshi a one-year visa and work permit after arrival; after he escaped a scam centre he never got them and became undocumented <sup>[N160](#n160)</sup>. A broker's promise that the job title on your visa will change later is covered in Work visa: company worker, [Step 2: The job offer and contract](#step-2-the-job-offer-and-contract) <sup>[N83](#n83)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - In 2026, can a Bangladeshi on a tourist visa get an EG or EB extension inside Cambodia through an agent, and at what price?
> - What does a Phnom Penh agent charge a Bangladeshi for a first EG (job-seeker) extension, and does it lead to a work permit?

<a id="having-a-child-in-cambodia"></a>

## 10 Having a child in Cambodia

A child born in Cambodia to two Bangladeshi parents is not Cambodian: a child born there to foreign parents gets Khmer nationality only if both parents were themselves born in Cambodia and live there legally, and a child with one Khmer parent is Khmer wherever born <sup>[98 (1996)](#o98)</sup>. The child is Bangladeshi by descent if the father or mother is a Bangladeshi citizen at the birth <sup>[118](#o118)</sup>.

1. <a id="having-a-child-in-cambodia-item-1"></a>**Register the birth within 30 days** at the commune or sangkat office; births to foreign parents living in Cambodia are registered the same way, and the birth certificate is free <sup>[N159 (2024)](#n159)</sup>.
2. <a id="having-a-child-in-cambodia-item-2"></a>**Have the Cambodian birth certificate translated into English and attested** by Cambodia's Ministry of Foreign Affairs <sup>[119](#o119)</sup>.
3. <a id="having-a-child-in-cambodia-item-3"></a>**Register the birth with Bangladesh.** Apply online at bdris.gov.bd, then give the printout and papers to the Embassy of Bangladesh in Bangkok, which registers births in Cambodia and issues the Birth Registration Certificate <sup>[119](#o119)</sup>. You'll need:
   - the attested Cambodian birth certificate <sup>[119](#o119)</sup>;
   - both parents' Bangladeshi birth registration certificates <sup>[119](#o119)</sup>;
   - the parents' work permit and visa copies <sup>[119](#o119)</sup>.

   It is free within 45 days of the birth and 36 baht after <sup>[114](#o114)</sup>. If a parent is Bangladeshi by descent only (born abroad), the child is a citizen only if the birth is registered at a Bangladesh mission <sup>[118](#o118)</sup>.
4. <a id="having-a-child-in-cambodia-item-4"></a>**Get the child a passport** at the Bangkok embassy: 3,960 baht regular (40 to 45 days) or 7,920 baht express (21 to 28 days) <sup>[114](#o114)</sup>. The Thai land border is closed, so a parent in Cambodia can't reach Bangkok overland <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can parents in Cambodia send the birth-registration and passport papers to the Bangkok embassy by post or courier, or must a parent travel to Bangkok?
> - What visa does a newborn of Bangladeshi workers need, and by when?
> - Can parents whose visas have expired register a birth at the commune office without being reported?

<a id="asylum"></a>

## 11 Asylum

Cambodia has ratified the 1951 Refugee Convention and its 1967 Protocol, but asylum is only for someone with a well-founded fear of persecution because of race, religion, nationality, membership of a particular social group or political opinion <sup>[N161](#n161), [120 (2009)](#o120)</sup>. A claim based purely on economic need or the wish for a better life can be rejected at once, in what appears to be a fast track with no appeal or review <sup>[120 (2009)](#o120), [N162 (2010)](#n162)</sup>. The rules are set by a sub-decree (a government regulation), and the Ministry of Interior decides claims through the Immigration Department of the National Police <sup>[120 (2009)](#o120)</sup>. UNHCR (the UN refugee agency) says Cambodia's General Department of Immigration (GDI, the Ministry of Interior's immigration department) oversees 26 refugees and 11 asylum seekers of all nationalities, that problems remain with access to asylum and case processing, and that UNHCR itself has no programmes or staff in Cambodia <sup>[121](#o121), [N2 (2024)](#n2)</sup>. Permission to stay lasts only as long as the procedure <sup>[120 (2009)](#o120)</sup>. Cambodian officers interview Bangladeshis applying to go home to check whether they are trafficking victims or linked to scam centres, and those found to be victims have been allowed home (see [Going home without valid papers](#going-home-without-valid-papers), [Step 2: Register with the Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok](#step-2-register-with-the-bangladesh-embassy-in-bangkok)) <sup>[N126](#n126)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Has any Bangladeshi applied for refugee status in Cambodia since the 2009 sub-decree, and with what result?
> - May an asylum seeker work while the claim is pending, and does the stay authorisation protect him from immigration detention?

<a id="how-to-apply"></a>

### 11.1 How to apply

1. <a id="how-to-apply-item-1"></a>**Apply to any Cambodian official,** by any appropriate means <sup>[120 (2009)](#o120)</sup>.
2. <a id="how-to-apply-item-2"></a>**Get your permission to stay:** a free written authorisation, either a visa in your passport or, without a passport, a letter signed and sealed by the Commissioner General of the National Police <sup>[120 (2009)](#o120)</sup>.
3. <a id="how-to-apply-item-3"></a>**Return the application form** completed and signed within one week <sup>[120 (2009)](#o120)</sup>.
4. <a id="how-to-apply-item-4"></a>**Attend the interview,** held within 15 days of the form, before two Interior Ministry officials and one Foreign Ministry official; a qualified interpreter is free at every stage <sup>[120 (2009)](#o120)</sup>. Missing the interview twice without good reason ends the case, and not giving accurate information gets the claim rejected at once <sup>[120 (2009)](#o120)</sup>.
5. <a id="how-to-apply-item-5"></a>**Wait for the written decision,** due within 45 days of the interview, extendable to 90 days in all <sup>[120 (2009)](#o120)</sup>.

An asylum seeker can approach UNHCR through the Ministry of Interior at any time <sup>[120 (2009)](#o120)</sup>. UNHCR's office covering Cambodia is in Bangkok: 3rd A Floor, United Nations Building, Rajdamnern Nok Avenue, Bangkok 10200; +66 2 288 2180; thaba@unhcr.org <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.

<a id="if-it-is-refused"></a>

### 11.2 If it is refused

1. <a id="if-it-is-refused-item-1"></a>**Appeal once,** in writing to the Immigration Department within 30 days, stating why; it acts within 15 days, and its decision is final <sup>[120 (2009)](#o120)</sup>. There is no appeal to a court <sup>[N162 (2010)](#n162)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-it-is-refused-item-2"></a>**Leave Cambodia within 15 days** of the final refusal (see [Going home without valid papers](#going-home-without-valid-papers)) <sup>[120 (2009)](#o120)</sup>.

<a id="if-you-are-recognised"></a>

### 11.3 If you are recognised

A refugee may work or run a business under Cambodian law <sup>[120 (2009)](#o120)</sup>. Status is cancelled if it was won with false information or documents <sup>[120 (2009)](#o120)</sup>.

<a id="irregular-routes-and-undocumented-workers"></a>

## 12 Irregular routes and undocumented workers

Many Bangladeshis went to Cambodia with valid passports, visas and government clearance and were then forced into scam compounds; others lost their status after arrival <sup>[N26](#n26), [N163](#n163), [N164](#n164)</sup>. The Bangladesh embassy put the number overstaying at 4,000 to 4,500 in mid-2026 <sup>[N163](#n163)</sup>. Cambodia's Ministry of Interior said it deported 3,364 Bangladeshis between 1 September 2023 and 13 September 2026, 852 of them linked to online scams <sup>[N164](#n164)</sup>. Overstaying costs USD 10 (about Tk 1,250) a day; entering illegally brings prison and then expulsion, and working without a work card can bring expulsion (see [What can get you jailed or deported](#what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported)) <sup>[N17](#n17), [2](#o2), [55 (1994)](#o55)</sup>. Breaking the rules on registration, residence cards and exit permits is fined 50,000 to 1,000,000 riel (Cambodia's currency; about Tk 1,500 to Tk 30,500), and 1,000,001 to 2,000,000 riel (up to about Tk 61,000) for a repeat <sup>[55 (1994)](#o55), [3](#o3)</sup>.

<a id="how-people-lose-their-papers"></a>

### 12.1 How people lose their papers

- **A visit or tourist visa instead of a work visa:** one worker left with BMET (Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training) emigration clearance and found on arrival he held only a one-month visit visa; another, promised an employment visa, found he had a tourist visa <sup>[5](#o5), [N163](#n163)</sup>.
- **A work permit that never comes:** recruiters promised one returnee a one-year visa and work permit and provided neither, leaving him without legal status <sup>[N164](#n164)</sup>.
- **A job that doesn't match the papers:** the same returnee was promised data entry, but his invitation letter named construction work <sup>[N164](#n164)</sup>.
- **A visa left to expire:** one victim said his middlemen refused to extend his expired visa <sup>[N165](#n165)</sup>.
- **Tourist e-visas by design:** in 2022 RAB (the Rapid Action Battalion, Bangladesh's elite police unit) said a trafficking gang sent Bangladeshis on Cambodian tourist e-visas arranged with Bangladeshis living in Cambodia <sup>[N70 (2022)](#n70)</sup>.
- **Routes round the checks:** after Bangladesh tightened checks on travel to Cambodia, some recruiters allegedly used fake BMET cards or sent workers to Thailand as tourists before moving them into Cambodia <sup>[N163](#n163)</sup>.
- **Leaving the employer:** the owner of Fahim Recruiting Agency said in July 2026 that some workers leave for better-paid jobs and their original employers then refuse to take them back <sup>[N132](#n132)</sup>.
- **Coming back after deportation:** Anirban Survivor Voice Foundation, a Bangladeshi survivors' group, warned in October 2026 that deported Bangladeshis may be lured back into Cambodia illegally through another country <sup>[N76](#n76)</sup>.

To check a visa and work permit before and after you fly, see [Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), [Step 5: The visa, and checking it is real](#step-5-the-visa-and-checking-it-is-real): The visa, and checking it is real.

> **Need to know**
> - When a worker leaves the employer whose quota he came on, what happens to his E-class (business) visa extension and work permit, and how many days does he have before he is out of status?
> - Is there a GDI counter or online check where a worker can see whether his visa extension was actually filed?
> - What does a fixer in Phnom Penh charge a Bangladeshi in 2026 to extend an expired visa or get a work permit after arrival?

<a id="cambodia-as-a-route-to-thailand-and-malaysia"></a>

### 12.2 Cambodia as a route to Thailand and Malaysia

Thai police said in 2024 that since early 2023 Bangladeshis have been smuggled into Thailand via Cambodia, moved in stages from the Cambodian border to Bangkok and then to southern Thailand, heading for Malaysia <sup>[N71 (2024)](#n71)</sup>. Cases on record:

- **December 2023, Sa Kaeo:** 11 Bangladeshis bound for Malaysia, who had travelled from Bangladesh to Cambodia and crossed into Thailand illegally, were caught when their pickup truck overturned in Sa Kaeo province <sup>[N166 (2023)](#n166)</sup>.
- **January 2024, Narathiwat:** Thai immigration police arrested 19 Bangladeshis at Taba Market, Tak Bai district <sup>[N71 (2024)](#n71)</sup>. Bangladeshis in Cambodia had taken their passports to get them fake Thai entry stamps; each paid Tk 4 to 5 lakh for the stamps and the agents, and all 19 passports carried irregular visa stickers <sup>[N71 (2024)](#n71)</sup>.
- **April 2026, southern Thailand:** 21 Bangladeshis who paid brokers Tk 7 lakh each for passage to Malaysia flew to Cambodia, crossed into Thailand on foot on 4 April, were hidden in a seaside resort in Chana whose owner took their phones, and were charged with illegal entry and stay <sup>[N66](#n66), [N167](#n167)</sup>.
- **May 2026, Sa Kaeo:** six Bangladeshis who had flown to Sihanoukville in January and worked about five months as labourers without pay crawled under the barbed-wire border fence from the Poipet side, meaning to reach the Bangladesh embassy in Bangkok for help going home; they were handed to Khlong Luek police for prosecution <sup>[N61](#n61)</sup>.
- **July 2026, Sa Kaeo:** seven Bangladeshis, none with a passport or ID, were among 11 people caught crossing from Cambodia after fleeing Cambodian crackdowns; each had paid a smuggling network 8,000 baht (Thai currency), was driven from Poipet to the border and walked across a natural crossing by three Cambodian guides, who escaped before Thai officers arrived <sup>[N72](#n72)</sup>.

All land border crossings between Cambodia and Thailand, including Poipet, are closed until further notice <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>. Entering Cambodia secretly or by fraud is itself punished with prison and expulsion (see [What can get you jailed or deported](#what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported)) <sup>[55 (1994)](#o55)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What does the Cambodia-to-Malaysia package (flight to Phnom Penh, border crossing, onward travel) sell for in Dhaka in 2026, and who runs it?
> - Do Bangladeshis also go on from Cambodia to Vietnam, and on what papers?
> - What sentence do Bangladeshis caught entering Thailand illegally get, and how long are they held in Thai immigration detention before deportation?

<a id="raids-detention-and-deportation"></a>

### 12.3 Raids, detention and deportation

Cambodia's crackdown on online-scam operations has brought deportations on a large scale <sup>[N7](#n7), [N147](#n147)</sup>:

- **Scale:** the GDI deported more than 45,000 foreigners in January to June 2026, not counting those who left on their own, and 52,039 from 87 countries in January to September 2026, more than 3,000 of them Bangladeshis <sup>[N7](#n7), [N168](#n168)</sup>. Cambodia's Commission for Combating Online Scams said it repatriated 23,760 foreigners and raided 972 scam operations between July 2025 and October 2026 <sup>[N147](#n147)</sup>.
- **Victims:** no figures show how many deported Bangladeshis were trafficking victims forced into scams <sup>[N168](#n168)</sup>. Amnesty International said Cambodia treats people leaving scam compounds as irregular migrants and detains them without access to lawyers or their embassy <sup>[N17](#n17)</sup>.
- **Leaving on their own:** 241,888 foreigners, Bangladeshis among them, left Cambodia voluntarily between mid-January and 19 April 2026 <sup>[N169](#n169)</sup>.

Where people are held:

- **Immigration detention centres** in Phnom Penh and Sihanoukville <sup>[N129 (2023)](#n129)</sup>. Detainees report officers demanding bribes to return phones, for a bed, bedding and a fan, to deliver food and for release <sup>[N129 (2023)](#n129)</sup>. One former scam worker said free drinking water was available only one hour a day and otherwise cost USD 2 (about Tk 250) <sup>[N17](#n17), [2](#o2)</sup>. Detainees may receive visitors <sup>[N129 (2023)](#n129)</sup>.
- **Bati Pre-Deportation Centre,** Sopheakmit Residence, Lumpong commune, Bati district, Takeo province, which in May 2026 took in 994 foreigners suspected of scam work, Bangladeshis among them <sup>[N130](#n130)</sup>. How long a person stays there depends on his embassy issuing travel documents to those without passports (see [Going home without valid papers](#going-home-without-valid-papers), [Step 3: Get a Travel Permit if you have no passport](#step-3-get-a-travel-permit-if-you-have-no-passport)) <sup>[N130](#n130)</sup>.

The expulsion order and how to challenge it are under What can get you jailed or deported; what happens to people taken out of compounds is under Scam compounds and trafficking.

> **Need to know**
> - How does a family in Bangladesh find out which detention centre a relative is in, and who at the GDI answers such a request?
> - Does the Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok visit detainees in Phnom Penh, Sihanoukville or Bati, and how often?

<a id="going-home-without-valid-papers"></a>

## 13 Going home without valid papers

This is for anyone whose visa has expired, whose passport is held by a broker or employer, or who has been released from a compound or detention <sup>[N164](#n164), [N163](#n163), [N13](#n13), [N131 (2022)](#n131), [N130](#n130)</sup>. In 2026 people went home this way: they waited for a window when Cambodia waived overstay fines, registered with the Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok, got a Travel Permit if they had no passport, and flew out <sup>[N122](#n122), [N163](#n163), [N164](#n164)</sup>. Outside a waiver, overstaying costs USD 10 (about Tk 1,250) a day, which can add up to thousands of dollars <sup>[N17](#n17), [2](#o2)</sup>.

<a id="help-from-bangladesh"></a>

### 13.1 Help from Bangladesh

- **The Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok** handles Bangladeshis in Cambodia, because Bangladesh has no embassy there (see [Step 2: Register with the Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok](#step-2-register-with-the-bangladesh-embassy-in-bangkok): Register with the Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok) <sup>[N9](#n9)</sup>.
- **Probashi call centre** of the Wage Earners' Welfare Board (WEWB, the government fund for migrant workers): 16135, free in Bangladesh, or +8809610102030 from abroad <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>.
- **WEWB by application:** a family can apply for a worker jailed abroad to be brought home, and for legal help abroad, through the expatriate welfare branch of the DC (Deputy Commissioner's) office, the DEMO (District Employment and Manpower Office) or directly to the Welfare Board, which writes to the embassy and tells the family the result <sup>[102](#o102), [103](#o103), [58](#o58)</sup>.
- **Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare and Overseas Employment:** a family can write to its grievance officer, the Joint Secretary (Admin and Finance), 02-41030224, 01712992086, adsadmin@probashi.gov.bd, who has 30 working days to reply <sup>[70](#o70)</sup>.
- **BRAC:** victims and families who contacted BRAC Migration Centre online got help arranging the return <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does the Ministry or the Welfare Board pay the air ticket for a stranded worker in Cambodia, and how does a family apply?
> - What phone number and WhatsApp should a family use to reach BRAC's Migration Programme about a relative stranded in Cambodia?

<a id="step-1-check-whether-a-fine-waiver-is-open"></a>

### 13.2 Step 1: Check whether a fine waiver is open

1. <a id="step-1-check-whether-a-fine-waiver-is-open-item-1"></a>**Find out whether Cambodia is waiving overstay fines.** In June 2026 Cambodia let overstayers leave without paying fines; at the Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok's request it extended the window to 1 September, and from 1 September it again let overstayers leave voluntarily without fines <sup>[N122](#n122), [N145](#n145)</sup>. That waiver was due to end on 31 October 2026 <sup>[N124](#n124), [N142](#n142)</sup>. Embassies negotiate waivers country by country, and the process is slow <sup>[N17](#n17)</sup>. One returnee learned of the waiver on Facebook, applied, bought a ticket and flew home on 14 June 2026; another's fine was waived 24 days after he applied <sup>[N164](#n164), [N13](#n13)</sup>. To ask the GDI directly, call its hotline for foreigners (see [Contacts in Cambodia](#contacts-in-cambodia)) <sup>[45](#o45)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-1-check-whether-a-fine-waiver-is-open-item-2"></a>**Leave inside the window.** Waivers are time-limited and can require departure within days <sup>[N144](#n144)</sup>. Someone who refused to leave in a window and was then detained is barred from Cambodia for life once deported (see [Bans after you leave](#bans-after-you-leave)) <sup>[N7](#n7)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Was the waiver extended past 31 October 2026, and is one open now? If not, what does a Bangladeshi overstayer pay to leave?
> - Where does the Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok announce waiver windows (Facebook page or website)?
> - Must a Bangladeshi granted a waiver send his ticket details to the GDI before flying, and how?

<a id="step-2-register-with-the-bangladesh-embassy-in-bangkok"></a>

### 13.3 Step 2: Register with the Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok

1. <a id="step-2-register-with-the-bangladesh-embassy-in-bangkok-item-1"></a>**Ask the Embassy's labour wing to put you on the return list.** The 1,796 Bangladeshis stranded in September 2026 had registered with the Embassy during Cambodia's return window, and requests for help reached it by phone, email, in person and through other Bangladeshis in Cambodia <sup>[N122](#n122), [N126](#n126)</sup>:
   - **Welfare hotline** for Bangladeshis in Thailand and Cambodia: +66-818-70-84-43 <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>.
   - **Labour wing:** First Secretary (Labour) Jahir Emam, +66 02-3905064, fsbangkok@probashi.gov.bd <sup>[110](#o110)</sup>.
   - **Consular email:** consular.bangkok@mofa.gov.bd <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-2-register-with-the-bangladesh-embassy-in-bangkok-item-2"></a>**Expect a Cambodian screening interview.** Cambodian officers interview everyone on the return list to check whether they are trafficking victims or linked to scam centres and trafficking networks, and question those who entered irregularly in more detail <sup>[N126](#n126), [N122](#n122)</sup>. Those found to be trafficking victims were allowed home <sup>[N126](#n126)</sup>. In October 2026 the screening was paused, and the Bangladesh mission asked for it to restart <sup>[N126](#n126)</sup>. By mid-October Cambodia had accepted a list of 1,797 Bangladeshis, with about 400 more in the same situation <sup>[N170](#n170)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is there a form or written list of what to send the Embassy to register for return?
> - Where and in what language is the Cambodian screening interview held, and is an interpreter provided?

<a id="step-3-get-a-travel-permit-if-you-have-no-passport"></a>

### 13.4 Step 3: Get a Travel Permit if you have no passport

1. <a id="step-3-get-a-travel-permit-if-you-have-no-passport-item-1"></a>**Apply for a Travel Permit,** a one-way document the Embassy issues to a Bangladeshi without a passport who wants to go home <sup>[122 (2023)](#o122)</sup>. In 2026 many Bangladeshis overstaying in Cambodia received travel passes to return, and the Embassy was working to send one woman home on a travel pass with the Cambodian government's cooperation <sup>[N163](#n163), [N30](#n30)</sup>.
   You'll need <sup>[122 (2023)](#o122)</sup>:
   - copies of your old passport and its visa page;
   - your air ticket;
   - your birth certificate or NID (national identity card);
   - a GD (general diary, a police report) if the passport was lost;
   - 2 photos and the filled-in form;
   - an interview in person at the Embassy.
2. <a id="step-3-get-a-travel-permit-if-you-have-no-passport-item-2"></a>**Pay the fee:** 550 baht (ordinary) or 1,100 baht (urgent) <sup>[122 (2023)](#o122), [77](#o77)</sup>. The consular section takes applications 9:30 am to 12:00 noon and delivers 3:30 to 4:30 pm, Monday to Friday <sup>[123 (2021)](#o123)</sup>.

All land border crossings between Cambodia and Thailand are closed, so a worker in Cambodia cannot reach the Embassy in Bangkok overland <sup>[13](#o13)</sup>. For people held at the Bati centre, release waits on their embassy issuing travel documents (see ["Raids, detention and deportation"](#raids-detention-and-deportation)) <sup>[N130](#n130)</sup>. When Cambodia waived 602 Nepalis' overstay fines in May 2026, Nepal's embassy in Bangkok, which also covers Cambodia, prepared one-way travel documents and sent them to Cambodia for those whose passports were lost or expired <sup>[N120](#n120)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - With the land border closed, how does someone in Cambodia without a passport do the Travel Permit interview: does the labour wing take applications in Phnom Penh, by video, or through a representative?
> - Is the 550-baht Travel Permit ready the same day or in two working days? (The Embassy's two pages differ.)

<a id="step-4-buy-the-ticket-and-leave"></a>

### 13.5 Step 4: Buy the ticket and leave

1. <a id="step-4-buy-the-ticket-and-leave-item-1"></a>**Buy a refundable ticket.** On the Embassy's instructions, many stranded Bangladeshis bought refundable tickets while they waited <sup>[N122](#n122)</sup>. Returnees in 2026 paid their own fares: the 30 released from detention in October 2026 bought their own tickets, one stranded man said he bought tickets two or three times and only lost money, and one returnee spent USD 2,266 (about Tk 2.8 lakh), including overstay penalties, to get home <sup>[N75](#n75), [N126](#n126), [N165](#n165), [2](#o2)</sup>. High airfares and paperwork kept many from leaving even when fines were waived <sup>[N142](#n142)</sup>. Under the May 2026 waiver for Nepalis, people had to send their travel details to Cambodia's immigration department as soon as they had tickets <sup>[N171](#n171), [N120](#n120)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-4-buy-the-ticket-and-leave-item-2"></a>**Wait for immigration clearance before going to the airport.** On 3 September 2026, 32 Bangladeshis with tickets were turned back at Phnom Penh airport while waiting for clearance and taken to an immigration detention centre (see [If you are detained](#if-you-are-detained)) <sup>[N48](#n48), [N122](#n122)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-4-buy-the-ticket-and-leave-item-3"></a>**If a broker stops you leaving,** tell the Embassy's welfare hotline at once (see [Step 2: Register with the Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok](#step-2-register-with-the-bangladesh-embassy-in-bangkok): Register with the Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok) <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>. Returnees told BRAC that Bangladeshis involved in trafficking in Cambodia intimidate victims trying to go home; one said brokers delayed his return by two months, and a stranded man's wife said his broker was stopping him from leaving <sup>[N145](#n145), [N122](#n122)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which air route home from Phnom Penh (via Bangkok, Kuala Lumpur or Singapore) is cheapest, and does the transit need a visa?

<a id="step-5-at-dhaka-airport"></a>

### 13.6 Step 5: At Dhaka airport

1. <a id="step-5-at-dhaka-airport-item-1"></a>**Expect questioning.** In October 2026 CID (the police Criminal Investigation Department) carried out forensic checks on returnees from Cambodia and examined their phones and belongings; one returnee had his phone seized <sup>[N75](#n75), [N122](#n122)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-5-at-dhaka-airport-item-2"></a>**Get help at the airport.** BRAC's Migration Programme gave returnees from Cambodia emergency help, counselling and money to reach home <sup>[N145](#n145)</sup>. The airport welfare desks and filing a case are under Coming home and Scam compounds and trafficking.

> **Need to know**
> - When CID seizes a returnee's phone, how does he get it back, and is he treated as a witness or a suspect?

<a id="if-you-are-detained"></a>

### 13.7 If you are detained

The 32 Bangladeshis taken from Phnom Penh airport in September 2026 were held without phones and could not contact their families; the Embassy sent food through a local representative <sup>[N122](#n122)</sup>. Thirty were released after more than six weeks, once WEWB's expatriate welfare branch wrote to the Embassy's First Secretary (Labour) on an application by Anirban Survivor Voice Foundation, and reached Dhaka on 20 October 2026 <sup>[N75](#n75), [N9](#n9), [N123](#n123)</sup>. A family can make the same application to WEWB (see [Help from Bangladesh](#help-from-bangladesh)) <sup>[102](#o102), [103](#o103)</sup>. Where people are held and what happens there is under Raids, detention and deportation.

<a id="bans-after-you-leave"></a>

### 13.8 Bans after you leave

- **After deportation for illegal entry or work:** blacklisted from re-entering Cambodia for at least three to five years, the GDI's deputy director general said in 2025 <sup>[N64](#n64)</sup>.
- **After refusing to leave in a waiver window and then being detained:** barred from Cambodia for life once deported, the GDI's Director General, Sok Veasna, said in July 2026 <sup>[N7](#n7)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does leaving under a fine waiver bring any entry ban, and can a Bangladeshi check whether he is blacklisted before paying for a new visa?

<a id="sending-money-home"></a>

## 14 Sending money home

**Send through a bank or a licensed money-transfer company, never hundi** (sending money through an unlicensed private agent outside the banking system), which is illegal in Bangladesh <sup>[N63 (2024)](#n63)</sup>. Money sent legally earns the family the government's 2.5% cash incentive, kept in the FY2026-27 budget, and at some banks a further 2.5% <sup>[N62](#n62), [124](#o124)</sup>. bKash, a Bangladeshi mobile-money wallet, does not list Cambodia among the countries it receives money from (checked 11 November 2026) <sup>[N62](#n62)</sup>. Bangladesh Bank publishes no separate figure for money sent from Cambodia; it counts it under "Others" <sup>[53 (2024)](#o53)</sup>. Opening a Cambodian bank account is under Work visa: company worker, [Step 7: After arrival](#step-7-after-arrival): After arrival <sup>[N97](#n97), [N100](#n100)</sup>.

<a id="how-to-send"></a>

### 14.1 How to send

**The channels:**
- **ACLEDA Bank** (263 branches in every province and town) sends abroad through Thunes (its international transfer service in the ACLEDA app), Western Union, MoneyGram, Ria and SWIFT (the international bank-to-bank network) <sup>[N96](#n96), [N95](#n95), [N172](#n172), [N173](#n173), [N174](#n174), [N175](#n175)</sup>.
- **ABA Bank** (Advanced Bank of Asia) sends from its app through Ria, MoneyGram, Western Union, SWIFT, Visa Direct and Mastercard Send <sup>[N93](#n93)</sup>.
- **Wing Bank** sends from its app through more than 30 partners, including Western Union, MoneyGram and Ria <sup>[N94](#n94)</sup>.

You'll need:
- your passport or other valid original ID <sup>[N172](#n172), [N174](#n174)</sup>;
- at an ABA branch without an ABA account, your passport and a valid visa <sup>[N93](#n93)</sup>;
- for a bKash wallet, if the service you use pays into bKash, the receiver's bKash number and full name exactly as registered with bKash <sup>[N62](#n62)</sup>.

1. <a id="how-to-send-item-1"></a>**Send the money.** Pick one:
   1. **ACLEDA app, Thunes:** open the app, select TRANSFERS and log in with fingerprint, face or PIN; select INTERNATIONAL TRANSFER, then Thunes; enter the amount in US dollars (a riel account is converted automatically), tap NEXT and enter the receiver's details; tick "I have read and agree to the Terms and Conditions", tap OK, check and tap CONFIRM <sup>[N176](#n176), [N95](#n95)</sup>. Up to 4 times a day <sup>[N95](#n95)</sup>.
   2. **ACLEDA branch, Western Union:** at a branch that serves Western Union, fill in the "To Send Money" form and hand it over with the money, the fee and your ID <sup>[N172](#n172)</sup>. Western Union and MoneyGram also go from the ACLEDA app, up to 4 times a day and USD 9,999 (about Tk 12.5 lakh) a transfer; Ria at any ACLEDA branch, up to USD 9,999 a transfer <sup>[N172](#n172), [N173](#n173), [N174](#n174), [2](#o2)</sup>.
   3. **ABA app, Ria:** go to Transfers, then International Transfer - Ria, then Send Money; choose "Pick up at Agent" (cash) or "Bank Deposit"; enter the receiver and the amount, from USD 10 to USD 9,999, and at most USD 9,999 in 30 days; the fee shows after you enter the amount <sup>[N93](#n93)</sup>.
   4. **Wing Bank app:** tap World Transfer, choose the country and partner, enter the receiver and amount, and confirm with your 4-digit PIN <sup>[N94](#n94)</sup>. MoneyGram also goes from a Wing outlet <sup>[N177](#n177)</sup>.
2. <a id="how-to-send-item-2"></a>**Keep the code and give it only to the receiver.** Western Union's receipt carries the MTCN (Money Transfer Control Number), which goes to the receiver with your name, the amount and "Cambodia" as the sending country <sup>[N172](#n172)</sup>. ABA's Ria code has 11 digits, lasts 21 days and tracks the transfer on riamoneytransfer.com <sup>[N93](#n93)</sup>. For a Thunes cash pickup, give the receiver the transfer details and the withdrawal code <sup>[N176](#n176)</sup>.
3. <a id="how-to-send-item-3"></a>**The family collects.** A bKash wallet takes up to Tk 2.5 lakh (about USD 2,000) a transfer, any number of times, and cashes out at Tk 7 per thousand at BRAC Bank, City Bank and Q-Cash ATMs <sup>[N62](#n62), [2](#o2)</sup>.

**What it costs.** ACLEDA's Thunes fees to Asia-Pacific countries <sup>[N95](#n95), [2](#o2)</sup>:
- **to a mobile wallet:** USD 3.70 (about Tk 460) up to USD 2,500 (about Tk 3.1 lakh), USD 6.20 up to USD 5,000, USD 10.20 up to USD 10,000;
- **to a bank account:** USD 4.50 (about Tk 560), USD 7.00 and USD 11.00 for the same bands;
- **for cash pickup:** USD 4.60 (about Tk 570), USD 7.10 and USD 11.10.

ACLEDA says these fees are indicative only <sup>[N95](#n95)</sup>. A MoneyGram fee at ACLEDA is charged to the sender only <sup>[N173](#n173)</sup>.

**If it fails:** a completed Thunes transfer can't be changed, cancelled or refunded; a Thunes cash-pickup transfer can, until it is collected <sup>[N95](#n95)</sup>. Changing or cancelling an ABA Ria transfer costs USD 5 (about Tk 620), and a bank-deposit transfer can't be changed <sup>[N93](#n93), [2](#o2)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which of these services pay into a bKash wallet or a Bangladeshi bank account when sending from Cambodia, and which do Bangladeshi workers in Phnom Penh actually use?
> - How long does a transfer from Cambodia take to reach a bKash wallet or a Bangladeshi bank account?
> - Is ACLEDA's Thunes limit USD 10,000 a transfer (its web page) or USD 2,500 (its brochure)?
> - Where does the NBC (National Bank of Cambodia, the central bank) publish its list of licensed banks and payment-service firms, so a worker can check a money-transfer shop?

<a id="if-the-money-doesnt-arrive"></a>

### 14.2 If the money doesn't arrive

Complain in this order:

1. <a id="if-the-money-doesnt-arrive-item-1"></a>**The bank in Cambodia,** with your receipt and the transfer code <sup>[N172](#n172), [N93](#n93)</sup>:
   - ACLEDA call centre, 24 hours: +855 23 994 444 or +855 15 999 233; then its Complaint Management Centre, 015 888 654, cmc@acledabank.com.kh, working days 7:30 to 16:30 <sup>[N95](#n95)</sup>;
   - ABA, 24 hours: 098 203 203 <sup>[N93](#n93)</sup>;
   - Wing: (+855) 23 999 989, care.centre@wingbank.com.kh <sup>[N94](#n94)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-the-money-doesnt-arrive-item-2"></a>**The NBC,** which takes consumers' complaints about financial services: 085 600 002, 098 220 002, 085 600 003, 097 278 3030 or 098 220 001; Phnom Penh branch 016 568 433; Preah Sihanouk branch 016 687 194 <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>.
3. <a id="if-the-money-doesnt-arrive-item-3"></a>**bKash,** for a wallet transfer: +88-02-55663001, support@bkash.com, its Facebook page or live chat <sup>[N62](#n62)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does the NBC hotline take complaints in English, and does it resolve them for foreign workers?

<a id="hundi"></a>

### 14.3 Hundi

Hundi brokers bring cash to the family's door, and in 2022-2024 paid more taka per dollar than banks (for example Tk 115 against about Tk 108) <sup>[N63 (2024)](#n63)</sup>. Workers without valid papers often have no other way to send money <sup>[N63 (2024)](#n63)</sup>. Brokers settle through bKash and Nagad (another Bangladeshi mobile wallet) accounts <sup>[N63 (2024)](#n63)</sup>. By June 2026 Bangladesh's BFIU (Financial Intelligence Unit) had suspended or frozen about 55,000 mobile-money accounts linked to online gambling and hundi, and in August 2026 it decided to freeze about 14,000 more <sup>[N178](#n178)</sup>. Money sent by hundi gets no 2.5% incentive <sup>[N62](#n62)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is there a hundi network for Bangladeshis in Phnom Penh or Sihanoukville, and what rate does it pay against the banks?

<a id="saving-at-home"></a>

### 14.4 Saving at home

- **Wage Earner Development Bond.** A 5-year Bangladesh government bond for workers who send money legally, sold at authorised bank branches in Bangladesh and at Bangladeshi banks' branches and exchange houses abroad, from Tk 25,000 to Tk 50 lakh, with no purchase limit <sup>[126](#o126)</sup>. It pays up to 12% simple profit at maturity <sup>[126](#o126)</sup>.
- **Probash pension.** Bangladesh's pension scheme for migrants takes monthly payments from abroad and pays a pension from age 60 (see [Your rights at work](#your-rights-at-work), Pension) <sup>[97](#o97), [96](#o96)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can a worker in Cambodia, where no Bangladeshi bank has a branch or exchange house, buy the Wage Earner Development Bond through his family in Bangladesh?

<a id="if-a-worker-dies-is-injured-or-loses-the-job"></a>

## 15 If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job

In Cambodia, NSSF (the National Social Security Fund) covers work injuries for all paid workers, Cambodian and foreign, and the employer must register you within 3 days of hiring, using your passport number <sup>[93 (2019)](#o93), [94 (2021)](#o94)</sup>. In Bangladesh the WEWB (Wage Earners' Welfare Board, the government board for migrants' welfare) pays grants to dead workers' families, and its Probashi call centre is 16135 (free), or +8809610102030 from abroad <sup>[102](#o102), [59](#o59)</sup>. Its Tk 3 lakh death grant goes only to workers who left with BMET (Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training) clearance or are Board members <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>. Bangladesh has no embassy in Cambodia; the Embassy in Bangkok handles it (see [The Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok](#the-bangladesh-embassy-in-bangkok)) <sup>[62](#o62), [N9](#n9)</sup>.

<a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill"></a>

### 15.1 If you are injured or fall ill

1. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-item-1"></a>**Get treated.** Go to a health facility with the NSSF logo and show your NSSF card; treatment is free <sup>[93 (2019)](#o93)</sup>. If the facility asks you to pay, show the card and call 1286; for an ambulance in an emergency, call 1286 <sup>[93 (2019)](#o93)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-item-2"></a>**Make sure the employer reports it.** The employer must tell NSSF at once on 1286 and send the work-injury form within 48 hours, to NSSF or to benefit@nssf.gov.kh <sup>[93 (2019)](#o93)</sup>.
3. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-item-3"></a>**Claim pay while off work.** The employer pays the first day; from the second day NSSF pays 70% of your contributory wage until you recover, and a person caring for you gets 50% of your daily allowance <sup>[93 (2019)](#o93)</sup>. Sickness that isn't from work is under Your rights at work, Sick leave and maternity <sup>[93 (2019)](#o93)</sup>.
4. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-item-4"></a>**Claim for lasting injury.** Permanent disability under 20% is paid as a lump sum, 20% or more as a monthly pension <sup>[93 (2019)](#o93)</sup>.
5. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-item-5"></a>**Keep to the deadlines.** Claim the daily allowance within 1 year and permanent disability within 5 years, with your passport and NSSF card attached <sup>[93 (2019)](#o93)</sup>. NSSF keeps originals, so keep copies; it phones you within 20 to 30 working days to collect a cheque <sup>[93 (2019)](#o93)</sup>.
6. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-item-6"></a>**Going home sick.** The WEWB's airport desks take sick returnees to hospital, with a free ambulance from Dhaka or Chattogram airport <sup>[5](#o5), [127](#o127)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Are injured Bangladeshi construction workers registered with NSSF in practice, or do employers pay hospital bills directly and send them home?
> - Does the WEWB pay a treatment grant to workers who come home from Cambodia disabled or critically ill, and how much in 2026?
> - Does the Tk 10 lakh BMET insurance pay for permanent disability, and where is the claim made?

<a id="if-a-worker-dies"></a>

### 15.2 If a worker dies

1. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-1"></a>**Ask the WEWB to bring the body home.** The family applies to the WEWB, which records the family's wishes, sends them to the embassy and follows up until the body arrives; call 16135 (free) or +8809610102030 from abroad <sup>[128](#o128), [59](#o59)</sup>. For Cambodia, the Embassy in Bangkok handles it, and the next of kin (spouse or children) applies on the Embassy's form <sup>[N9](#n9), [129 (2023)](#o129)</sup>. In Cambodia a death must be registered at the commune or sangkat office within 15 days, and for a foreigner the civil registrar issues the death certificate as soon as possible so the body can be taken across the border, cremated or buried <sup>[N159 (2024)](#n159)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[129 (2023)](#o129)</sup>:
   - the deceased's passport and visa copy;
   - the hospital and district death certificates, with English translations;
   - the receiver's NID (national identity card) copy and proof of relationship;
   - the next of kin's application.

   The bodies of three Bangladeshis were brought home from Cambodia on 8 October 2026 <sup>[N117](#n117)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Who pays to fly a body from Phnom Penh to Dhaka when the employer won't, what does it cost, and how many days does it take through the Embassy in Bangkok?
   > - Who registers a Bangladeshi worker's death at the commune office when the employer or broker won't, and how does a family arrange burial in Cambodia instead?

2. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-2"></a>**Claim in Cambodia.** If a work injury kills the worker, NSSF pays to transport the body and pays 4 million riel (about Tk 1.2 lakh) to whoever holds the funeral <sup>[93 (2019)](#o93), [3](#o3)</sup>. Survivors claiming NSSF benefits need a family record book, residence certificate or marriage certificate <sup>[93 (2019)](#o93)</sup>. When the Embassy reports that compensation may be due, the WEWB asks the family for a power of attorney so the case can be run in Cambodia, and its legal cell helps prepare it <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>. Compensation, unpaid wages and insurance the Embassy recovers go to the WEWB, which pays the heirs <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>.
3. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-3"></a>**At Dhaka airport,** the WEWB's desk gives the family a Tk 35,000 cheque for transport and burial on the spot when the body is handed over, against an heirship certificate from the Union Parishad chairman, mayor or councillor, plus a photo and NID or passport copy of the nominated person <sup>[5](#o5), [131](#o131)</sup>. The cheque is lost if not collected within 3 months <sup>[131](#o131)</sup>. A free ambulance carries the body from Dhaka or Chattogram airport <sup>[127](#o127)</sup>. Dhaka desk officer in charge: +8801971 430203 <sup>[131](#o131)</sup>.
4. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-4"></a>**The Tk 3 lakh grant.** The WEWB pays it only if the worker died in the country his clearance was for <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>. If the family took the Tk 35,000 cheque at the airport, no separate application is needed; if the body was buried abroad, apply within 1 year of the death to the WEWB's Director General or through the district Probashi Kallyan Centre <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>. It goes to the spouse, children and parents, or to full brothers and sisters if the worker was unmarried and both parents are dead, by BEFTN (Bangladesh Electronic Funds Transfer Network) transfer to a bank account <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>.

   You'll need:
   - a family-members certificate from the Union Parishad chairman, mayor or councillor, countersigned by the UNO (Upazila Nirbahi Officer, the subdistrict's chief officer) <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>;
   - a release, undertaking and authorisation on Tk 400 non-judicial stamp paper, in the WEWB's sample format <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>;
   - a copy of the Embassy's NOC (no-objection certificate) or the death certificate <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>;
   - a bank certificate or original statement for the receiver's account, with the routing number <sup>[102](#o102)</sup>.

5. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-5"></a>**The insurance.** Workers who left with BMET clearance since 12 March 2023 are insured for Tk 10 lakh for 5 years, bought with a one-time Tk 1,000 premium at clearance (see [Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), [Step 6: Orientation, BMET clearance and the flight](#step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight): Orientation, BMET clearance and the flight) <sup>[51](#o51)</sup>.
6. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-6"></a>**Register the death** at the Embassy in Bangkok: immediate family applies with their own passport copy, the local death certificate in English and the deceased's passport copy; it is free within 45 days, 36 Baht after <sup>[119](#o119), [77](#o77)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Has the WEWB paid the Tk 3 lakh grant for a Bangladeshi who died in a Cambodian scam compound?
> - Where and with which papers does a family claim the Tk 10 lakh insurance?

<a id="if-you-lose-the-job"></a>

### 15.3 If you lose the job

1. <a id="if-you-lose-the-job-item-1"></a>**Your work permit can go.** MLVT (the Ministry of Labour and Vocational Training, which issues work permits) can cancel it if you are out of work for more than a month (see [Your residence permit](#your-residence-permit), If you lose your job) <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-you-lose-the-job-item-2"></a>**Get what you are owed.** Dismissal without proper notice means pay for the notice period, and a business that closes still owes it; wages and any indemnity are due within 48 hours of the contract ending (see [Changing employer or leaving](#changing-employer-or-leaving)) <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>.
3. <a id="if-you-lose-the-job-item-3"></a>**If you aren't paid,** follow If you aren't paid; a wage claim lapses three years after the wage was due <sup>[7 (1997)](#o7)</sup>.
4. <a id="if-you-lose-the-job-item-4"></a>**Hold the agency to its undertaking.** BMET's permission conditions for Cambodia say approved workers must get the job, and if anything goes wrong the agency pays the worker's food and lodging, return airfare and compensation, under an undertaking on Tk 300 stamp paper <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>. BMET's complaint form against an agency lists "job not provided", "non-payment of salary" and "non-existence of company" <sup>[69 (2024)](#o69)</sup>. Send the complaint in writing, with evidence, to BMET's Director General; it is free, should be settled in 30 working days, and you get a receipt with a complaint ID <sup>[5](#o5), [69 (2024)](#o69)</sup>.
5. <a id="if-you-lose-the-job-item-5"></a>**Don't overstay.** An overstay costs USD 10 (about Tk 1,250) a day (see [What can get you jailed or deported](#what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported)) <sup>[N121](#n121), [2](#o2)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Has any agency been made to pay a Cambodia worker's food, lodging and return airfare under its Tk 300 undertaking, and how does a worker claim it from Cambodia?
> - Does the BMET insurance pay anything to a worker sent home after losing the job?

<a id="coming-home"></a>

## 16 Coming home

- **Airport help.** The WEWB's Probashi Kallyan desks at Dhaka, Chattogram and Sylhet airports help with customs clearance and lost luggage, and take complaints from workers returning after abuse or fraud <sup>[132](#o132)</sup>. Women returning within a year after abuse or fraud get Tk 5,000, and sick returnees are taken to hospital <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>. Dhaka desk: +8802 8901040, 01404 413388; Chattogram: 01913631797; Sylhet: 01558277904 <sup>[127](#o127), [5](#o5)</sup>.
- **BRAC's help.** BRAC's Migration Programme gives returnees from Cambodia emergency help, counselling and money to reach home at Dhaka airport <sup>[N25](#n25), [N24](#n24)</sup>. Survivors of scam compounds have sought help, including legal support, at BRAC's Migration Service Centres <sup>[N49](#n49)</sup>.
- **The CID at the airport.** The CID (Criminal Investigation Department, Bangladesh Police) has run forensic checks on returnees from Cambodia and their phones on arrival <sup>[N123](#n123)</sup>. A trafficking case against a broker is under Scam compounds and trafficking <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>.
- **A place to stay.** The Wage Earners' Centre near Shahjalal airport costs Tk 100 to enter and Tk 200 a night; book at wec.wewb.gov.bd <sup>[133](#o133)</sup>. The Probashi Lounge at Shahjalal airport gives free wifi and telephone and cheaper food to workers who show BMET clearance or WEWB membership <sup>[134](#o134)</sup>.
- **A complaint against the agency** goes in writing to BMET's Director General (see [If you lose the job, item 4: Hold the agency to its undertaking](#if-you-lose-the-job-item-4)) <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>.
- **Legal help for your family in Bangladesh:** apply to the WEWB directly, or through the expatriate welfare branch of the DC (Deputy Commissioner, the district administration) office or the DEMO (District Employment and Manpower Office) <sup>[135](#o135)</sup>.
- **A business loan.** PKB (Probashi Kallyan Bank, the government bank for migrants) lends returnees up to Tk 50 lakh, up to Tk 3 lakh without collateral, against an up-to-date trade licence; helpline 16238, or +880 9677-787878 from abroad <sup>[136](#o136), [52](#o52)</sup>.
- **If you are coming home without a valid visa or passport,** see Going home without valid papers <sup>[122 (2023)](#o122)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does the WEWB or BMET compensate workers who went to Cambodia with BMET clearance and ended up in a scam compound, and has any agency been made to repay them?
> - What phone number and WhatsApp should a returnee from Cambodia call at BRAC's Migration Programme?

<a id="what-you-can-carry-home"></a>

### 16.1 What you can carry home

- **Your Cambodian bank account.** An ACLEDA account with no deposit, withdrawal or transfer for more than 12 months becomes dormant and is charged fees; ABA charges USD 50 (about Tk 6,200) after 4 years without a transaction and USD 100 (about Tk 12,500) a year after that <sup>[N98](#n98), [N100](#n100), [2](#o2)</sup>. Closing an ABA current account is free; ACLEDA may charge for closing a savings account within 3 months of opening <sup>[N100](#n100), [N96](#n96)</sup>.
- **Leaving Cambodia:**
  - **Cash:** declare USD 10,000 (about Tk 12.5 lakh) or more, or the same in riel, to customs; not declaring is fined 10% of the amount <sup>[106 (1997)](#o106), [2](#o2)</sup>. Taking out more than USD 10,000 in foreign currency that you did not declare on arrival needs a National Bank of Cambodia permit and customs approval <sup>[N89](#n89), [106 (1997)](#o106)</sup>.
  - **Gold:** raw gold or precious stones worth USD 10,000 or more must be declared to the National Bank in advance <sup>[106 (1997)](#o106)</sup>.
  - **Drugs:** trafficking can be punished with life imprisonment <sup>[N89](#n89)</sup>.
- **Arriving in Bangladesh:**
  - **Cash:** any amount of foreign currency, but more than USD 10,000 must be declared on the FMJ form (the customs currency declaration) <sup>[137](#o137)</sup>.
  - **Phones:** 2 used phones, plus 1 new phone once a year, without duty or tax <sup>[137](#o137)</sup>.
  - **Duty-free:** one item from a list that includes a laptop, and 100 g of gold and 200 g of silver jewellery (no more than 12 pieces of one type) <sup>[137](#o137)</sup>.
  - **Gold bar:** once a year, one declared bar of up to 117 g (10 tola), at Tk 5,000 duty per 11.664 g (about Tk 50,000 for 117 g); undeclared gold means detention, arrest and a criminal case, and gold above 117 g is confiscated <sup>[137](#o137)</sup>.
  - **Alcohol:** none for Bangladeshi passport holders <sup>[137](#o137)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Are the Dhaka airport baggage rules (an undated customs page) still current after the FY2026-27 budget?

<a id="contacts"></a>

## 17 Contacts

<a id="the-bangladesh-embassy-in-bangkok"></a>

### 17.1 The Bangladesh Embassy in Bangkok

Bangladesh has no embassy in Cambodia; its Embassy in Bangkok, Thailand, covers Cambodia <sup>[62](#o62), [N9](#n9)</sup>. In May 2026 Thailand's Prime Minister said the Thai-Cambodian border checkpoints would stay closed until Cambodia accepted Thailand's conditions <sup>[N73](#n73)</sup>. All land border crossings between Cambodia and Thailand are closed until further notice, so a worker in Cambodia can't reach the Embassy overland for services that need him in person, such as a Travel Permit interview or a power of attorney <sup>[13](#o13), [122 (2023)](#o122), [138](#o138)</sup>.

- **Address:** 47/8 Ekamai Soi 30, Sukhumvit 63, Bangkok 10110, Thailand; 9 am to 5 pm, closed weekends and public holidays <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>.
- **Phone:** +66 (0)2 390 5107 to 8 <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>.
- **Welfare hotline for Bangladeshis in Thailand and Cambodia:** +66-818-70-84-43 <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>.
- **Labour wing:** First Secretary (Labour) Jahir Emam, +66 02-3905064, fsbangkok@probashi.gov.bd <sup>[110](#o110)</sup>.
- **Consular:** Counsellor (Consular) Montasir Rahman Chowdhury, +66-027116651, counsellor.consular.bkkemb@gmail.com; consular.bangkok@mofa.gov.bd; applications 9:30 am to 12:00 noon, delivery 3:30 to 4:30 pm, Monday to Friday <sup>[110](#o110), [63](#o63), [123 (2021)](#o123)</sup>.
- **Email:** mission.bangkok@mofa.gov.bd <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>.

**Services:**
- **Passport:** MRP (machine-readable passport) 3,960 Baht regular (40-45 days) or 7,920 Baht express (21-28 days) <sup>[77](#o77)</sup>.
- **Travel Permit,** issued to a Bangladeshi without a passport who wants to go home (see [Going home without valid papers](#going-home-without-valid-papers)) <sup>[122 (2023)](#o122)</sup>.
- **Birth registration** of a child born in Cambodia to Bangladeshi parents: free within 45 days, 36 Baht after (see [Having a child in Cambodia](#having-a-child-in-cambodia)) <sup>[119](#o119), [77](#o77)</sup>.
- **Death registration and bringing a body home** (see [If a worker dies](#if-a-worker-dies)) <sup>[119](#o119), [129 (2023)](#o129)</sup>.
- **Power of attorney:** 510 Baht a page for pages 1-5, 170 Baht for each page after; the giver must come in person, and a Bangladeshi living in Cambodia must show a work permit and a valid visa (Business, Non-O or Resident) <sup>[77](#o77), [138](#o138)</sup>.
- **Police clearance attestation:** 140 Baht <sup>[77](#o77)</sup>.

**What it does in practice.** During the Thailand-Cambodia fighting in July 2025, the Embassy's Counsellor (Labour) went to Phnom Penh and arranged temporary food, shelter, transport and medical care for displaced Bangladeshis, with help from the Bangladeshi community <sup>[42](#o42)</sup>. BMET's permission letters for Cambodia are copied to the Embassy's labour counsellor <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>. Its "Labour Welfare Relations" web page is under construction <sup>[139 (2024)](#o139)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Are the Thai-Cambodian border checkpoints still closed in November 2026, and does the Embassy send a team or accept papers by post from Bangladeshis in Cambodia instead?
> - Does the +66-818-70-84-43 hotline take WhatsApp messages, and in which hours?
> - Is there a Bangladeshi community contact or association in Phnom Penh that the Embassy works through, with a phone number workers can call?

<a id="contacts-in-cambodia"></a>

### 17.2 Contacts in Cambodia

- **MLVT (Ministry of Labour and Vocational Training):** 023 884 375, hotline 1297; anonymous complaint box on its website <sup>[104](#o104)</sup>. Unpaid-wage complaints go to its Labour Dispute Department or the provincial Department of Labour (see [If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid)) <sup>[N103](#n103)</sup>.
- **FWCMS** (Foreign Workers Centralized Management System, MLVT's online work-permit system), technical support: online.fwcms@gmail.com <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.
- **NSSF:** Building 3, Russian Federation Boulevard, Tuek Laak I, Toul Kork, Phnom Penh (inside the MLVT compound); hotline and ambulance 1286; info@nssf.gov.kh <sup>[93 (2019)](#o93)</sup>.
- **CENTRAL** (Center for Alliance of Labor and Human Rights), legal aid on labour rights: #487 St. 53BT, Sangkat Boeng Tumpun, Khan Meanchey, Phnom Penh; +855 93 556 603 <sup>[N116](#n116)</sup>.
- **Police anti-trafficking hotline:** 1288, in Chinese, English, Khmer and Vietnamese (see [Scam compounds and trafficking](#scam-compounds-and-trafficking)) <sup>[111 (2022)](#o111), [N102](#n102)</sup>.
- **Police online scam reporting, 24 hours:** hotline.police.gov.kh, with a complaint form <sup>[112](#o112)</sup>. The GDI (General Department of Immigration) hotline for foreigners is 068 38 66 99, 078 38 66 99 or 087 38 66 99, email hotline@immigration.gov.kh; its office is on National Road No. 21, Boeung Chhouk village, Sangkat Nirouth, Khan Chbar Ampov, Phnom Penh <sup>[45](#o45), [88](#o88)</sup>.
- **NBC, complaints about banks and transfers:** 085 600 002, 098 220 002; Phnom Penh branch 016 568 433; Preah Sihanouk branch 016 687 194 <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>.
- **Banks:** ACLEDA +855 23 994 444 (24 hours); ABA 098 203 203 (24 hours) or 1 800 203 203; Wing (+855) 23 999 989 <sup>[N95](#n95), [N93](#n93), [N100](#n100), [N94](#n94)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What are Cambodia's emergency numbers for police and fire, and do operators speak English?
> - What are the Arbitration Council's and the Preah Sihanouk provincial Department of Labour's current address and phone?

<a id="contacts-in-bangladesh"></a>

### 17.3 Contacts in Bangladesh

- **Probashi call centre (WEWB), toll free:** 16135, or +8809610102030 from abroad <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>.
- **BMET:** 89/2 Kakrail, Dhaka-1000; complaints against an agency in writing, with evidence, to the Director General, free, 30 working days <sup>[61 (2021)](#o61), [5](#o5)</sup>. Director (Emigration): +8802222222415, 01711-135901, diremi@bmet.gov.bd; Additional Director General (Employment): 02-48321949, 01720105492, adgemp@bmet.gov.bd <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>.
- **Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare and Overseas Employment:** Probashi Kallyan Bhaban, 71-72 Old Elephant Road, Dhaka-1000; grievance officer 02-41030224, 01712992086, adsadmin@probashi.gov.bd, 30 working days <sup>[140](#o140), [70](#o70)</sup>. Its complaint cell takes complaints from migrants abroad at minister.expat@gmail.com and puts them to the Minister and the Secretary daily and weekly <sup>[141](#o141)</sup>.
- **WEWB:** Probashi Kallyan Bhaban, 71-72 Eskaton Garden, Ramna, Dhaka <sup>[142](#o142)</sup>.
- **WEWB airport desks:** Dhaka +8802 8901040, 01404 413388, officer in charge +8801971 430203; Chattogram 01913631797, pkdctg@gmail.com; Sylhet 01558277904, pkdsylhet@gmail.com <sup>[127](#o127), [131](#o131), [5](#o5)</sup>.
- **PKB:** 16238, or +880 9677-787878 from abroad <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>.
- **Passport helpline, 24 hours:** 16445, or 09666716445 from abroad <sup>[76 (2023)](#o76)</sup>.
- **Police clearance helpdesk,** Sunday to Thursday, 9 am to 5 pm: 01320001824, 01320001825, help.pcc@police.gov.bd <sup>[66](#o66)</sup>.
- **Royal Embassy of Cambodia in New Delhi,** which covers Bangladesh: A-1/309, Safdarjung Enclave, New Delhi 110029; +91 11 41717136, +91 11 41717143; camemb.ind@mfaic.gov.kh <sup>[12](#o12), [116 (2024)](#o116)</sup>.
- **bKash:** +88-02-55663001, support@bkash.com <sup>[N62](#n62)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is there a Cambodian honorary consul in Dhaka, and does he issue or check visas?
